# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 1985

## Śrīla Prabhupādadeva-praṇaty-aṣṭakam

*by Kuśakratha dāsa*

### Text 1

*yan-nathyamāno naṭanātha-nātho*  
*su-maṅgalaṁ paścima-jaṅgaleṣu*  
*gaurāṅgacandraś caraṇaṁ dadhāti*  
*namāmi taṁ śrī-prabhupādadevam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*yat—*by whom; *nathyamānaḥ—*appealed; *naṭanātha—*of dancers; *nāthaḥ—*the king; *su-maṅgalaṁ—*auspicious; *paścima—*of the Western countries *; jaṅgaleṣu—*in the great jungle; *gaurāṅgacandraḥ—*Lord Caitanya; *caraṇaṁ—*a foot; *dadāti—*places; *namāmi—*I offer my respectful obeisances; *taṁ—*to him; *śrī-prabhupādadevam—*His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

**Перевод:**

**I offer my respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. Because of his prayers, Lord Gaurāṅgacandra, the king of dancers, has placed His auspicious lotus feet in the jungle of the Western countries.**

### Text 2

*yad-yatnataś cakra-gadā-patākā-*  
*candrārdha-vidyuj-jalajāṅkuśāṅkaiḥ*  
*alaṅkṛtā hṛt-saraṇīśa-pādaiḥ*  
*namāmi taṁ śrī-prabhupādadevam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*yat—of* whom; *yatnataḥ—*because of the efforts; *cakra—*of the *cakra; gadā—club; patākā—*flag; *candra-ardha—*half-moon; *vidyut—*lightning bolt; *jalaja—*conch, fish, and lotus; *aṅkuśa—*and rod for controlling elephants; *aṅkaiḥ—*with the marks; *alaṅkṛtā—*decorated; *hṛt—*of the heart; *saraṇī—*the path; *īśa—*of the Supreme Personality of Godhead; *pādaiḥ—*with the footprints.

**Перевод:**

**I offer my respectful obeisances to His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. Because of his efforts. Lord Kṛṣṇa's footprints, which bear the marks of** ***cakra,*** **club, flag, half-moon, lightning bolt, conch, fish, lotus, and rod for controlling elephants, are now imprinted on the pathway of the living entities' hearts.**

*yad-yatnataḥ śrī-vraja-ballavasya*

*bhajanti lokāḥ karuṇena yasya*  
*namāmi taṁ śrī-prabhupādadevam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*māyā—*Māyā; *piśācī*—from the witch; *ku-hṛṣīka—*of the material senses; *harṣa—*of happiness; *svāpam—*the dream; *vihāya—*abandoning; *adya—*now; *hṛṣīka-devam—*Lord Kṛṣṇa, the master of the senses; *bhajanti—*worships; *lokāḥ—*the world; *karuṇena—*by the mercy; *yasya—*of whom.

By his mercy the conditioned souls are awakening from the nightmare of material sense happiness created by the witch Māyā. They now see the reality of devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa, the master of the senses.**

### Text 8

*viśvaṁ tv acaitanyam idaṁ tu kṛṣṇa-*  
*caitanya-pūrṇatvam alambataiva*  
*caitanya-sandeśa-vadena yena*  
*namāmi taṁ śrī-prabhupādadevam*

**Пословный перевод:**

*viśvaṁ—*universe; *tu—*indeed; *acaitanyam—*lifeless and unconscious; *idam—*this; *tu—*indeed; *kṛṣṇa*—of Lord Kṛṣṇa; *caitanya—*with consciousness; *pūrṇatvam—*the state of being filled; *alambata—*attained; *eva—*certainly; *caitanya—*of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu; *sandeśa—*the message; *vadena—*speaking; *yena—*by whom.

\*Note: This poem is written in *upajāti,* the same meter as Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura’s *Śrī Gurv-aṣṭakam.*

## Meaning of Vyāsa-Pūjā by Garuḍa dāsa

There is no principle more essential and sacred to Vaiṣṇava life than that of the relationship between the guru and disciple. It is the perfection of the relationship between a teacher and a student. Vyāsa-pūjā is that day on which the Vaiṣṇava student celebrates this most sacred of all relationships by specially honoring the *guru* on the day of his appearance.

In every culture of human civilization the relationship between the "teacher" and the "student" is found. The function of any teacher is to transmit the knowledge of a particular culture to the student for the purpose of establishing a *connection* between the student and that culture. It is through this teacher-student relationship that every culture, material or spiritual, passes down its knowledge and tradition. Since the educational aims and ultimate goals of cultures vary, the nature and quality of this teacher-student relationship varies greatly from one society to another. In Vaiṣṇava culture we find the ultimate teacher-student relationship, which is uniquely that of the *guru* and disciple.

The literal meaning of *guru-tattva* is the "truth or reality of the teacher." Because the connective power of *guru-tattva* originates in the Supreme, it is seen active in some basic form in all cultures. God Himself is the original supreme teacher of everyone, and therefore this teacher-student relationship originating in *guru-tattva* is universal. Indeed, *guru-tattva* is one of the divine potencies of the Lord as represented by Nityānanda Rāma *(bhakta-svarūpa),* the ultimate source of all teaching potencies. However, *guru-tattva* is manifested incompletely within most cultures, because they lack the knowledge of the original supreme teacher. There are teachers in these cultures, but their knowledge is incomplete and distorted, replete with half-truths. The Vaiṣṇava *guru,* however, is the perfect teacher, because he comes in a line of spiritual teachers who have been recognized and authorized by their teachers and by Śrī Kṛṣṇa Himself, the source of all true disciplic successions.

The following verse reveals this mystery of disciplic succession:

*vyāsa-prasādāc chrutavān*  
*etad guhyam ahaṁ param*  
*yogaṁ yogeśvarāt kṛṣṇāt*  
*sākṣāt kathayataḥ svayam*

Herein, Sañjaya, the narrator of the *Bhagavad-gītā,* states that it is by the grace of Vyāsadeva *(vyāsa-prasādāt)* that he has heard (*śrutavān*) directly the divine conversation (*sākṣāt kathayataḥ*) and received the confidential and transcendental connection *(guhyaṁ paraṁ yogam)* that is established by Kṛṣṇa Himself. Although Sañjaya was not present at Kurukṣetra, he nevertheless heard directly by the grace of Vyāsadeva. The mystery of the disciplic succession is that the *guru* is able to directly transmit to the disciple the teachings of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, transcending time and space, exactly as Vyāsa was able to transmit the divine conversation between Arjuna and Kṛṣṇa to Sañjaya. (That is why the day on which we honor the appearance of our *guru* is called Vyāsa-pūjā.) The connection between the Lord and His devotee, and the continuity of teachings, which the *guru* reveals through the power of disciplic succession, demonstrate the oneness in *guru-tattva.* It is in this sense that we can say *"guru* is one."

There is still another aspect of the mystery of disciplic succession: in the individual presentations of all *gurus* in *paramparā,* and especially of certain great *ācāryas,* there exists, along with the oneness of representation and continuity, a variety, an ever-increasing transcendental fullness and sweetness of the Vaiṣṇava teachings. For example, the *Vedas* could not be approached by persons in this age. But after Vyāsadeva compiled and arranged them, they became somewhat accessible. Then, by distilling the message of the *Vedas* further, Vyāsa presented their essence in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* where the Vedic message became more understandable and relishable due to his transcendental presentation. The increased fullness and sweetness did not stop there; the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* itself became still more relishable when recited to Parīkṣit by Śukadeva, who is compared to the parrot who sweetens the mango with its beak. And throughout history, great Vaiṣṇavas have continued to recite and compile commentaries on the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* increasing its fullness and sweetness, benefitinggenerations of Vaiṣṇavas.

Thus the mystery of disciplic succession lies in the simultaneous continuity and variation in both the presentation of teachings *(śikṣā)* and representation of the Lord *(dīkṣā)* by the *guru-paramparā.* This variety within the oneness *of guru-tattva* is partially attributable to the external variation found in the ways and means of preaching, due to differences in time, place, and circumstance. But more fundamentally, this variety in preaching is itself a manifestation of the deeper internal reality of the ever-increasing intensity of bliss experienced in *bhakti-rasa* within the heart of the exalted Vaiṣṇava. On Vyāsa-pūjā, we celebrate and glorify both the powerful connection that *guru* has with *guru-paramparā* and the *guru's* transcendentally unique mission and superexcellent personal qualities.

And now we may ask what stands out about the meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā on the appearance day of His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Śrīla Prabhupāda, to whom this book of offerings is dedicated. It is this: Śrīla Prabhupāda's unique mission in presenting the Vaiṣṇava teachings, and his personal example of Kṛṣṇa conscious realization, have caused the celebration of Vyāsa-pūjā to arise like a cooling spring, flooding the hot, dry desert sands of Kali-yuga. Now, from this spring, in rivers of pure devotion to Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the sacred waters will flow down throughout this age. For the first time in history, "Vyāsa-pūjā" is being celebrated around the world, introducing the transcendental relationship between the *guru* and the disciple to all cultures.

Garuḍa dāsa

## Introduction by Gopīparāṇadhana dāsa

It is now almost twenty years since His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda first set foot in America to put his life's mission into effect. Twenty years, in human terms, is a substantial duration—the time it takes a generation to grow from birth to adulthood—and for Śrīla Prabhupāda's spiritual child, the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, it has gradually nourished a maturing in dedication and perspective.

Every devotee in ISKCON has personally felt the power of Śrīla Prabhupāda's instructions will save ourselves and in due course save the whole world. It is this conviction and hope that inspire us to participate, had not experienced this power for himself, we would have never been able to surrender to the strict Vaiṣṇava way of life. Each one of us sustaining our service in ISKCON is convinced that obedience to Śrīla Prabhupāda's instructions will save ourselves, and in due course save the whole world. It is this conviction and hope that inspire us to participate, each to his capacity, in the cooperative preaching of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu's *saṅkīrtana.* These are the most immediately felt realizations of Śrīla Prabhupāda's disciples and granddisciples.

Yet even beyond such private appreciations, we should understand the distinct historical purpose Prabhupāda had for descending to the world. He didn't come just to deliver a few lucky individuals, but to inaugurate a worldwide spiritual revolution. Of this we need to be more aware, and perhaps the twenty years that have passed since ISKCON's founding will allow us the perspective to begin seeing our still-recent past as significant history.

Śrīla Prabhupāda's special contribution, which marks him as unique even among the divine company of Vaiṣṇava *ācāryas,* is the institution he established, the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. This had been predicted by an astrologer who once commented about Prabhupāda that he would "build a house the whole world can live in." Every bona fide Vaiṣṇava *ācārya* inspires his disciples with pure love of God, and the greatest of the *ācāryas* leave behind them, in their words and deeds, a personal influence that lasts thousands or even millions of years. But until Śrīla Prabhupāda came to the West, never in this Kali-yuga had such a vast organization been put together to engage all people without discrimination and spiritualize all kinds of material assets, channeling them all into the purest service of Their Lordships Gaura-Nityānanda and Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.

We can learn from the Vedic scriptures of the more traditional role of previous *dcaryas,* who avoided making themselves accessible to unqualified students. In the beginning of the *Praśna Upaniṣad* we read,

"Sukeśa, the son of Bharadvāja; Satyakāma, the son of Śibi; Gārgya, the son of Sūryāyaṇa; Kauśalya, the son of Aśvalāyana; Bhārgava, the son of Vidarbha; and Kabandhī, the descendant of Katyāyana were all dedicated seekers of the Absolute Truth. They were learned in the *Vedas* and fixed in spiritual austerities.

"Humbly carrying firewood in their hands, they approached the powerful saint Pippalāda, thinking, 'He will certainly tell us everything about the Supreme.'

"But the sage told them, 'Go home and remain there one year, practicing austerities, celibacy, and devotion. Then, if you wish, come back and submit your questions. If we know the answers, we will tell you everything.' "

The six students of sage Pippalāda, themselves all offspring of great souls, were exceptionally sincere, and they patiently followed their vows for one year as ordered. To our great fortune, however, Śrīla Prabhupāda never demanded the same standard of initiative on the part of his Kali-yuga disciples. Rather, he saw it as a practical necessity of the age for the *jagad-guru* to go out and actively canvass, even if it meant leaving holy India for the lower hells of New York City. Modern preaching requires more than quiet *bhajana* in seclusion; without the organized Striving of a spiritual army, there is little chance of being heard nowadays over the prevalent din of materialism and impersonalism—the great babel of propaganda and advertising, information and misinformation.

Prabhupāda taught us in person for only twelve short years. Yet in that time he built the foundation and framework of his house, ISKCON, and in its *saṅkīrtana* garden planted the seeds of pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness, his books. Let us each pray that we never abandon Prabhupāda's house or try to tear it down. Let us remain vigilant against becoming either *guru-tyāgī* or *guru-bhogī,* either refusing to fulfill his desire or else pretending to do so while merely "resting on our laurels." Above all, let us somehow cooperate for the higher purpose of developing Śrīla Prabhupāda's mission into a glorious transformation of the age that will be gratefully recorded in the history books of the future.

*ebe yaśa ghuṣuk tri-bhuvana*

It is time now, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that your fame become known throughout the three worlds.

Gopīparāṇadhana dāsa

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from the G.B.C.

## Ātreya Ṛṣi dāsa

There you were, the greatest *ācārya* of this age, the one ' who has been accredited with fulfilling Lord Caitanya's promise, the one who had acted in the mood of Vāsudeva Datta by trying to relieve the most fallen in the I universe. There you were, Śrīla Prabhupāda, lying helplessly on that bed in your room here in Vṛndāvana, acting totally dependent on the help of your disciples, begging forgiveness from your Godbrothers—a living example of the blade of grass, seeing everyone in their service and relationship to Kṛṣṇa.

And there I was, one of your many disciples having been ordered to your bedside by Your Divine Grace. I would have rather stayed behind my desk making money. I would have rather stayed by the side of my wife, tending the newborn, feeling some disappointment for failing to see my name on the list recently sent around. There I was, in that very depressing atmosphere, a total neophyte, not knowing what is going to happen next, helpless, tired, worried, concerned. I was shedding hayfever tears mixed with sentimentality. Day after day, night after night, nothing to satisfy my passionate mind.

I would grab your lotus feet, skin and bone, with mixed emotions and intentions. Here was a chance for me to touch a pure devotee so intimately; here was my only hope and trusted person giving me a chance to express my useless attempt at devotional service. Here was the opportunity to feel united in our desperation, free from the spirit of one-upmanship I had experienced in my dealings with my Godbrothers. I was getting all this, at your expense, foolishly injecting all my material and neophyte feelings into your body in my attempt to massage your leg. I was also aware of my thoughts, my inadequacies, my impurities. I was full of fear, doubt, and anxiety, having some realization of my unfortunate condition. Bharadrāja was massaging your other leg. There he was, tears in his eyes, sincere and innocent, putting his whole being into your leg. We were feeling unified, with a very rare affection for one another.

All of a sudden you made a sound. You were attempting to say something. Bharadrāja jumped towards your head, put his ear near your mouth. My hands froze. I could feel that you were saying something about your legs. I feared that you were about to expose my impurities. Here I was, caught red-handed, destroying my spiritual master; here was the confirmation of my apprehension that I was responsible for your condition. In fear and in agony, I awaited Bharadrāja’s face and looked into his eyes with query and doubt. He whispered, "Prabhupāda likes the way you are doing. He wants me to do the same."

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am still the same rascal, but am still here, visiting you in Vṛndāvana with great hesitation and resistence, by your mercy. Every year, I fear this would be the last, and every year, I am back, having failed to make any advancement.

All glories to your causeless mercy!

*Your faltering servant,*  
Ātreya Ṛṣi dāsa

## Bhagavān dāsa Goswami

**Spiritual Geometries**

Keeping Śrīla Prabhupāda in the Center

**Axioms**

Our existence and service have expanded from you as a circle takes birth from a point.

The unlimited points which make up the circle are simply expanded representations of the center.

Each and every point has a unique relationship with the center, as will the points on future concentric circles.

Only when there is a common relationship with the center do the points themselves perfectly align with each other and form the circle.

To establish a relationship with the center without a bond of cooperation between the points will produce a spoke rather than a wheel. This spoke will neither be able to roll steadily forward nor carry much weight.

However, having a connection between points who have lost their relationship with the center gives a twisted and bent "eccentric" circle.

Along with creating the circle, you have simultaneously created ample space within it, where all points can find complete engagement in your service.

A radiant radius of mercy emanates from the center equally to each point on the circle. Any point which takes full advantage of this mercy can expand the entire circle and increase the space of devotional service for everyone.

In time, as this radius passes through various points and is in turn equally expanded outward from them, there will be a succession of symmetrical and intersecting circles which all meet again in the center to form the full lotus of ISKCON preaching.

Although expanding the circle seems to move it further away from the center, in reality every point furthers its relationship with the center by establishing innumerable new points that connect with it.

**Rules**

As the center of ISKCON you emanate unlimited transcendental energy in the form of intelligence and inspiration to expand the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. Thus the space which was once found to be outside the circle is now within its bounds. In this way, ISKCON simultaneously leads the world and becomes the world, and the *varṇāśrama* society is born anew.

Kali-yuga destroys identity, as it is impossible to be a devoted servant or servant of a servant, what to speak of an assertive leader, without coming under attack. As you are the perfection of both servant and leader, those who center their life on you will find their identity again and readjust this neurosis in our society.

Losing sight of you, some have become *"rasa* fortune-hunters," seeking the ill advice and "wise lies" of pretender *bhais.* In their intellectual sense gratification they fail to see that you have already given the highest *rasa* of *saṅkīrtana-līlā.* Although Lakṣmī wanted to change *her rasa* and experience that of the *gopīs,* they had already decided to become associates of Caitanya Mahāprabhu to perform *saṅkīrtana* and distribute love of Kṛṣṇa all over the world. Not desiring even Vaiṣṇava liberation, we also consider that the perfection of *rasa* is to distribute Kṛṣṇa consciousness and the nectar of Prabhupāda, birth after birth.

As Viṣṇu rests on Śeṣa and dreams the cosmic manifestation, you are resting on the bed of the disciplic succession, and your dreams for expanding Kṛṣṇa consciousness are reality awaiting to unfold. For those who are realists, being awake means to enter your dreams.

Having received the right angle of preaching vision from you, we should prepare ourselves to meet various tests coming in the form of extremism, setback, success, anarchy, impatience, material attachment, accountability, formless spontaneity in the name of service, and being properly placed to serve you.

Although theoretically a point cannot be seen, the very existence of the circle expands and depends on it. In the same way, although you are now absent from our vision, having accepted you as the center, we will go on expanding Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the limits of space and beyond, where our service to your lotus feet will then go on eternally and unlimitedly.

Bhagavān dāsa Goswami

## Bhaktitīrtha Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*evaṁ paramparā-prāptam*  
*imaṁ rājarṣayo viduḥ*

Mahā-Viṣṇu exhales, and millions of universes come out of His pores. The field of activity is the *mahat-tattva,* and the conditioned souls are the fugitives running at a tremendous, suicidal pace. Lord Brahma has the difficult task of first establishing the atmosphere which will allow the living entities to forget who they are, where they have come from, and where they are going. Due to a tenacious attachment to sense gratification, the living entities allow themselves to remain blinded by the coverings of *ahaṅkāra.* Lord Brahma, other higher authorities, and even Kṛṣṇa Himself patiently watch as we mostly degrade ourselves as we move from Satya-yuga to Treta to Dvapara down to Kali-yuga. We renounce our godly qualities, taking on more and more the life-style of savages. The conditioned soul is practically in a helpless condition as he takes more and more intoxication, therefore incarcerating himself much more by basing his life on temporary, superficial pleasures, i.e., mainly sex life.

Kṛṣṇa, out of His intense love, has Himself entered into these dark planets of the dead to bring light. He speaks the science of liberation to Brahmā, who speaks to Nārada, who informs Vyāsa, who passes the message to Madhva, so that it continues in an unbroken disciplic line right down to yourself, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda. Your affixation on the orders of your spiritual master and your determination to keep this *paramparā* message intact engendered such profound compassion that you nobly took the task of going to the most hellish part of this world to preach to the most degraded *jīvas.* Even though we could not properly hear and failed you in so many ways, you kept guiding us and preaching to us up till your last breath. Now that Kṛṣṇa has removed you from our external perception, we have accomplished many things but again have disappointed you tremendously, because many of our actions have been a direct attack on the disciplic succession. Some of us whom you have deputized to be leaders have abused our positions, and others have begun to overreact in such a way that the future of our movement as a homogeneous entity is being threatened.

We are now at a stage of debate on all major issues and are often taking more shelter of legislation than consciousness elevating. Of course, legislation is of great significance, but if many who present laws are lawbreakers themselves by being guilty of not chanting sixteen rounds each day and by breaking other principles, then such laws will simply be tools for procrastination and even obfuscation. Those whose *sādhana* has become weak are developing a very poor resistance and are becoming influenced by negative sources. When such things begin to happen, one will find himself ultimately in a very precarious predicament. Let the debates go on, for surely the higher *bhakti* will prevail; however, anyone who is not following the basic principles has already disqualified himself from this arena.

The incoherences that we are now experiencing are of course nothing new, for as one examines Vaiṣṇava history one will see that most of these debates and conflicts were there not only in the Gauḍīya Math but even during the time of Madhva and Rāmānuja *ācāryas,* etc. Challenges and attacks are inevitable—they are actually a major part of our maturing. We should, however, learn from the mistakes of others, so as not to commit them ourselves. It also seems very obvious, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that those who are not situating themselves properly are failing you, as they are losing their spiritual equilibrium.

I am most fallen, as I am so arrogant as to agree to take up the post as one of your monitors (consult *Easy Journey to Other Planets* p. 34: concept of monitor-*guru*), when I know nothing except that you have begged us to DISTRIBUTE BOOKS AND GIVE UP SENSE GRATIFICATION.

Once, when you thanked me for certain services rendered, I mentioned to you that it was only by your mercy and that because you were forcing me to do such service that things were happening successfully. You quickly explained to me, "This is how *the paramparā* is going on." You said, "Just as my spiritual master has forced me, I am forcing you. This is *paramparā.'"* At that time I did not have the slightest appreciation of this position. Today, my dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am trying to increase my understanding and participation with this *paramparā* system by begging you to increase that pressure on me. Please force me to represent you nicely. I know for a fact if you do not force me to do the right thing I will be an embarrassment to you.

On this day of your Vyāsa-pūjā I thank you for allowing a fool like myself to try to once again catch up with you, to assist you in your mission for this world. Please force me to be an excellent monitor, so I can assist you properly rather than being a liability. I am very frightened, and at the same time very thankful to be able to help in carrying on the *paramparā* system. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the *jagad-guru, so* now use us as you see fit. We, all of your disciples, owe it to you to give up sense gratification so as to become your perfect representatives. By leaving us as you did, you are forcing us to become Kṛṣṇa conscious—otherwise, we will one by one fall to the wayside. If we do not remain fit, we will become casualties, and your divine *śakti* will leave those who are no longer fit and will manifest through others. Please teach us how to create such a powerful nucleus that everyone will find complete shelter in your ISKCON society, for it is you who can create a house that everyone can live in.

*Your lowest servant,*  
Bhaktitīrtha Swami

## Bhavānanda Goswami

On this day when our duty is to offer Your Divine Grace gifts,  
I can only meditate on the wonderful gifts you have given to us and the world.

By your selfless dedication to the order of your spiritual master  
You have shown us how to become perfect.

By forgoing sense gratification  
You have shown us how to experience the highest pleasure.  
By forceful and authoritative preaching  
You have shown us how to destroy the doubts we have as to the supremacy of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

All the examples of Kṛṣṇa conscious perfection and the process to achieve it you have recorded for posterity in your transcendental literature.  
That is the supreme and sublime gift which sets you apart as the crown jewel in the dazzling line of Vaiṣṇava deary as which decorate the body of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

The missionary work of all previous ācāryas has reached its culmination in your divine appearance.  
Your Divine Grace is their gift to us.  
You realized the essence of their desire and worked selflessly your entire life to manifest that desire on a global scale, through the medium of ISKCON.

ISKCON gives us all a glimpse of spiritual reality where all diverse personalities are able to work cooperatively to serve Kṛṣṇa. That is ISKCON's special beauty. Unity in Diversity!  
We would want it no other way.

Appreciating the opportunity which you have given us all to realize our unique, individual identities,  
I take this opportunity to rededicate myself to working in a humble way to serve Your Divine Grace and the lotus feet of your  
disciples who are working so diligently to carry your message of divine cooperation to all the nations of the world.

If I may beg one favor on this auspicious day, bless me that I may eternally be fixed, undeviating from your desire.

In 1976 in Delhi, I knelt before you and requested one favor. "Please," I said, "let me be eternally engaged in service to you any here you desire."  
"Because you are sincere, "you replied, "you will very quickly have your desire fulfilled. Now go and preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. Jai!"

My dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, I again beg that favor and appeal to you to bless me again with renewed enthusiasm to serve your divine lotus feet eternally.

*Śrīla Prabhupāda ki jaya!*  
*Bhavānanda Goswami*

## Girirāja Swami

**Eternal**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our eternal master.

When a disciple asked you what happens when we go to the spiritual world

You replied that the spiritual master introduces us to Kṛṣṇa.

"Then what about our relationship with you? Does it continue?"

"Yes, it continues.

"Just like when a father introduces his daughter to her husband,  
Her relationship with her father continues to exist."

We simply aspire to be your eternal servant.

We may be in heaven, hell, or Vaikuṇṭha,

Our first duty is to serve you.  
But how to serve you?  
When a disciple asked you,  
"How can we please?"  
You replied,  
"By desiring to please me  
You are pleasing me."

At this stage we have no other offering.

Girirāja Swami

## Harikeśa Swami

*(Sung to the tune of "Gurudeva")*

Prabhupāda,  
ISKCON founder-ācārya, spiritual master  
savior of the fallen souls  
Kṛṣṇa's mercy on us, all glories to you  
jaya jaya hari bol!

Prabhupāda, O lord and master, give us your shelter  
In your books are all conclusions  
Protect our tender creepers of pure devotion  
Help us free from all confusions

Engage us in your service, freed from all desires  
your mission only our direction  
Enable us to please you, help us to hear  
Let us be your pure reflections

(Bridge) (from "Ohe Vaiṣṇava Ṭhākura")  
Translating and printing all your books throughout the world  
Selling them for what they're worth o'er obstacles we're hurled  
Give us your mercy, give us your shelter, please . . .

Prabhupāda,  
We, your fallen servants, bow down before you  
begging you in all submission  
Keep us always busy, pleasing you and Kṛṣṇa  
expanding more your splendid mission

Don't let us forget you, O lord and master  
throughout the coming generations  
Let us worship you together, with deep devotion  
and chant and dance in pure elation

Prabhupāda  
Jaya Prabhupāda, Jaya Prabhupāda, Jaya Prabhupāda, Jaya Prabhupāda!

*Presented for your glorification and worship.*

*Your fallen servant,*  
Harikeśa Swami

## Hṛdayānanda dāsa Goswami

That colossal, roaring faith—  
That pure, bright, burning love—  
That fierce, fearless, courage—  
That is all you, Prabhupāda.

Stepping off the Jaladuta,  
You took a giant's bold steps  
On these lusty Western lands,  
Towering over the trivia  
Of our rowdy, metallic success,  
Exposing the emptiness  
Of our festering, famished hearts.

Then your giant, verbal hands  
Tore open the floodgates of love's healing ocean.  
The original, natural, medicinal, eternal yoga,  
Love of God, gushed jubilantly forth from heaven,  
Down to our wild, feverish hearts,  
And cured them.

And your mighty, devotional arms,  
Limbs of a better Atlas,  
Held mortal earth and spiritual sun  
Too together, bringing  
God's face to man and mortal to Him,

Joined in the sandalwood-fragrant, sanctified  
Reconciliatory mansion of your presence,  
Prabhupāda!

To you, Prabhupāda—  
Infinite bows and obeisances, on behalf  
Of our dwarfed universe, illumined by you,  
The official eyeball of God.  
And even I, O holy master,  
Grabbed timely by you, while  
Gallantly gulping, like the prince of mules,  
Poisoned barbs called mortal love,  
Clasping hard their slicing edge  
To my stupid bosom,  
Replete with barking sobs, reeking tears,  
For all the wicked things lost  
Already, in a brief, tenebrous life.

In the midst of all that, Prabhupāda—  
You saved me too, you save me, and always  
Will save me, dear, most dear Prabhupāda.

Hṛdayānanda dāsa Goswami

## Jagadīśa Goswami

**Kṛṣṇa's Ambassador**

He left the land of no return  
Requested by the Lord of light.  
To satisfy his master's yearn  
He crossed the sea of darkest night.

Protected by his golden Lord,  
Transporting that which would delight  
And liberate the drowning horde,  
He crossed the sea of darkest night.

He offered visas readily  
From many embassies of light  
And aided all who'd willingly  
Escape the sea of darkest night.

He reached so many yearning hearts  
To lift them to his ship of light  
And give them chance to then depart  
From their accursed sea of night.

Entire earth he wants to turn  
Into a transcendental flight  
Back to the land of no return,  
Out from the sea of darkest night.

Jagadīśa Goswami

## Jagajīvana dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

**"I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A, C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet."**

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**"Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and are delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism."**

As always  
Lamentation  
Presents her glance  
This time  
Every year  
And makes me think  
What can I say?  
What can I write?  
He whose realization is nil,  
What can he say?  
What can he write?

I would like to be Kṛṣṇa conscious  
I would like to be Prabhupāda conscious  
You taught  
To be Kṛṣṇa conscious  
Be attached to someone  
Attached to Kṛṣṇa  
So, to be Prabhupāda conscious  
Be attached to someone  
Attached to Prabhupāda.

Then  
The reality  
Of his presence  
Manifests—  
We are satisfied!

My dear brother  
Do not be  
Unchaste.  
Attach yourself  
Exclusively  
To those  
Attached fully  
To his service  
Serving always  
His lotus feet.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda’s  
ISKCON movement  
Ki jaya!

Jagajīvana dāsa Goswami

## Kīrtanānanda Swami

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

PLEASE accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet again and again. I am helpless in trying to express my appreciation to you for all you have done for me, for you have literally saved me from hell. Now this life is not mine; it is yours. You can do with me what you want. You are all-good in whatever you do.

Sometimes materialists, infatuated by mundane feelings of friendship and love, also express such sentiments; but they cannot endure. But this one I think was especially meant for you, and therefore I am including it in my offering today. I hope that I can forever maintain this mood:

Being your slave, what should I do but tend  
Upon the hours and times of your desire?  
I have no precious time at all to spend.  
Nor services to do, till you require.  
Nor dare I chide the world-without-end hour  
Whilst I, my sovereign, watch the clock for you.  
Nor think the bitterness of absence sour  
When you have bid your servant once adieu.  
Not dare I question with my jealous thought  
Where you may be, or your affairs suppose,  
But, like a sad slave, stay and think of nought  
Save where you are how happy you make those.  
So true a fool is love that in your will,  
Though you do anything, he thinks no ill.  
Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, just let me be your slave birth after birth after birth.

*Your lowest of the lowly sons,*  
Kīrtanānanda Swami

## Mukunda Goswami

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. Please give me the benediction to always remember you and never forget you.

"By the mercy of the spiritual master, one receives the benediction of the mercy of Kṛṣṇa."

I fervently pray that I may develop an awareness that you are always walking by my side or sitting near me, guiding my decisions and actions and purifying my recalcitrant mind.

You have assigned us the ostensibly impossible task of respiritualizing a society that often rages defiantly against God and His laws, but fortunately we have nonetheless discerned within your pure, transcendental instructions the inconceivable power of Lord Kṛṣṇa to impart to us the detailed wisdom we require to successfully execute your Kṛṣṇa conscious orders.

"To one who worships Me exclusively and with love, I give him the intelligence by which he can come to Me."

We are witnessing a great miracle. Almost eight years after your physical disappearance, you are still attracting millions of souls deeply submerged within the great ocean of Kali. This is possible because your transcendental desires are nondifferent from those of your dear Lord Kṛṣṇa, the greatest miracle-maker.

I pray that your ISKCON will begin to mature at least as rapidly as it seems to be growing; that the brotherhood and organizational expertise you repeatedly implored us to develop will manifest an infrastructure that will sustain, for centuries to come, a thriving network of worldwide cooperation; that a dramatically improved, revitalized and reconstituted ISKCON will more readily provide its members with all the spiritual, intellectual, cultural, social, communal, economic, psychological, and physical requirements for living peacefully together while scientifically propagating Lord Caitanya's *saṅkīrtana* movement to the far corners of the globe.

Every obstacle and adversity we encounter on the path to fulfilling your desires is but a test of our sincerity and determination. You often said that Kṛṣṇa is not cheap, that we shall not attain spiritual perfection with ease, but by austerity of body, mind, and words. You taught us that the more difficult the battle with Māyā, the sweeter the victory. You often said that "spiritual life is like a razor's edge."

In 1976 you made it clear to us that as ISKCON would grow, so would its detractors. You said that this also happened during the time of Lord Caitanya's *Kid* on earth, and that this was a symptom of ISKCON's potency and fidelity to Lord Caitanya. And certainly in past decades many new and unforseen challenges have arisen and indeed continue to test our capacity to carry out your desires with great faith and determination. No doubt many more adversities will arise in the future, but we know that by following your instructions to join together and work cooperatively in the way you wanted, these adversities will be turned into victories, one after the other.

Please bless me with the power to rely exclusively on the mercy of *guru* and Kṛṣṇa and not on anything mundane or independent of your desires. Please guide me in such a way that I shall not offend Vaiṣṇavas, and kindly arrange that I will always remain in the intimate association of those surrendered to your lotus feet. And please free me from every last vestige of material attachment, because I know that nothing less will satisfy and please you, and pleasing you is the only goal of my life.

Mukunda Goswami

## Pañcadraviḍa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
You came as an emissary of mercy, from Kṛṣṇa.  
Undaunted, you walked amidst the dust of this lifeless planet,  
Breathing life into dead forms, through the power  
Of the holy names, which always rest upon your lotus lips.

My mind became the servant of your smile,  
Your pleasure sought after  
As one longs to see the rays of the rising sun  
After the long night has covered all with darkness.

In India, how many times I heard the loudspeakers blare your message  
As I nestled myself close to the lion paws of your Vyāsāsana.  
As a lion protects her cubs (although death on the prowl),  
So you shelter all, but your words mean  
Death for one's material desires.  
I know only you, who have come to take us  
Back home, back to Godhead.  
As Kṛṣṇa never left Vṛndāvana, but stayed on  
In the hearts of His eternal servitors,  
So you too have never left us, but are locked up in the  
Hearts of those who serve your ISKCON.  
No one came, no one spoke, no one loved,  
No one braved the unknown,  
No one so full of compassion, or so personal as you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.  
Where are they, who stood up to be counted,  
Who surrendered to you, the Jagad-guru,  
Who knew your will, and your contract with Kṛṣṇa,  
Who came to give, who loved you?  
(None cared to aid the lone traveler.)  
My tears are only for you, for I know no other  
As my savior, who gave me Kṛṣṇa  
When I was down in this material world,  
With the mark of death upon my brow.

Now it is the beginning of the five hundredth year  
Since Lord Caitanya's advent,  
And from across the river, Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura blesses your movement  
As he looks out his window at Mahāprabhu’s city.  
While we walk with you in the early morning mist,  
You deftly poke your cane into the dust,  
You head is erect, your body wrapped in your wool chadar.  
We lean forward, hoping to catch your words  
In the dawn's new stillness of the fields of Śrī Māyāpur dhāma.  
My life is offered endlessly to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
As the surrendering process is eternal,  
And we live with you by your instructions.  
Let the challenge of time establish ISKCON to the world,  
So that you alone may be glorified  
As "Save the whole world. Śrīla Prabhupāda!"  
And let those who follow faithfully your lotus feet  
Wear the garland of mercy which you  
Award to your true followers.

I pray to have your darśana again,  
By my allegiance to your instructions.

*Your fallen servant,*  
Pañcadraviḍa Swami

## Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

You are so kind to have accepted this fallen soul as your disciple. I was born in the darkest ignorance and was living like a foolish animal, completely bereft of any spiritual understanding and only interested in material sense pleasure. By your causeless mercy you lifted me out of that bewildered condition and engaged me in the service of your lotus feet. When one takes shelter of a fully empowered, bona fide spiritual master like yourself, this is considered to be a second birth. A newborn baby has to be carefully protected by his parents, and because he cannot understand anything that is going on around him, his parents have to very patiently train him how to function in the world. Similarly, you gave protection to us, your spiritual children, and lovingly trained us to function on the spiritual platform. You taught us to see the material world for what it is—a miserable, hellish place full of pitfalls ready to entrap the conditioned soul. You taught us also that you had come with a sacred mission, given to you by your Guru Maharaj—to deliver the unfortunate conditioned souls from this miserable place and take them back to the spiritual world, the abode of Lord Kṛṣṇa. You requested that we, as your dutiful spiritual sons and daughters, should assist you in this mission and expand it to every nook and corner of the planet, in order to fulfill the prophecy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu that the holy name would be chanted in every town and village. This is not such an easy job, you said, because Māyā does not agree so easily to relinquish her grasp on the conditioned souls. Rather, she fights back viciously every time someone tries to escape her clutches. Intact, you often compared this mission to fighting a war. In a war, if the general is expert, then even if the soldiers are inexperienced, the army can be victorious. So we were all fresh, young recruits, but because you were present on the battlefield we took courage and fought valiantly to assist you. You provided us with unlimited ammunition in the form of your books and instructions, and you also established many transcendental base camps in the form of your temples, where the soldiers could rest for a while, and become inspired to go out and fight with renewed strength and enthusiasm. The ISKCON army was marching forward victoriously when suddenly you left us in the midst of the battle. You left by your own sweet will, in the most glorious manner, preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness up till the last moment, just as Bhīṣmadeva did. We were fearful that in the absence of our great general, the inexperienced army would flounder and be defeated. You told us not to worry, that you had trained us adequately, and if we just stood our ground and went on distributing more and more of your books, we would be victorious. Not only that, but you stated that you would live forever by this book distribution.

So now, by your divine instructions, and by the arrangement of your G.B.C. body, I find myself entrusted, even though still green and inexpert, with establishing the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement in part of South-east Asia. On several occasions you ordered me to "establish an empire of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, just as the British had established an empire by their courage and determination." This is not an easy task for one so unqualified, especially since these countries are full of grossly ignorant people bent on materialistic sense gratification. Often I wonder how I am going to be successful in carrying out your order. But then I think that every empire needs an emperor, and that you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the real emperor, so somehow or other, if we can keep you in the forefront by always placing great emphasis on producing and distributing your books, then success is guaranteed. Furthermore, I would like to thank you for giving me the association of so many wonderful and qualified disciples and granddisciples of yours. By their courage and determination, in the face of great adversity, we have been able to establish a few more base camps for your transcendental army. Now we are enthusiastically looking forward to producing more and more of your books in all the different languages here and dropping these "bombs" into the laps of the conditioned souls.

So far as I myself am concerned, I would like to ask you one benediction on this auspicious day. That is, that I may always have the great fortune to be personally involved in printing your books, and also in going out on *saṅkīrtana* to distribute them. I often ponder where we will get the intelligence and inspiration to manage and develop an entire zone, but when we go out for book distribution, then our intelligence becomes sharpened, and we feel a great surge of transcendental enthusiasm. This is because by such service one gets very intimate association with you, the source of all intelligence and inspiration, and thus even the most difficult tasks become easy. Once while I was traveling with the BBT library party in India, you told me that "the first soldier who goes out on the front lines \[to distribute books\] is the real hero." I think it is important that those of us who have to take up positions of leadership should always be most conscious of what it means to go out on the front lines, just as in Vedic times the kings would always be in the forefront of the battle. This will keep us very closely in touch with the preachers and enable us to inspire them by personal example. You once said that "this movement is not for eating and sleeping, but for going door to door to preach the message of *Bhagavad-gītā."* Personally, I find this activity to be most relishable, so it is my humble request that you please never allow me to become complacent, lazy, or bewildered, but kindly always push me out on *saṅkīrtana,* to preach your glories and distribute your books.

*I beg to remain your eternal servant,*  
Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

## Rāmeśvara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

WE ARE beginning to understand the meaning of love by loving you. Your desire to liberate all fallen souls has become our obsession. The practical management of your movement involves bearing continuous burdens, and accepting all of them without reservation is the test of our love. Each new challenge or difficulty must be seen at once as our happiness. We should never feel even a slight tinge of distaste or hesitation in serving you in any way required. This spontaneous service attitude is the awakening of the transcendental stage of our love for you. To achieve this, our hearts must be freed from all material desires, and our senses must no longer have any attraction for material gratification, headed by sex attraction. I know we cannot be pretentious or have any of the subtle material motives of a separatist.

You act like Kṛṣṇa in entering into increasing loving relationships with your surrendered devotees. Externally, they are empowered to fulfill your many desires with great, efficient, and pleasing results. Internally, they are empowered to remember you at every step, and take your lotus feet into their hearts. Whatever they touch or receive is instantly your property. They are factually possessionless and never hanker for anything from this material society, because they are exclusively satisfied by just the nine processes of devotional service.

Prabhupāda, I think that to become a pure devotee of your mission (your lotus feet) is the most satisfying pleasure of love I have ever been able to imagine. I have seen how material affection simply dilutes our devotion for you and, like an ignorant intoxicant, covers our realization of the urgency of your mission and the urgency of entering into the ecstatic loving relationships you are offering us. Therefore, I am convinced that undivided loving service to you is the most intense pleasure in this world. I am profoundly lamenting my impurity and endeavoring my utmost to reach this stage, however long it takes. A glimpse of realization of this love can strengthen us for more than even this lifetime, and thus we may be able to say that you may do with us whatever you like, but we will never slacken or quit our service.

*Your aspiring devotee,*  
Rāmeśvara Swami

## Rūpānuga dāsa

*Dear Śrīla Prqhhupada.*

Please accept my humble obeisances.

One of my best recollections of you is how you were always so uncompromising. For example, during the late 1960s, many of the young people around you were influenced by antiwar sentiments. Nevertheless, you preached that a Vaiṣṇava knew when violence was necessary, that there could be war for the right cause, like the Battle of Kurukṣetra, and that wars will always be with us, as they have been since time immemorial. You insisted that intoxicants could have no spiritual value at all. You refused to grant dispensations about what we could eat: "Kṛṣṇa doesn't take any, so we cannot offer it." You wouldn't accept less than sixteen rounds and four regulative principles from your initiated followers. You presented the Fifth Canto unadulterated, knowing that some would lose faith. You admonished your unjustly accused disciples to go into a New York courtroom and speak so straightforwardly that the case was quickly won, although you were prepared to fight it for fourteen years. You got your own example from Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Prabhupāda: "For my part," you said, "I have taken up the policy of Guru Mahārāja—no compromise. All these so-called scholars, scientists, philosophers who do not accept Kṛṣṇa are nothing more than rascals, fools, lowest of mankind, etc." Ultimately, you offered Kṛṣṇa consciousness exactly as it is, without watering it down in hopes of winning over more followers.

There is always a danger of compromise. The weed of compromise disguises itself as preaching, but it only encourages more economics, sectarianism, mundane desires, or impersonalism *(artha, dharma, kāma, mokṣa).* Even Śrīla Vyāsadeva himself attempted to compromise, avoiding the direct presentation of the Absolute Truth Personality of Godhead.

Thinking how to benefit the people in general, he compiled scriptures for everyone, according to their activities under the modes of material nature. Concerned about the future of the less intelligent, he compiled the histories to capture their interest. Yet he remained dissatisfied in his heart (despite his great qualifications) until Narada Muni explained that the people would not actually benefit. Rather, they would avoid any prohibitions expected of them by using their intelligence to worship the mundane in the name of religion. When Śrīla Vyāsadeva realized that any dissatisfaction he felt was due to his compromise of the truth, in great happiness he became directly merciful to the people by presenting the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* without any deference to their material desires.

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, seated on the *vyāsāsana* (the seat of Vyāsadeva), personally gave us Kṛṣṇa's purifying association in the form of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* with your Bhaktivedanta purports. On this day of Vyāsa-pūjā, celebrated once each year, we honor you because you have truly represented Śrīla Vyāsadeva. From the *vyāsāsana* you spoke only the Absolute Truth.

Thank you for teaching us how to be uncompromising, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and by your incessant blessings may we always remain uncompromisers, like you.

*Your most fallen disciple,*  
Rūpānuga dāsa

## Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

On the occasion of the Vyāsa-pūjā day of His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, I would like to address the disciples and granddisciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda on an important method for demonstrating our obedience and love for Śrīla Prabhupāda. And unless we can act in obedience to Prabhupāda, then how can we claim to sincerely love or worship him? Obedience means we have to avoid the four kinds of sinful activities and always chant sixteen rounds daily. But to be a true Prabhupāda follower we also have to take up some kind of practical devotional service, and in order to do that, we have to work *in the association of devotees.* Therefore, our efforts to always stay in favorable association with his devotees, despite the difficulties that may bring us, is a real test of whether we are genuine followers of Śrīla Prabhupāda. As he said, "Your love for me will be shown by how much you cooperate to keep this institution together after I am gone."

Śrīla Prabhupāda made it clear that association with devotees means to live in connection with the society he created and worked for, the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. Those who advocate that ISKCON may be abandoned are actually working against Śrīla Prabhupāda, even if they quote his name or words in their arguments. They who work against ISKCON become the enemies of Prabhupāda's mission. To break away from the society of temples and the activities of ISKCON, to select other leaders or doctrines—these are the activities of the schism-makers, whom Śrīla Prabhupāda has warned us about in the pages of the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta.*

While discussing how the followers of Advaita Ācārya created a schism, Śrīla Prabhupāda gives the modern example of the Gaudiya Math after the disappearance of Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. The cause of this grievous split, according to Śrīla Prabhupāda, was that certain parties did not follow the Governing Body Commission, as was the desire of the *ācārya.* "Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, at the time of his departure," writes Prabhupāda, "requested all of his disciples to form a Governing Body and conduct missionary activities cooperatively . . . despite the spiritual master's order to form a Governing Body and execute the missionary activities of the Gaudiya Math, the two unauthorized factions began litigation . . ."

During the years that Śrīla Prabhupāda was personally with us, I never imagined that any splits could occur. But while traveling with Śrīla Prabhupāda as his secretary in 1974,I personally witnessed how, even in his presence, some of his disciples were declaring themselves leaders in defiance of the G.B.C. and ISKCON and trying to claim shelter and authorization by Prabhupāda. As Prabhupāda traveled he strongly dealt with deviances and indicated that his sincere followers should work within his ISKCON. I wrote about my feeling, and about an exchange I had with Prabhupāda on this subject, in *Life with the Perfect Master,* which I would like to quote here:

It seemed to me a most dangerous thing, a split. So I wanted to ask Prabhupāda something more about it, although up to now I had not been much involved. At an opportune time, I approached Śrīla Prabhupāda and asked, "Śrīla Prabhupāda, what about splits in our movement?"

Prabhupāda immediately cut me off. "There is no such thing as a split," he said. "There is only insincerity, that's all. I chant sixteen rounds and follow the principles and preach, and you do also. There is no split. Only if one is insincere and doesn't follow."

I said no more. My very uttering of the word "split" had seemed impertinent to him. I took his fiery reaction to mean that there is no justification for disciples' working against the spiritual master's movement. His movement and the directions he gives are perfectly clear, and everyone should follow. If someone doesn't follow, he cannot be credited with creating something separate, as if the "split" is a new spiritual entity. Rather, such a person dies spiritually. He becomes *asāra,* useless, as had the disobedient sons of Advaita Ācārya, whom Prabhupāda had described in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* purport. Those who leave ISKCON are *apa-sampradāya.* They are like a branch broken off from a tree; they wither away. At least that is my understanding. And from what I saw of Śrīla Prabhupāda in action as he traveled from Hawaii to Japan to Hong Kong, fighting an outbreak of propaganda against his ISKCON, he gave no credence to a split.

Therefore, to continue working together favorably, even though there are differences, is to pass the test of the spiritual master. Śrīla Prabhupāda acknowledged that there could be differences of opinion among his disciples as to the best way to preach or please the spiritual master. Nevertheless, differing parties had to ultimately agree with the order of the spiritual master or his authorized representatives. Prabhupāda compared such disagreement to the different viewpoints held by political parties, who nevertheless work and come together for the ultimate good of the nation. Even in the spiritual world, there are sometimes different parties among the *gopis,* but the goal of loving service to Kṛṣṇa is never disrupted by schisms or by any fighting that would disturb the pastimes of Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Within this world, within the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya, we will please Śrīla Prabhupāda by staying within the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, accepting the authority of temple / zonal leaders and G.B.C., and either living within an ISKCON *dhāma* or cooperating closely with one. In a purport in the *Upadeśāmṛta,* Śrīla Prabhupāda compares such association of devotees in ISKCON with the association of the stock exchange for businessmen. His analogy is that those who want to take real advantage of business opportunities put themselves in the favorable association that is found at the stock exchange rather than in some alienated position. Similarly, we should be looking for the fullest opportunity for going back to home, back to Godhead, and discharging the orders of the spiritual master—and that opportunity is found in the society of devotees, as created by Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Some say that ISKCON was once Prabhupāda's society, but now it is no longer his movement, because it bears no similarity to the movement he founded. But these enemies cannot prove that the spirit has actually left the body of ISKCON. They can point to the mistakes of this movement, and its growing pains, but there are too many positive signs of life to declare ISKCON spiritually dead. There are too many signs of surrender to Prabhupāda and Kṛṣṇa. Thinking about this recently, I wrote the following poem while en route to the 1985 Māyāpur festival:

Signs of Life  
Tiny sparks, blessed to burn,  
Fly to India now,  
Bright embers of the ISKCON fire.  
(And in every Kṛṣṇa temple  
They 're worshiping their Lords.)  
Who claims these are signs of death?  
Has the Caitanya-candrodaya Mandira vanished?  
Are there no pilgrims this year?  
Wherefrom comes this strong kīrtana sound?

Because they have deserted  
They say all have deserted;  
Like the deaf who claim all others are deaf.  
They no longer come to Māyāpur,  
But a thousand faithful do,  
Dancing under the order  
Of the śaktyāveśa saint.  
"But devotees quarrel as to who should be guru!"  
Yes, but differing is also a sign of life,  
As long as they merge, after quarrels,  
In the Ganges, and together dance at kīrtana,  
As long as they reside  
In the Māyāpur dhāma  
And worship together  
Rādhā-Madhava and Prabhupāda.

Let us pray, therefore, to Prabhupāda and Kṛṣṇa that we disciples and all followers of Prabhupāda may always work together in his movement, despite our inevitable differences of opinion. It may be more difficult nowadays to settle differences, since we cannot go into Prabhupāda's room in two dissenting groups and have him immediately make a decision and pacify all concerned. But there is still full opportunity for us to satisfy Prabhupāda by serving in separation and using the means of cooperation he has given us, by *iṣṭa-goṣṭhī,* by philosophical exchanges based on *śāstra,* by Vaiṣṇava etiquette, and by submission to the decisions of the Governing Body Commission. Within these boundaries there is yet full scope for individual expression and for creation of preaching results that are pleasing to Śrīla Prabhupāda and all the previous *ācāryas.* And outside these guidelines there is *apa-sampradāya.*

Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

## Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami

In your footsteps we must tread,  
Free of Māyā’s menacing hold.  
Calm, serene,  
Peacefully established  
With Kṛṣṇa.

Steadfast you stood  
Against Māyā’s mighty agents:  
The sensuous serpent's fangs  
lay broken at your feet,  
Anger's vicious whip  
silent and limp,  
Burning lust  
reduced to ashes,  
And greed searching forlornly  
amidst desire's vacant house.

Will you please, Prabhupāda,  
Teach us how to be austere?  
Transfer earth s burden  
To our young and willing shoulders?  
Do not worry—  
We shall hold her  
Above attachment's ugly face,  
For we are walking upon the path of renunciation.

Are we pure?  
Others question, we worry Are we pure?  
For while you were present, Purīty  
Gushed forcefully from your being.  
Now we are attempting to preserve a few drops  
That are yet enough  
to nourish the world

How difficult tolerance of  
Another's presence, others' thoughts.  
"Can the world accommodate more than one me?  
Only if they follow my plan. "  
Prabhupāda, dearmost master,  
Please teach us to be insignificant  
before Kṛṣṇa,  
Who is everywhere,  
At all times.

You, a spiritual giant.  
Lived amidst we ants,  
And nobody got hurt by your lotus footsteps,  
Whose vast imprints form the continents  
in which we now dwell.  
O master,  
Teach us that mystic art  
That makes the greatest  
become the smallest.

One who knows Kṛṣṇa  
knows everything,  
And one who knows your books  
knows Kṛṣṇa.  
Thus your books,  
by revealing Kṛṣṇa,  
Provide all the knowledge to guide the world.

O Prabhupāda,  
More than ever before, we pray;  
Please help us—  
To believe,  
To practice,  
To distribute and  
To teach  
what you have written  
in your books,  
So that we may lead the world to follow  
in your footsteps.

Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Gosvāmī

## Vāsudeva dāsa

*Dear Śrīla Prahhupada,*

On this glorious day of your appearance I offer my humble *daṇḍavats* at your lotus feet again and again.

You are unlimitedly merciful, and as such, your mercy cannot be expressed by us in words. By your causeless mercy you have given us secure shelter at your lotus feet. I beg for your blessings that I can remain in your service birth after birth.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your humble servant,*  
Vāsudeva dāsa

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from ISKCON centers around the world

## Abeokuta

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

"When Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the great gardener, sees that people are chanting, dancing, and laughing, and that some of them are rolling on the floor and some are making loud humming sounds. He smiles with great pleasure." (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi* 9.50)

When Your Divine Grace came to America, you came along as a gardener assistant with a seed that was planted where there was unfertile land. But because of your total surrender to the lotus feet of Śrī Gaurasundara, the land became very fertile and had some product like Kīrtanānanda Swami and others. When He (Kīrtanānanda Swami) and others chanted the *mahā-mantra,* they were forced by the transcendental potency of the Lord's name to dance in an ecstatic way. When you saw this, you smiled with great pleasure.

Thus, following in your footsteps, Kīrtanānanda Swami has taken the seed to the lowest side of the world, Africa, where the seed was also planted by your order. It has grown up to a point where Kīrtanānanda Swami now has another gardener assistant, Bhaktitīrtha Swami, and they are very dear to you.

Your totality of surrender to Kṛṣṇa is beyond mundane calculation. Your mercy to the world is inestimable and can only be experienced by your pure devotees, because they have totally surrendered to your divine lotus feet. At this point I pray that your causeless mercy may continue to flow forever, because I know that it has no end, unlimitedly.

All glories to your lotus feet.

*Your grandsons at ISKCON Abeokuta, Nigeria,*

**Granddisciples**

Parīkṣit dāsa / Upendra dāsa / Yamarāja dāsa / Haridāsa dāsa / Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam dāsa / Saccidānanda dāsa / Bhakta Mohammed / Bhaktin Dorcas

## Adelaide

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

**"I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet."**

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**"Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and are delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism."**

The living entity in the material world has come under the stringent laws of material nature and consequently has to undergo the repeated cycle of birth and death and the subsequent miseries thereof. Taking shelter of so-called family, society, friendship, and love, he endeavors for the achievement of lasting happiness but never achieves such, as he is looking in the wrong place. Under the influence of *māyā,* we are chasing illusory happiness and achieving only disappointment.

It is advised by the Lord Himself that if one wants to cease this hard struggle for existence, then he must take shelter of His bona fide representative, the spiritual master.

*tad viddhi praṇipātena*  
*paripraśnena sevayā*  
*upadekṣyanti te jñānaṁ*  
*jñāninas tattva-darśinaḥ*

**"Just try to learn the truth by approaching the spiritual master. Inquire from him submissively and render service unto him. The self-realized soul can impart knowledge unto you because he has seen the truth."**

This meeting with the spiritual master is likened to a second birth. In the material world we get many mothers and fathers, life after life. But the relationship which we can establish with the spiritual master is very rare indeed.

*brahmāṇḍa brahmite kona bhagyavān jīva*  
*guru kṛṣṇa prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bhīja*

**"The living entities are wandering throughout this material universe, but by the mercy of Guru and Kṛṣṇa they can get the seed of devotional service and can go back home, back to Godhead."**

*yasya deve parā bhaktir*  
*yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ*  
*prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

**"Only unto those great souls who have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed."**

So it is very obvious that the secret for success in spiritual life is to take complete shelter of the spiritual master. Of course, that does not mean that one must always have the personal association of the spiritual master. That is not always possible. We have to learn to take shelter of the instructions of the spiritual master, for this is a higher association. And when one becomes a little steady in following those instructions, he can begin to appreciate the great gift that the spiritual master has given.

A person who was in a very diseased condition and has been cured by the expert physician naturally feels intense gratitude towards his benefactor. Similarly, the disciple who has been saved from the disease of *avidyā,* material ignorance, should feel that he owes a great debt to his spiritual master. Since the spiritual master is fully satisfied in his relationship with Kṛṣṇa, nothing material we could offer him would be of any significance. What does please the spiritual master, though, is to see his disciples carefully following his instructions and making advancement in their Kṛṣṇa consciousness. And on their part, the disciples should want, like responsible sons, to assist the spiritual master in his work. This pleases the spiritual master even more.

Vyāsa-pūjā is the time when the disciple remembers this debt he owes the spiritual master and renews again his pledge to devote himself to the lotus feet of his spiritual master.

ALL GLORIES TO ŚRĪLA PRABHUPĀDA!!!

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Kṛpākara dāsa / Bhūtanātha dāsa / Uṣāpati dāsa / Ādi-puruṣa dāsa

## Ahmedabad

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. Who can understand the transcendental glories of your lotus feet, which traversed all over the earth fifteen times and delivered every town and village of this earth by direct and indirect presence?

Certainly, after the disappearance of your Guru Mahārāja, this world was thirsty for spiritual direction. As you have mentioned, when the *ācārya* leaves the body, there is always some darkness, chaos, and disturbance. There was a great impasse, spiritual vacuum. Mediocre preachers could not turn the demoniac course of the fast-degrading Kali-yuga. The transcendental glories of the holy name sounded empty, and the scriptures appeared exaggerations. The power of Vaiṣṇavas was never seen. Only animalistic politicians, godless scientists, the industrial entrepreneurs, and the cinema stars looked respectable. In the history books, the stories of Lord Rāma and Kṛṣṇa were being replaced by those of demoniac politicians. *Gurukulas* were being replaced by modern slaughterhouse schools and colleges. Sense gratification was the only goal of human civilization. Simple village life was turning into demoniac city life. The modes of passion and ignorance reigned supreme. Your God-brothers lost hope. They gave up. They said, "We were thinking that Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu has predicted that ‘In every town and village My holy name will spread,’ but that is impossible, that should not be taken literally." They were right, because there was no hope. There was despair everywhere. Everything has changed, within just fifty years.

But you were different. When everyone else was ready to give up, you were building up strength to attack the Kali-yuga. While others advised, "Do not waste your time by going abroad. At the most you can give those foreigners some lectures, but you can never make them Vaiṣṇavas," you were dreaming of a worldwide community of Vaiṣṇavas, temples, *gurukulas,* millions of books, and high court judges with *tilaka.* Just as the earth was overburdened by the demoniac kings before Kṛṣṇa's appearance, the earth was overburdened by demoniac politicians, demoniac scientists, industrialists, and *bhogi-yogīs* before the appearance of your movement. By the middle of the twentieth century, the world was fast reaching its peak in scientific advancement—big cars, televisions, rock stars. The human being was reduced to a polished animal. The stage was perfectly set to destroy the rat-race civilization. Like the demigods appearing in the Yadu dynasty and Gokula, so many of your assistants had already taken birth as hippies, frustrated youths, the distressed, all seeking material happiness, sometimes inquisitive, and renounced young men and women. All the paraphernalia for delivering the world was accumulated. Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi's mercy, the grace of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, the *gopīs,* the cowherd boys of Vṛndāvana, the blessings of *ācāryas* Rūpa Gosvāmī, Sanātana Gosvāmī, Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, and Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī were invoked by you. The power to write the Vedic literature was granted to you. Simply equipped with the wealth of three volumes of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* you caused the reappearance of Kṛṣṇa in the form of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness—ISKCON. And with that, the reappearance of *sanātana-dharma.* The human race, which was almost dead, got a new lease of life. The world was rescued from going to the dogs. The glories of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa were proved beyond the shadow of a doubt. Thousands of Ajāmilas and thousands of Jagāis and Mādhāis were saved. The words of *śāstras* came true: the authenticity of the scriptures was reestablished. The Indians regained self-respect, self-confidence. Faith in religion, pious life, and austerity was reestablished. You did the greatest service to mother India; you were the greatest ambassador indeed. Embodied with the mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, you flooded the world with chanting, dancing, and feasting! All the directions were full with the vibration *paraṁ vijayate śrī-kṛṣṇa-saṅkīrtanam.*

Our words fail to describe your glories. Someday Śrīla Vedavyāsa will compose poetry in your glorification, and Nārada will broadcast it all over the universe. Even the most wretched Kali-yuga has become glorified because of your presence on this earth. The most fallen and unfortunate men of the twentieth century have been given the benediction of *prema-bhakti,* by your causeless mercy. Even for those who have not been able to surrender their entire lives at your lotus feet, there is a great sense of religious revival. So many pseudo spiritual movements have sprung up today, like weeds, along with the expansion of the Caitanya tree. But you are the most expert gardener, and the mood of your carefully nurtured movement will go on shining, eternally unchecked by the glowworms.

As far as we are concerned, we just cannot imagine how much we managed to squeeze into those twelve short years of your fully manifest pastimes as a world teacher. Those precious moments with you are our life and soul, the only sustaining force in our otherwise crippled lives. We pray at your lotus feet on this auspicious day that we may never deviate even an inch from your path, and thus have strength to continuously expand your mission, which is the only hope for humanity. It was your humble desire to impress the masses of India, and now slowly it is happening. Your movement is slowly emerging as the most prominent spiritual force amongst all other *dsramas* and institutions. People have started respecting your followers as genuine *sādhus,* and we are very sure that if we just work hard and preach relentlessly, there will be a total spiritual revolution, beginning from India and extending in all directions.

Please accept our thousands and millions of prostrations again and again. Please give us a drop of your mercy and purify us.

*Your eternal servants in ISKCON Gujarat,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**
