## Denver

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this especially significant day of your appearance on the planet, we pray to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that we may increase our service to you and Lord Kṛṣṇa by better understanding your instructions to us. Because of these instructions, this movement is able to go on even though *māyā* may test us with the seemingly overwhelming odds that we have been able to transcend. Because so many times we are not appreciating enough your position and instructions, we are losing the opportunity to preach, or on so many occasions we are even risking the reputation of the ISKCON society

As the years pass since your departure from this planet, people will come to realize your greatness and your glories. Without discrimination, you single-handedly distributed love of God to countless living entities of this planet. It is impossible for us as neophytes to understand or even estimate your transcendental position, but gradually, as your preaching work takes effect on the people of the world, we will see a glimmer of your greatness.

Just as the order of Lord Caitanya is to spread this movement to every town and village, you have followed these instructions perfectly. Although we are very fallen, you have allowed us to assist you in your mission to save the world. We have nothing to offer you in return, because everything we have, you have given us. We only hope that you will kindly accept our bodies, minds, and words in pushing on this *saṅkīrtana* movement. This is your causeless mercy, and our only qualification for understanding the sublime message of Lord Caitanya.

So it is our anxiety, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that when will these conditioned souls give up the cycle of birth and death and simply hear your messages of love of Godhead, as we have done, and become ecstatic? The suffering of the conditioned souls is due to their being neglectful of the order of the Supreme Lord. Therefore great saints and sages have concluded, after careful study, that the problems of life, especially in Kali-yuga, can be solved by the methods of widespread propaganda to promote *bhakti-yoga,* or devotional service to Kṛṣṇa. So we as your disciples do not know any other shelter than your lotus feet, which you so kindly placed upon our heads in the form of your books. Therefore we hold these books very close to our heart, along with the order "Distribute my books! Distribute my books! Distribute my books!" because by this distribution the unfortunate inhabitants of Kali-yuga will be delivered.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have saved us from the cycle of repeated birth and death. If somehow or other, by your causeless mercy, we could just keep distributing your books and remember with appreciation your glorious pastimes, our lives will be ecstatic and completely successful.

*Your servants in ISKCON Colorado,*

## Detroit

It was September 7, 1977. Your Divine Grace had arrived in England some days before on what was to be your last visit to the West. Your body was ravaged by the effects of illness and old age, but the spirit was indomitable—guiding and inspiring us in those precious days by the living and vivid example of" the soul's utter transcendence over the material situation. For even though your body scarcely allowed anything but the most basic of activities and was constantly a source of pain, you were so transparently absorbed in devotional service. Every morning your loving disciples would carry you (you were much too weak to walk) from your quarters to the temple room, where you would greet Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gokulānanda. After taking *caraṇāmṛta,* you were taken to the raised dais at the back of the temple room, where your *vyāsāsana* was placed. There you stayed, sitting in the simple palanquin that was used to carry you, watching a sea of assembled devotees as they performed *kīrtana,* swaying and rocking with upraised arms. For months the devotees had been praying, "My dear Lord Kṛṣṇa, if You so desire, please save Śrīla Prabhupāda." And this *kīrtana* was a focus for all those intense desires. Now the devotees seemed to be petitioning you directly, "Please stay with us." As the *kīrtana* became more ecstatic, you nodded approval, and the ecstasy increased. Sometimes if we looked closely we could see tears gliding down your hollow cheeks, and we would perhaps foolishly imagine that we could understand a little of the transcendental emotions that you were feeling as you watched your loving disciples. This particular day was very significant, the occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā, and even though the devotees would not and could not conceive that it was possibly the last time that you would personally be present on such a day, there was obviously a marked and special poignancy surrounding the event. After the *kīrtana,* one of your very dear disciples began to recount, in your presence, the story of your great mission—the story of a sublime vision of the victory of Kṛṣṇa consciousness gradually being translated into fact—a story of great sacrifice and struggle of complete surrender and, above all, of overwhelming compassion. Sometimes you would interject, adding something the speaker had overlooked. At each turn of the story the devotees cheered. Sometimes your interjection would make us laugh. So there we were, sometimes laughing and sometimes crying, always gripped with awe, listening to the greatest story ever told.

No one who was there would ever forget the enriching experience. We had all shared in something that was very special and that would be indelibly inscribed in our hearts and would help shape the course of our lives.

Then just a few months later you were no longer with us. Your disappearance brought us to the brink of an abyss. A great chasm of emptiness threatened to engulf us. But even in those dark days you continued to guide us. Your instructions, the training you had given us, your books—all showed us the way ahead, and that great hurdle was surmounted. Enthused with the desire to work cooperatively, your disciples carried on your great mission.

And so it was that on August 27 of the next year the devotees gathered together again at Bhaktivedanta Manor to honor the occasion of your appearance, the remembrance of that historic day the previous year still burned into their memory. The day was to be celebrated in the way that you had taught us. The formalities proceeded very well, for we had been well taught. There was an ecstatic *guru-pūjā,* after which the new Vyāsa-pūjā book was offered. It was when we started to read sections from the Vyāsa-pūjā book that the decorum that had hitherto governed began to break down. One devotee was reading the story recounted in the book by someone who had met you in the very early days. You had offered him a *dhotī* to wear, which the boy hesitatingly refused, proffering that he was not quite ready. As he left, you walked after him with tears in your eyes, pleading with him to please come back. Something in that small incident, one of many thousands of similar ones, sparked a remembrance into the mind of the devotee who was reading, a feeling perhaps of love or of gratitude so deep that it stopped the words in his throat. Someone else then tried to read an offering by one of your personal servants who is now a G.B.C. man and initiating *guru.* He described in very moving terms and in great detail how in his heart that personal service was continuing. Our distress was almost too great to bear, but it wasn't the loss that was breaking our hearts, it was the feeling of being brought so close to you even in separation. Now every detail of your glories and every realization of every devotee would further increase the ocean of separation. Grown men and women were sobbing unashamedly, and try as we may, we could not recapture the tone of formality that the occasion required. And yet the whole event was invested with a dignity all of its own. And even though the joyousness of the association with you the previous year was now replaced with an almost unutterable melancholy, we were all once again sharing something beyond material comprehension. It was not the ordinary grief born of material attachment, but it was a deep and abiding common love for the person who had saved us and who we could now sense would never leave us, even till the end of time.

Now there are several long years between us and those awesome days, but that love that we felt in 1977 and 1978 continues to be the impetus and sustaining principle in our society as we stand before you on this day in 1985. We may waver or even stray, but we can never forget. We have been touched by you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and so will always remember the sacrifices you have made for us and for the whole of mankind. And as our Society continues to grow and to have greater and greater impact on the world, it will always be our love for you that binds us together, sustains us, and provides us with the strength to go on. Because you continue to be with us in this way, we have confidence that despite the many trials we will have to face, our endeavors in your name and on your behalf will be successful.

(Written by Vicitravirya dāsa)

*We, the residents of Devasadana Mandira, Detroit, beg to remain always in the shade of your lotus feet.*

## Durban

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.*

Please accept our humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you!

In the years to come, this Vyāsa-pūjā day of yours is going to become one of the most important occasions in the world's calendar. Whereas nowadays the people in general may think that the birthdays of personalities such as Christ and Muhammad are the most important days, we know for sure that gradually the significance of your appearance will eclipse other events and be seen for what it is—unique.

Of course, we are not trying to belittle these great personalities in any way; but still the fact must be recognized. The real credit must be given. You came to the Western world and gave it what it had not had, practically since the beginning of this Kali-yuga—pure love of God, as taught directly by the Supreme Lord Himself. Appropriate recognition of this fact will be given by everyone eventually, and when this happens, it will be on a scale that we cannot even imagine in our wildest dreams now. Hundreds of millions of people in every town and village will chant your glories and dance in ecstasy.

In you the concepts of *śaktyāveśa-avatāra* and fully surrendered devotee are quintessentially balanced. You are a ray of Viṣṇu, descended to this dismal world from the Lord's side to sprinkle the dust from His lotus feet on the heads of the conditioned souls. You are simultaneously the most exemplary devotee and disciple, enthusiastically taking the mission given by your spiritual master as your life and soul, and steadfastly enduring any hardships you encounter on your path.

As the perfect *mahā-bhāgavata,* you have resoundingly defeated the cunning arguments of the materialistic intellectuals. Your explanations of the Absolute Truth are so multifaceted that any person will find within your books arguments just appropriate to convince him, regardless of his background.

Even in your absence we are seeing your extraordinary position proven. Let the envious "doubting Thomases" say what they may, but we see by direct experience that whoever strictly adheres to your instructions and your preaching spirit becomes an empowered, inspired devotee, and that whoever does not becomes sunken once again in *māyā’s* whirlpool of contradictions. You are not just an artistic cosmetician who can cleverly cover over his client's sores, but you are an expert surgeon, who cuts out his patient's disease right at the roots.

In your physical absence, it is our duty to carry you forward to your rightful place at the forefront of everyone's consciousness. To us now it seems that it may be a Herculean feat, to say the least, as we are blind, lame, dumb, and like children spiritually. But we know that if we accept your divine grace—your divine mercy in the form of your instructions and your example—we will undoubtedly develop the ability to see this world simply as facility for service to Kṛṣṇa, the strength to carry your mission to everyone throughout this planet, the eloquence to present the essential truth succinctly, and the intelligence to grasp the jewels of knowledge of the Absolute that you have given us. In this way only will the qualifications needed for us to achieve our goals come.

And finally, when the world has accepted your sublime position in human society, has realized the fact that you were able to purify and empower persons as lowly as ourselves, then they willfully understand your uniqueness.

*Your servants of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rādhānātha Temple, Durban, South Africa.*

## Fiji

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.*

Please accept our prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet.

On this most glorious day of Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā, we do not have sufficient adjectives to describe your unlimited glories. Moreover, we are not even qualified to make an attempt. Nevertheless, the *least* we will say is, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you taught us the *most* direct and sublime method of worshiping the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, for which we will remain eternally indebted to you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have trained your senior children so expertly in the performance of devotional service to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, as well as in the management of spiritual life, that we, your younger children and grandchildren, do not have to fear the onslaughts of the illusory energy, provided we strictly adhere to their instructions and ultimately surrender our lives to them.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we feel very safe and secure in your movement. On this auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, please bless us so that we remain in your movement and continue to serve you and Śrī Kṛṣṇa Kāliya eternally.

Śrīla Prabhupāda ki jaya!

(Written by Jagannātha dāsa)

*Your unworthy servants of ISKCON Fiji.*

## Finland

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances as we fall at your lotus feet with all the humility that we can possibly offer you on this, your Vyāsa-pūjā day. Actually, it's a fact that whatever we may offer to you has been given to us by you; therefore you do not depend on anyone except Kṛṣṇa. As soon as we think we have done something ourselves, at that point everything fails, and false ego causes our falldown. One time you asked a disciple to fetch some wood for a fire sacrifice. When he came back, you said, "I got that wood. You only helped me." In this way you taught us one of the most important principles to be always remembered—that we are not so great after all, that the spiritual master deserves all credit, and that our real position is simply to assist him. But out of your kindness, Śrīla Prabhupāda, in order that we can serve, you sometimes appeared to be dependent, and then you would respond by saying, "Thank you very much," as if to say, without us nothing could have been accomplished. But the real fact is that everything was started by you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and everything now and in the future will continue to be maintained by you, and as we push forward our preaching endeavors, your very presence is obvious to all those who participate.

Just as heat cannot be separated from fire and liquid cannot be separated from water, so in the same way we cannot be separated from you as long as we are connected with your preaching movement through printing and distributing your books, living in your temples, attending your *guru-pūjā,* and accepting instructions from your representatives. You also said that Kṛṣṇa consciousness has a very simple formula, which is: chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, take *prasādam,* and be happy. There is no difficulty; what is difficult is the determination to become Kṛṣṇa conscious. So we pray for your mercy to always have that determination within the core of our hearts and to always act in a capacity devoid of false prestige, and we pray that we may always remember your lotus feet and fully surrender to your divine instructions.

*Your humble servants at ISKCON Finland,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Dhīraśānta dāsa

## Fullerton

*Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Recently, high in the sky.  
returning from the dhāma,  
Between Bangkok and Hong Kong I sat  
chanting your holy nāma.

I thought of how shamefully little  
I've been able to do for you.  
Although I've somewhat tried,  
the potency has only trickled through.

So f prayed to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
to please let me serve you more.  
But that, I knew, required purity  
down into my heart's core.

Sitting there, I could painfully see  
that my heart was as black as coal.  
But you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I knew,  
could unearth my covered soul.

Then you kindly helped me understand.  
you revealed to me the key:  
"Appreciation and gratitude"  
for what you've already given me.

Why should I remain a beggar  
with both my hands held out?  
So I looked and found unlimited things  
to be thankful about.

After all, when love matures  
one isn't always on the lake.  
He feels indebted, he wants to give  
for the beloved's sake.

To list the gifts you’ve already given  
would take hundreds of pages.  
Suffice it to say, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
you're the most merciful of all sages.

On the plane that night, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
you were so kind to me.  
The glimpse you gave me helped me understand  
what my Godbrothers could already see.

I realized it was a privilege  
to be intimate with you.  
And those who are, are the ones you use  
to render service through.

So when you feel I qualify,  
when my attitude is pure,  
Please use me as your instrument  
to administer the cure.

*Your fallen servants of New Śāntipura Dhāma, Fullerton, California,*

## Gītā-nagari

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. Your appearance day marks another year that your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement has been maintained, fortified, and expanded throughout the world. \[The passage of time is simply a further testimony to your great potency as an *ācārya* in the Gauḍiya-Vaiṣṇava-Sampradāya. Sometimes we see that Kṛṣṇa puts obstacles before His devotees just so they can become more glorious by overcoming them. So you become more and more glorious each year that your movement continues and obstacles are overcome, by your grace.

We are eternally indebted to you for engaging us in Kṛṣṇa's service and for giving us authorized versions of the *śāstras* to study and learn about Kṛṣṇa, for as you explained, if you are going to love somebody, you have to know him in great depth. So in your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is.* in your *Śrīmad- Bhāgavatam* with elaborate purports, in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* you have handed us everything that we need to become *kṛṣṇa-bhaktas.* Now the burden is on ourselves to study it and put it into practice in our lives, as you did. Not that mundane scholarship will bring us to the goal of pure devotion. But transcendental sound vibration coming from the lips and the pen of a pure devotee is what you have actually given us.

You saw the heart of all your disciples and didn't judge them or engage them according to some material calculation. Through your *uttama-adhikārī* eyes, you saw us all as pure devotees of Kṛṣṇa, and you invited us to join with you in the dancing party of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. You wanted all unlucky persons to join and become fortunate, and your life was simply dedicated to that end.

We pray to you for guidance in how to maintain and expand your movement so that it reaches more and more of the unlucky living entities of the material world and does not get changed or diminished by the forces of *māyā.* At the end of your stay here in the material world, you wanted to establish *varṇāśrama* at Gītā-nāgarī. So we pray to you for guidance in how to implement your desire. We can study your books and take consultation with other devotees and hope that we can figure out what you would have done.

The foremost program at Gītā-nāgarī is to relieve your anxiety about cow-killing. I remember once when I was showing you my work horses, and you simply said, "Where are the bulls?" I glimpsed your anxiety that people won't stop cow-killing unless they see a practical alternative, a higher taste. On one side, produce pure foods, and on the other side, protect the cow so she produces abundant milk, and use the bulls to replace all machinery. The result is a more advanced society both materially and spiritually. The proportions of the crime of cow slaughter are very large, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but your deep compassion for those poor souls shows the importance of the struggle to save them. Even though we are sometimes up against great odds, and experience repeated setbacks, the thought of relieving your anxiety pushes us on.

(Written by Paramānanda dāsa)

## Guadalajara

Please accept our humble obeisances millions of times unto your blessed lotus feet.

O Jagad-guru, your kindness is as unfathomable as the ocean. You are the lord and master of all the living entities that sleep in the dream of material life, and you are reaching to them through your beautiful books, touching them to awake them to the joyful life of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Actually, in this dream *these jīvas* are burning themselves in the fire of material existence, anxiously searching for beauty, wealth, fame, and power, while they are all being reduced to ashes by the other burning fire, which is time. But when they receive the showers of your blessing, not only are these fires extinguished, but they also drink the nectar of love for Kṛṣṇa. You are traveling throughout the universe, giving *darśana* freely to all, and all their sufferings are alleviated because you are touching them with your pure compassion.

The mercy of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu and the whole disciplic succession is personified in you, and in this way you are invincible in the task of reclaiming conditioned souls. Everything about you is worship-able and perfect, because your sole aim is to serve Kṛṣṇa and His mission.

When we were committing suicide, flinging ourselves into the whirlpool of repeated birth and death, you gave us all the love of Kṛṣṇa. You reserved nothing. Your love rescued us forever, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and we want to be your servants. We firmly believe that there is not a more ecstatic position.

We feel your absence, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but we also feel your eternal presence in your books, in your instructions, in your followers, the present *ācāryas,* and in all that is ISKCON.

We are fools, and we are small, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and it is only through your causeless mercy as spiritual teacher and father, master and friend, well-wisher and savior, instructor and leader that we can be elevated to the loving devotional service of Kṛṣṇa.

Jaya Prabhupāda!

*Your spiritual children and grandchildren of New Nīlācala Mandira (Gaudalajara, Mexico).*

## Guyana

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance, we beg to offer our prostrated obeisances, a thousand, a million, nay, uncountable times at the dust of your lotus feet.

As it is clearly stated in the scriptures, it is not at all possible for one to understand the sublime nature of the transcendental activities of a *mahā-bhāgavata* like Your Divine Grace. But by your mercy alone, one who has dedicated himself to carrying out your order as his life and soul immediately becomes empowered to act as your bona fide representative and expand the mission of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu on your behalf. Indeed, this is your transcendental desire, that Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu's prediction would be fulfilled. You were the perfect instrument to fulfill this prediction of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and now that you are no longer physically present, you are kindly allowing us to act as your instruments for the propagation of the sublime teachings of this most munificent incarnation of Godhead.

Here in Guyana, we are especially blessed to be receiving genuine guidance from two of your most intimate disciples, who have been delegated by your causeless mercy to accept future generations of disciples on your behalf in this country. By their shining example of purity and unalloyed dedication to your mission, we are able to understand the import of the most important principle of guru-disciple relationship.

*yasya deve parā bhaktir*  
*yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ*  
*prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

**"Only unto those great souls who have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed."**

Obviously your sojourn during the short span of your stay within this material world ushered in a whole new realm of revolutionary encounters within this present degrading age of Kali by your divine presence. And it would be impractical for us to think of Kṛṣṇa without thinking about you. As it is said, "There is no point being 'Kṛṣṇa conscious' without being 'Prabhupāda conscious.' " Now that consciousness of you is given to us by your confidential representatives, namely Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami and Agrāṇī Mahārāja. As such we can only please you by giving full recognition to their great effort and determination to enlighten us.

Because you are completely perfect, each and every scheme you devised for the continuation of your ISKCON movement after your demise is also completely perfect. Therefore our only ambition is to humbly place ourselves at the lotus feet of your bona fide representatives.

(Written by Bhūtādi dāsa)

*Begging for a permanent place in your international family of ISKCON, your servants,*

## Hartford

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

This is a most sobering time, a time of complete concentration on you, Śrīla Prabhupāda; a time when we put all other thoughts out and simply keep our minds fixed on you. Actually, I should always be concentrating my mind on you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, twenty-four hours a day. This is the perfection I am striving for. When I think of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I become very serious and grave. I manifest the qualities of a devotee. This is proof that the process works if one simply applies it properly.

I am praying at your lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that I and all the devotees of ISKCON East Hartford, and all the devotees around the world, apply this process properly, by fixing our minds on you. All glories unto you, *Jagad-guru,* Śrīla Prabhupāda.

(Written by Pyārimohana dāsa)

## Hawaiian Islands

Homage to Śrīla Prabhupāda  
The time of death had come again  
So much desire remaining then.  
What form I had, I do not know  
But from that life I had to go.

Forgetful of sanātana dharma  
Rotating on the wheel of karma  
Sometimes perhaps a demigod  
Sometimes a worm beneath the sod.

No sooner than I'd meet my doom  
Again I'm back within the womb.  
Of miseries there is no dearth  
And soon again another birth.

Oh, when, oh, when will it all end  
And from this place will I descend?  
But if I could, where would I go?  
Because there's nothing else I know.

What is this strange new dress I wear?  
It seems so odd, it must be rare.  
A human form is what they say  
But like the rest, it will decay.

And who's this man with smiling face  
He must be from another place.  
He always speaks and writes of God  
They say his name is Prabhupāda.

Though I'm so dull and full of pride  
With all my nonsense justified,  
He speaks and cuts through my defense  
And shows his greatest opulence.

Such love I cannot comprehend  
But I know that I've found a friend.  
No longer will I have to roam  
For he has come to take me home.

Now Prabhupāda you're all I know  
Although my mind is very slow.  
This sojourn, soon I will complete  
If I stay at your lotus feet.

But you've not come just for my sake  
It's the whole world you've come to take.  
And soon they all will know it's true  
That Prabhupāda, you're jagad-guru.

(Written by Narahari Goswami)

## Hobart

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**"I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, who has opened my eyes, which were blinded by the darkness of ignorance, with the torchlight of knowledge."**

Our humble obeisances are offered at your lotus feet again and again.

Ananta Śeṣa with His thousand mouths has been glorifying Kṛṣṇa since time immemorial without repeating Himself. If we were as capable we could also glorify you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, in a similar way, for your transcendental qualities know no limits.

Unfortunately, our material attachments impede the ability to sufficiently praise the nature of your immense potency. Here you come to our rescue by presenting us with *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, the cream of all Vedic literature, which itself directs us to hear the narration of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* from the person *Bhāgavatam*.

In this dense, dark Kali-yuga it is only this *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that gives the light to the misdirected civilization, and you have come as the bearer of this *Bhāgavatam*.

By glorifying the story of the Supreme Personality of Godhead we are glorifying you, for the key to unlock the hidden treasure of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti*, our eternal love for God, is guided by your loving purports.

On this anniversary of your appearance day we beg for the ability to glorify you by being able to always and ever-increasingly distribute your books, distribute your books, distribute your books!

(Written by Brhaspati dāsa)

*Your servants at Hobart Yātrā,*

*mūkuṁ karoti vācālaṁ*  
*paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā taṁ ahaṁ vande*  
*śrī-guruṁ dīna-taraṇam*

He reasons ill who tells that Vaiṣṇavas die  
when thou art living still in sound.  
The Vaiṣṇavas die to live, and living try  
To spread the holy name around.  
—Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura

According to the distorted vision of the mundane materialistic autocrat, when the living entity's sojourn within this body ceases, so ceases any possibility of future pastimes or activities. For the foolish conditioned soul this may indeed be true; for the pure Vaiṣṇava devotee of the Lord, never. By dint of the pure devotee's transcendental position as Kṛṣṇa's representative, he is present eternally in his *vāṇī* manifestation.

Therefore, as you taught us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the degree to which your instructions are followed is what distinguishes a pure devotee from a neophyte.

*yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

To call oneself your disciple necessitates that one know what your instructions and desires are and work tirelessly to execute them without deviation. There is nothing vague or unanswered; everything is crystal clear, for everything is, as you said, contained within your books. Does this mean that any one of us should think we have some greater realization of your instructions beyond everyone else's and attempt to execute those instructions independently? No, this you have also answered perfectly clearly: "Your love for me will be shown by how well you cooperate together," and "ISKCON is my body, the BBT my heart." Therefore real worship of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is cooperating together to fulfill your desires. This is the real purport of *yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo / yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto pi.* Any so-called devotional service deviating from this principle is simply mental concoction and will surely carry one to hell.

So we are praying on this Vyāsa-pūjā day to eternally have the clear recollection of your transcendental pastimes and instructions. No one could have possibly accomplished what you did, and no one will be able to accomplish such feats in the future. It is all a part of Kṛṣṇa's divine plan. From your transcendental appearance in this material world until your physical departure in 1977, you demonstrated nectarean pastimes which will be relished by your disciples and granddisciples eternally. And your *līlā* does not cease here, but rather it is increasing daily through your ISKCON movement. We humbly beg to always be purely engaged in your service, lifetime after lifetime.

*Your servants of the China mission,*

## Indonesia

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

On this most auspicious day of your appearance in this world, please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Each year when we write your Vyāsa-pūjā homage, we realize how unqualified we are to try to describe your glories. Your unlimited qualities and pastimes have been described very nicely by your confidential disciples who had the chance to have your close association, to hear your direct instructions, and to serve you directly. So who are we, and what can we add to such choice words in your glorification composed by those who have shown by their actions that they have understood your desire to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world and are continuing your mission more and more?

Your appearance in this world is, along with the appearance of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and His confidential associates, the most significant event in this Kali-yuga. If not for your unlimited kindness, we and millions of others would still be rotting in *māyā,* with no hope of salvation. Your every thought, word, and action is the perfect example of the pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa. The brilliance of your transcendental activities as the *founder-ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness is illuminating the dense darkness of ignorance of this age, and will continue to do so for years to come.

Your books are the only hope for those drowning in nescience. The atheistic propaganda and mental speculation of this age are destroyed by the pristine clarity of your Bhaktivedanta purports to the authoritative Vedic literatures which were for so long locked away in esoteric Sanskrit and known to only a few. You have opened the treasure house of love of God stored up in the *Bhagavad-gītā, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* for without you, these literatures would never have been revealed.

You established temples all over the world so that people of all races and creeds could learn practically the science of God consciousness as described in your books. These temples are just like embassies of the supreme kingdom of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, in the nether world of birth and death. By pleasing Kṛṣṇa under your guidance in these temples, people can very easily obtain "permanent residence" in the spiritual world, rather than the miserable, temporary existence of ignorance in this world.

Although we are not qualified to glorify you, still we feel our connection with you as your servants and servants of the servants, and we pray that we may always remain so, even in the midst of the greatest dangers and calamities. You performed so many austerities to bring this Kṛṣṇa consciousness to us, so we must also perform so many austerities to bring your message to others.

We pray that we may always have the chance to serve you in some way birth after birth. We know you are always guiding us, both individually and as a society, as long as we stick firmly to your instructions.

Śrīla Prabhupāda *ki jaya!*

*Your unworthy servants,*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*

I am feeble and confused by material nature. You have entered my life so blissfully and rescued me from a hellish future. It is not possible to repay you, but still I pray that I can be of some service to you some day.

Kindly allow this wretched soul to dedicate his every breath to your service birth after birth. We are not afraid of anything as long as you are with us. We can easily defeat anyone by taking shelter of your lotus feet.

This movement is destined to revolutionize our misguided world. We thank you repeatedly for the thrill of being part of ISKCON.

We will not trade our devotional service for anything offered by *māyā.* This is your causeless mercy.

*Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. On the anniversary of your most auspicious appearance we are begging for this mercy, that we be found always engaged in activities that are pleasing to you. That opportunity is there because your mission is to reclaim the fallen souls of Kali-yuga, and there is no shortage of conditioned souls (including ourselves). You have given everything; the only item missing is our serious surrender to you. Please let this day (and every day) mark a greater dedication to your mission and to your glorification.

## Johannesburg

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

On this most auspicious occasion of your divine appearance within this material world, we can only continue to fix our minds on your activities, instructions, and fond remembrances of you. For a disciple, one's perfection (there is no better) is to completely absorb one's mind and intelligence in the guru's pastimes and instructions.

When Lord Caitanya asked Rāmānanda Rāya what is considered to be the most painful existence, Rāmananda Raya replied that separation from a pure devotee constitutes the most painful existence. In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (3.30.7), you have written in the purport, "In Kṛṣṇaloka there are also family, friends, society, father and mother, everything is there, but they are eternal."

Our relationship with you is an introduction to our eternal spiritual *rasa* with the Lord and His associates. For your disciples and granddisciples, the sweetest, most valuable possessions we have are the sublime memories of your *līlā* and instructions. Therefore let us humbly offer some anecdotes of your pastimes in 1975 while you were in South Africa.

Everyone was amazed by your presence in South Africa, because due to the system of apartheid it was very unusual to see white-skinned Europeans offering their *daṇḍavats* to an Indian *sādhu.*

Your first major program in South Africa was at Durban City Hall, and it was filled to capacity. An Indian businessman sitting in the front row boldly got up after your lecture and asked why it was important to become a vegetarian. As a stunned crowd of three thousand listened, you replied, "Even the monkeys are vegetarian . . . rather we should offer the food to Kṛṣṇa."

At Westville University in Durban you met with the chancellor, a Professor S. P. Olivier, and impressed him so much that he returned your visit and spent three hours with you. By your preaching he became so enlivened that he offered your ISKCON an academic position at the university.

In Johannesburg you went for your morning walks on Yeoville Golf Course and gave your divine association to three Indian tailors who came practically every day.

One day in Durban you sent us out to get *datoons,* toothbrushes made from eucalyptus twigs. We would climb trees like monkeys, breaking off branches, trying to find the perfect twig. It was not until later that we realized that Your Divine Grace had a toothache.

We beg to remain,

*Your servants of ISKCON Johannesburg,*

## Knoxville

Jaya Prabhupāda! Jaya Gauranga!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The spiritual master is to be honored as much as the Lord because he is the most confidential servant of the Lord.

Just as Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu is considered more merciful and magnanimous than any other *avatāra,* even than Lord Kṛṣṇa, because of His activities of delivering love of God to wretched and fallen souls, so we; your followers, consider you to be the most special *ācārya* in history, because of your wonderful service to the Lord. Indeed, even in the midst of piles of money, thousands of followers, beautiful young women, opulent worship, and comfortable living arrangements, you remained the servant of Kṛṣṇa,- "always humbler than an ordinary man." Thus, we became convinced of our great good fortune in getting your holy association. *Śrotriyaṁ brahma-niṣṭham.* You had heard nicely from authority and were fixed on the pure spiritual platform of complete, unwavering loving service to the Lord. No wonder that, in our devotion to you, we felt that no order of yours, however difficult to execute, should be neglected. Such was the force of your inspiration.

However, even as the purely spiritual religion of *bhāgavata-dharma,* personally established by the Lord's incarnation, becomes eroded and sullied by this nasty material world, so since your departure, we too have experienced many problems due to the powerful influence of all of our own polluted material tendencies that we still carry.

However, all is not lost. You have informed us that the spiritual master lives with the disciple who follows *the guru's* order. We are still chanting sixteen rounds per day, minimum, and are following the four regulative principles as we promised you when we were initiated. We are trying to understand Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the association of our Godbrothers and are preaching it to others. We are distributing more and more of your books each year. We are scouring your books, your tapes, and even your letters, to help us separate truth from fiction and reality from illusion.

Kindly be pleased on our foolish and insincere selves that the mercy of Kṛṣṇa which you give is not withdrawn, even despite our lack of qualification for it, because we are *nowhere* without it. We were already nowhere before meeting you, and now we've become so puffed up and arrogant over what great souls we've become in such a short time! We hope that if we go on singing *yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo, yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto 'pi* that we'll soon realize some of that deep meaning. Then we'll be easily able to cooperate nicely together to joyfully spread this mission for your transcendental satisfaction.

*Your servants at ISKCON Knoxville, Tennessee,*

## Lagos

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Your shower of mercy here in West Africa reminds us of a story from the *Mahābhārata* in which is related the history of Ekalavya, a young black youth from the hills, whose father, named Hiraṇyadhanus, was the king of I the *niśāda* race. As you had explained in your purports to the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* the *niśādas* are descendants of Bahūka. After King Vena was cursed to die on account of his atrocious activities, such as stopping the *brāhmaṇas* from performing fire *yajñas,* the sages began to churn his thighs, hoping to revive the powerful dynasty in which Vena was born. From his body was produced first a short black fellow, Ekalavya, who was the form of Vena's sinful activities; thus his domain became the hills and forests and his livelihood plundering, stealing, and hunting. The *niśāda* Ekalavya, although lowborn, still approached Droṇācārya in the forest and asked to be instructed in archery, submitting himself humbly before his would-be *guru.* As Droṇa would only accept *kṣatriyas* from the royal dynasties as disciples, however, it was left to Ekalavya alone to perfect himself. And this he did by first of all molding a form of his *guru* out of mud and duly worshiping it, as well as performing daily drills with his bow and arrow.

It was when the Pāṇḍavas were on a picnic in the woods that they saw Ekalavya dressed in leopard skins and being harassed by their own dog. Ekalavya, much to the astonishment of the princes, sealed the furious canine's mouth shut with a slew of arrows from his bow. Although Arjuna had been promised by Droṇa to become the best archer in the world, it was then clear to them that Ekalavya was unexcelled, despite being unattended by Droṇa.

It also may be astonishing to some how this race of Kīratas has produced so many enthusiastic followers of Lord Caitanya's Saṅkīrtana movement. But when one sees their sincere dedication to the lotus feet of their Guru Mahārāja, it is easily understood as the great mercy of the *parama-guru,* Śrīla Prabhupāda.

It was Śrīla Prabhupāda who had first gone personally to the Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa Temple in Nairobi to open up the doors to the African people, who crowded the hall to hear the devotees perform *saṅkīrtana.* Now, by the mercy of their spiritual master and Kṛṣṇa, the Africans have organized large *saṅkīrtana* parties to inundate the west coast. Crowds of people throng to see the dancing devotees playing the *mṛdaṅga* like nobody's business and defeating the sounds of a fast-moving steam locomotive with their clashing *karatālas.* With voices booming and pamphlets and books flying out, it is clearly turning the Gold Coast into Gaura-Hari Coast. Everywhere you turn, there are devotees and people calling out the holy names—custom's men, policemen, bankers, doctors, lawyers, judges, newspaper boys, carpenters, electricians, farmers, plumbers, etc.

Even though you are not with us personally, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we can sense the smile on your face, which is all we ever wanted in life.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for bringing light to the "dark continent."

*Your surrendered servants at ISKCON Lagos,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Bhaktitīrtha Swami / Upananda dāsa

## Laguṇa Beach

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

O friend of the fallen, although dwelling in Vṛndāvana, you descended for the welfare of fallen souls like ourselves. On this most blessed day we thank you for giving us the wonderful opportunity to serve your great mission of fulfilling the desires of your Guru Mahārāja and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. With your blessings and the expert guidance of your intelligent G.B.C. man Rāmeśvara Swami, we were able to launch a massive attack on the student community, who are generally very expert in avoiding the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. However, our "Festival of India" program, headed by your humble disciple Madhuhā prabhu, was so attractive that thousand of students became immersed in an inundation of love of Godhead as we distributed over nine thousand pieces of your transcendental literatures, along with continuous *kīrtana* and *prasādam,* in just three days. Śrīla Prabhupāda, your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is not stereotyped or stagnant. It is spreading all over the world, in spite of all obstacles, because we are being guided by ideal *ācāryas.* Thus we feel very fortunate to be allowed to assist you in spreading the *saṅkīrtana* movement. Praying at the lotus feet of the Pañca-tattva and always hoping for their mercy and kindness, we offer ourselves to Their most dear servitor.

*Feeling deeply indebted, we rededicate ourselves,*

## Lake Huntington

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

A nitya-siddha, but more than that;  
Śaktyāveśa-avatāra come to "mleccha-dhāma, "  
Appearing upon Lord Kṛṣṇa's request,  
And struggling through the pains of labor  
To give us real birth.

Conditioned since time immemorial,  
Fallen souls, struggling against māyā’s onslaught,  
Stumble forward and fall again and again,  
Only to be lifted up repeatedly  
By their loving father.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, how can we thank you?  
It's not possible, not for millions of kalpas,  
Save to glorify you eternally  
By following your instructions strictly  
And pushing on your mission

(Written by Muralīvādaka dāsa)

## London

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

While engaging in the service of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Londonīśvara, we are constantly reminded of your special pastime of establishing the *saṅkīrtana* movement in this country. Boldly and daringly, with full faith in the potency of the holy name, you trained your *kīrtana* party in Canada and sent them like a commando unit to chant in the streets of London. This was the city which had been the capital of the proud and arrogant British Empire, the same empire that had considered India to be the "jewel in the crown," the same city which you described simply as "hell."

When you came to preach here, Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa came with you. You enthroned Them and declared that Kṛṣṇa was the real controller of London. Now you have mercifully given the task of serving Their Lordships to your disciples and granddisciples. For many years Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Londonīśvara have been overseeing the preaching in London. Like precious, hidden jewels. They have stayed in boxlike quarters, first in Bury Place, and now in Soho Street. From Their little temple, surrounded by the chaos and strife of this noisy, filthy, stinking, and decadent city. They have drawn in many conditioned souls and changed their lives from ones of boredom and emptiness into ones full of blissful devotional service. This is the sixteenth year that They have been here, and just this year Lord Jagannātha, Lord Baladeva, and Lady Subhadrā have returned to take up residence with Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. A period of uncertainty and insecurity in the history of the British Yātrā is drawing to a close, and we are looking to a time in the very near future when Their Lordships will no longer appear to be hidden but will come into the full view of the public in a grand palace that will display Them as the real monarchs of Britain. Thus Britain will be the "jewel in the crown" of the transcendental empire of Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Londonīśvara are yours, and you are Theirs. Their mood and your mood are the same—deep compassion for the fallen conditioned souls, exhibited by your every action. By your mercy and Theirs there is a spiritual embassy in this city and in so many other hellish cities around the world. Now the path back home, back to Godhead, has been clearly revealed for all the lost and wayward souls who loiter in the streets.

Your appearance in this world has yet to be fully known and understood by the general mass of people, and so many demoniac regimes are entrenched all over the world with the sole purpose of destroying all that is good in human society. But we are comforted and enlivened to know that by your divine grace the real rulers of the world have again manifested Themselves and are gradually bringing things back into proper order. It is your special mercy upon us that we have been engaged thus in the service of Their Lordships. For this we are eternally grateful.

*Your servants,*

## Long Island

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

You are the perfect ācārya and spiritual master of the whole world. As such, you are worshipable by all, even the demigods. You are unsurpassed in your service to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. You are the greatest Vaiṣṇava preacher and staunch soldier in the saṅkīrtana army of Lord Caitanya. You were undaunted by any obstacle in your preaching work. You demonstrated by practical example that "Impossible is a word in a fool's dictionary." Your determination to preach and transcend all material impediments can be compared to the mood of Grandfather Bhīṣma, who remained in perfect meditation on Lord Kṛṣṇa even while pierced by so many arrows. Similarly, even in advanced age and failing health, you taught us to preach until one's final breath.

Our greatest fortune is in meeting you. Attachment to your lotus feet is the means of our perfection. We beg to remain your servants birth after birth.

*Your humble followers,*

## Los Angeles

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet, the spot where real life begins and material suffering ends. Someone may ask. What is so special about your transcendental feet? And we will say that \[when one surrenders to them, they get "the mercy." What is "the mercy"?

*Mercy—*"kindness, as to a wrongdoer or enemy, the power to forgive or be kind, a lucky thing, blessing."

*Mercy,* a simple world which perfectly describes what you have given us and what you are always giving to all conditioned souls of this material world.

*vāñchā-kalpa-tarubhyaś ca kṛpa-sindhubhya eva ca*  
*patitānāṁ pāvanebhyo vaiṣṇavebhyo namo namaḥ*

In our material way we tend to reject the simple for the complicated things of the material world. But we are simple by nature, just servants of God, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. The mercy you have given us is what is perfectly natural to a servant of God—service.

For example, isn't it more difficult being in charge of some project with all the organization, management, etc., than being a worker on that job? Aren't there more headaches being the rich man and owning the mansion and the Rolls Royce car than to be the servant of such a man? Similarly, you have taught us that to simply surrender to our nature as servants of Kṛṣṇa is actually the easiest thing we could ever do. As you would often say, "What is the difficulty?"

Therefore we always pray that we will at every moment accept your mercy and return to our original consciousness, Kṛṣṇa consciousness. And in this way everything will become "simply wonderful."

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for "the mercy."

(Written by Vatsala dāsa)

*Your eternal servants,*

## Malaysia

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. Eight long years have passed since you parted. During this time there is one thing which I miss more than any other—the most uplifting news of "Prabhupāda is coming." As soon as the message came by any means, immediately every devotee moved into top gear—cleaning, gardening, cooking, getting the best of everything; no shirking, no laziness, no time to think of one's problems, hardly time to eat or sleep. Everyone woke up, to serve the most confidential servitor of the Lord. Sometimes we would get lax when you were away, but as soon as news of your coming was received, then we would again remember in full your instructions. You inspired us to give up everything in service of the Lord, doing everything just correct—*capātī* perfectly round, fully puffed up, not burnt, but small black spots evenly distributed; not a spot of dust should be left anywhere; sit properly. In this way you guided us, awakening us with every word and deed.

Now in your physical absence, due to the influence of Kali, it is only too often that laziness takes control, thinking no one can see—who cares anyway? The very thing you fought against—impersonalism—again edges in. I ask myself how I can keep this mood of "Prabhupāda's coming" fresh in my heart at every moment. Yes, this must be our meditation, that you have not left, but at every moment you are coming. When you came to the temple then everything would have to be just right. So by strictly sticking to the principles you have given, surely you will never leave us but will be right here with us always.

Let us always keep you at the center by worshiping the instructions which you have given us—to distribute books in the way you taught, to chant and hear, to take only *prasādam*, perform gorgeous deity worship, keep everything clean! The list is unlimited. If we knew Prabhupāda were coming in one day, one hour, one minute, would we be doing what we are doing now, or thinking now? If not, then what's wrong?

So, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please be merciful unto us and help us always bear in mind that "Prabhupāda is coming" every day, every moment, and you will come to one whose heart is purely fixed in the dust of your lotus feet.

You live forever, and your sincere follower lives with you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda *ki jaya!*

(Written by Janānanda dāsa)

*Your humble servants of ISKCON Malaysia,*

## Mauritius

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to your transcendental appearance day, which gives us every year an increased impetus to reflect on what immense treasures you gave us and the world.

Looking back to the humble beginnings of-this faraway *yātrā,* we can only notice with great amazement how it has grown into probably one of the most favorable preaching places in the world. For this to happen, there is no other explanation but your mercy.

In the beginning you came here and stirred up the sleeping souls of this island with your sharp, uncompromising message of "Surrender to Kṛṣṇa, your eternal, but now forgotten, father." By hearing the lecture tapes of this unforgettable visit, which have taken more than nine years to reach here (that's how far away we are, in the middle of the ocean), we have relived your visit. The original words of your lectures are now being recalled by this fallen servant of yours, as if they had been spoken just yesterday. And just to prove how your message had actually touched those few souls who were present at those lectures almost ten years ago, we recently met one elderly gentleman who practically quoted word for word the most outstanding points of your speeches!

How could an ordinary person remember what you spoke ten years ago and repeat it almost exactly, if that message hadn't gone straight into the core of his heart? That is your potency, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Your existence on this planet was extraordinary. To read about it in the *Līlāmṛta* is the most nectarean activity. You stood out from the ordinary group of *svāmīs, sādhus, ācāryas,* etc., as the full moon stands out among the stars.

To remember and to meditate on these things is our daily duty, but specifically on the occasion of writing your Vyāsa-pūjā offering, this meditation becomes very intense and fills the heart with great transcendental joy.

At the same time, we break our dull heads against the rocks, lamenting for our small appreciation of your physical presence. But as a pure devotee—the ocean of mercy and compassion that you are—you have given us another chance to make up what we missed by leaving behind your books, tapes, and instructions. In this way we actually remain close to you, in the shelter of your lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we feel very fortunate to have been accepted by Your Divine Grace as disciples. Please let us keep on serving you, life after life. We have no other shelter but you.

*Your insignificant servants.*

## Māyāpur

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

It is said in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* that the pastimes of Śrī Caitanya are still going on in Māyāpur today, but they become manifest only to those fortunate persons with spiritual vision. We do not have that vision. We have not been able to see Lord Caitanya's eternal pastimes here in Māyāpur. But, although unqualified, we have been so very fortunate to see your pastimes in Māyāpur. And we feel you are also eternally present here, along with Lord Caitanya.

Anyone who saw you in Māyāpur can now see your presence in everything here. With walking stick in hand, your saffron silk cloth shining brightly with the early morning sun, your head held high, you walked through the fields of Māyāpur, observing everything with your meticulous glance. Wherever you noticed that something was wrong, you would immediately point it out and incessantly advise your dedicated disciples how to develop Śrī Māyāpur Dhāma. You made everyone realize that Māyāpur is the eternal abode of Lord Caitanya Mahā-prabhu, nondifferent from Vṛndāvana. It is not only a transcendental place but the highest abode of the spiritual sky, and by the inconceivable potency of the Lord that abode has become manifest on this earth as Śrī Māyāpur Dhāma.

And since Lord Caitanya is eternally present in Śrī Māyāpur, then as His eternal associate you must also be present here eternally.

"Then why can't we see you?" we feel like crying out in acute despair. This is not a matter for mundane sentiment. Your van; form reminds us it is only our unlimited defects that make us unable to see you. "As long as you are attached to matter, you won't be able to see the spirit." You make us painfully aware of our insincerity. How dedicated have we actually become to your lotus feet? Where is that intense yearning to be with you? Where is that anguish of separation that would make the pain of death appear like the most soothing relief?

What should we do, Śrīla Prabhupāda? Everything was so easy when you were personally present here. Spurred by the desire to please you, to hear some words of appreciation from you, or even to see your eyes light up with joy, we could so easily become obsessed with our service. But now we lack that direct inspiration.

We know it is a test, and we must do well in this test, and not fail. And we know the key to pass the test is to develop intense attachment to your lotus feet. We may not see you, but you are seeing us. We may be able to fool the whole world, and even fool ourselves, but we will never be able to fool you, because you are sitting in the deepest core of our hearts. You see everything. And all you want to see is our honest dedication towards your ISKCON.

How wonderfully you have bestowed your mercy upon us. You have allowed us to serve you in Māyāpur, the heart of ISKCON. So, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most auspicious day, please bless us that we can dedicate ourselves completely to serve your lotus feet. Like the heart, we can spread our good will and vitality throughout the entire body of ISKCON. Then, we know, you will remove the covering of *māyā* and become manifest once again in front of our eyes.

## Medellin

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.*

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Kṛṣṇa explains in the *Bhagavad-gītā,* "Only one who is fully situated in transcendental knowledge can overcome the influence of *māyā."* Without the attainment of transcendental knowledge, any sacrifice people perform remains on a platform which is contaminated by the three modes of material nature. Real knowledge is Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the highest stage of transcendental awareness. As you said, "Without the elevation of knowledge, sacrifices are simply material activities. When, however, they are elevated to the level of transcendental knowledge, all such activities enter onto the spiritual platform."

The criterion of what enters and what doesn't enter onto the spiritual platform is explained by Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī:

*anyābhilāṣitā-śūnyaṁ*  
*jñāna-karmādy-anāvṛtam*  
*ānukūlyena kṛṣṇānu-*  
*śīlanam bhaktir uttamā*

Devotional service should be free of selfish desires and mental speculation. Another important ingredient involves the rendering of devotional service in a way that is favorable to the Lord. Without satisfying you, O *jagad-guru* Śrīla Prabhupāda, there is no other way of pleasing Kṛṣṇa. And satisfying you means to stick to the decisions of the Governing Body Commission (G.B.C.). With this attitude in mind, *gata-saṅgasya,* the state of liberation from the modes of nature is automatically attained.

Here in Colombia, the preaching had to suffer a lot of difficulties—from politics to bomb attacks. But in spite of all this, we are receiving full protection from you. We are fighting for you till the last breath. This we consider the perfection of our lives. Please forgive all our offenses we have committed, and allow us to push forward, on your behalf, the mission of Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

*yāre dekha tāre kaha kṛṣṇa-upadeśa*  
*āmara ajñaya guru tanhara ei deśa*

*Your servants in Medellin,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Mahā Rāma dāsa

## Mexico City

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our prostrated obeisances in the holy dust of your holy lotus feet.

You are the supreme servitor of Kṛṣṇa, whom we accept as captain of the ship to cross over this dangerously fearful and formidable ocean of the age of Kali, which deteriorates all the good qualities of the human beings.

With your transcendental words you bring light into the darkness, where we are situated in ignorance and attachment. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, clear up the clouds of our misunderstanding and bewilderment.

Out of intense love and compassion for fallen souls, you have appeared within our dark lives. With beautiful, swanlike radiance you are illuminating all corners and crevices of this nether world with the light of knowledge of service to the Supreme Lord, Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have appeared just to reclaim the conditioned, fallen souls; therefore we can say, as Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura sang, "We assure you that you will not find more greatly fallen souls than us; therefore, our claim is first."

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray to you that we never give up devotional service at your divine lotus feet.

*Your most fallen servants of Mexico City,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Rasasun-devī dāsī

## Mexico City, Centro Cultural Govinda

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In one of your purports in the *Śrīmad- Bhāgavatam* you have said:

"Everything, everywhere, is performed in terms of three principles, *jñāna-bala-kriyā—*knowledge, strength and activity. In every field, if there is not full knowledge, full strength and full activity, an endeavor is never successful. Therefore, if one wants success in everything, one must be backed by these three principles." *(Bhāg.* 10.2.26, purport)

Fortunately, Your Divine Grace has made such items available to us so that we can go on successfully in our preaching mission.

We have received knowledge, or *jñāna,* in the form of your beautiful books, and in this regard you have said-

"If one very carefully reads *Bhagavad-gītā, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Teachings of Lord Caitanya* and *The Nectar of Devotion,* that will give him sufficient knowledge to understand the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. One need not take the trouble of reading other books." (NOD p. 65)

"These four works are sufficient for preaching purposes. They are adequate for the understanding of philosophy and the spreading of missionary activities all over the world." (Cc. *Madhya* 22.118)

By just analyzing and emulating the strength, or *bala,* with which you developed your movement, we can also hope to have enough strength to go on preaching and propagating Kṛṣṇa consciousness during our lives and develop successfully your Govinda's Cultural Center.

Your movement is an ocean of activities, or *kriyā,* in the service of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, in which every kind of talent can be properly used to make spiritual advancement. Artists, writers, politicians, professionals and many other types of people are coming forward here in Mexico and requesting to be engaged in the activities of the Center. And they are finding new life in it.

We pray for your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Give us knowledge to develop the strength to be able to act in such a way that we can be of some assistance to you, and let us always remember that the Center of our lives should be Govinda.

*Your servants at Centro Cultural Govinda, Mexico City.*

## Melbourne

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*brahmāṇḍa brahmite kona bhagyavān jīva*  
*guru kṛṣṇa prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bhīja*

We were all trudging endlessly across the desert of material life, searching out the waterdrops of happiness that never really quenched our thirst. Occasionally we would run—there was water! But no, just another mirage, and again we would perish on the burning sands. And for the meagre happiness that we did find (that came of its own accord), there was a price: another prison-house womb.

Just like poor, maddened creatures in the desert, we wandered all over this creation, from germ body to demigod body, chewing the chewed; but alas, still we were not happy. Condemned to the world of death, what was our future? Hopeless struggle for nil—the "survival of the fittest" where no one survived.

But you came to this savage world by your sweet mercy to reclaim all of us lost children, who were wandering far, far away from home, forgetting our real father, tasting the gutter water as nectar and being kicked in the head for it.

You came as a divine messenger—"Don't you know that Kṛṣṇa is your eternal father and that you don't belong here in this world? You are not Māyā's servants. You are Kṛṣṇa-dāsa. Come with me and enjoy what is rightfully yours."

By the grace of Kṛṣṇa we fallen souls have come in contact with your merciful lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Such great mercy is so very, very rare. We certainly did not deserve this!

Now, by your grace, we have found relief from this desert world, in the chanting of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Now our duty is to fulfill your real desire as your ever-committed servants—to save all other souls who are still lost and confused, and to bring them back, too.

Please give us the strength to distribute your mercy guilelessly and without envy.

(Written by Kūrma dāsa)

*Your servants,*

## Miami Beach

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

We are lost for the proper words to glorify such a magnanimous, transcendental direct associate of Bhagavān Śrī Kṛṣṇa. This is definitely the most auspicious day—the appearance day of the topmost swanlike *paramahaṁsa jagad-guru,* Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given us the rare opportunity of becoming free from the clutches of *māyā,* from perpetually suffering and repeatedly dying. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for having brought us all together under the shelter of ISKCON and teaching us the process of *sādhana-bhakti.* You are our real father and eternal guide.

By your mercy we have been given the opportunity to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness from Govinda's Vaikuṇṭha Building. We are sure you would like the GVB. It is big, beautiful, and in a great location (world-famous Miami Beach), directly on the ocean. You could take your morning walks on the beach, and while walking you would defeat the arguments of the atheistic scientists. The weather is like India's; we're sure you would be pleased. The GVB is attractive to the materialists; as they stroll the boardwalk they stop to view your GVB, take pictures, and wonder in amazement, "What is that singing and dancing?"

Hṛdayānanda Goswami is often here, working on translating as the humble servant of Your Divine Grace. More and more books are coming from Govinda's Vaikuṇṭha Building BBT staff, glorifying Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu and all His confidential associates like you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Please give us the vision and spiritual strength to enthusiastically push on the wonderful gift you have mercifully bestowed upon us: *kṛṣṇa-bhakti,* devotion and love for the Supreme Personality of Godhead. We are not poetic, nor do we have a good brain for glorifying you, nor is it in our power to convince anyone about the goal of life. But you, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, can make all our endeavors successful by your mercy.

We repeatedly beg your grace and inspiration in our meager attempt to glorify a great Vaiṣṇava. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

## Montreal

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
We were born in the darkest ignorance,  
You enlivened our hearts sad.  
Today we chant and dance.

To save us from calamity  
You had us perform austerity:  
To māyā renounce  
And on nectar bounce.

You flamed our appetite  
For a life pure  
In a house that would endure  
Despite Time's fight.

You made us forget  
Working like an ass,  
An old bodily concept  
Again that couldn't last.

New burden, new weight  
On our delicate shoulders,  
Ecstatic freight  
Your transcendental orders.

Soon learning  
To live with you always  
As if never leaving  
Your Divine Grace.

Sunsetless empire,  
The books everywhere.  
Kali has no effect;  
Your Divine Grace never left.

Healthy jubilation  
For the devotees ablaze,  
Desperate in devotion  
Your lotus feet to gaze.

Careless of the odds,  
Today bold preachers  
At your lotus feet nod  
And weep their way to surrender.

Beggars they stand,  
Eager to decorate their necks with your lotus commands,  
Blessed with peace and satisfaction,  
They worship you as their only possession.

You are their life and soul,  
Your satisfaction their only goal.  
Unafraid of birth and death,  
They want to repay their eternal debt.

*Śrīla Prabhupāda ki jaya!*

## Morgantown

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our worthless obeisances. All glories to your divine lotus feet, which have penetrated this deep, dark dungeon of material existence.

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**"I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, who has opened my eyes, which were blinded by the darkness of ignorance, with the torchlight of knowledge."**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, in the *Mahābhārata* there is a description of the extraordinary *saubha* constructed for the Pandavas by Māyā Danava. In the account it describes how if one entered the *saubha* with envy in the heart, it had the astonishing subtle ability to bewitch and illusion him. In this way Duryodhana was confounded when he mistook the wall to be a doorway, knocking his head and causing his nose to bleed, which caused Duryodhana great anxiety and torment.

So in the same way this superlative *saubha* called *mahāt-tattva* has the intrinsic ability to bewilder whoever enters if he has envy toward its owner, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. The interloper becomes illusioned, mistaking matter for spirit and bringing upon himself the pain caused by misidentifying with the threefold miseries of material existence.

Dear spiritual master, in our preaching attempts, by your causeless mercy, we have to some degree become appreciative of the extensive nature of envy, which covers, like a cataract, the materially diseased living entity's vision. Nationalism, family attachment, humanitarianism, sectarian religious dogmatism, obstinance to authority, impersonalism, voidism, and attachment to temporary sense pleasures are but a few of the many manifestations of envy. However, Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your divine compassion you are delivering the most profound message, leaving even the most envious thunderstruck and awed as you remove the lunacy perpetrated by material existence, like an expert surgeon excising a cataract, and establish the core of absolute existence: unmotivated, uninterrupted service to the Supreme Person, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. As you have explained in *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is,* Śrīla Prabhupāda:

*tad viddhi praṇipātena paripraśnena sevayā*  
*upadeksyanti te jñānaṁ jñāninas tattva-darśinaḥ*

**"Just try to learn the truth by approaching a spiritual master. Inquire from him submissively and render service unto him. The self-realized soul can impart knowledge unto you because he has seen the truth."**

Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, in our feeble way we humbly beg that you allow us to serve your divine lotus feet, enabling our hearts to become submissive to all your instructions, cleansing them of envy, so our blindness will be cured.

By your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, may we become better instruments in assisting you and your divine mission of retrieving all the conditioned souls from their precarious fate of birth, old age, disease, and death and reuniting them in their original, supreme relationship with Kṛṣṇa.

*Your fallen servants at the Morgantown Yātrā,*

Nṛsiṁha Caitanya dāsa / Tapana Ācārya dāsa / Anala-devī dāsī / Akrūra dāsa / Pawan / Anukūṭa / Kṛṣṇa-śakti / Bhakta-mohī / Ācārya dāsa

## Murwillumbah

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, O savior of the world,  
Sometimes I wonder how you did so much.  
You came to the West without money, men, or help,  
But your sweetness changed the hearts of all you touched.

Materially you had nothing save the clothes that you wore  
And a suitcase full of transcendental knowledge,  
But in your heart you knew that the time had surely come  
When somehow we would hear your sacred message.

"Yes, we have our temples . . . and farms and restaurants too!"  
You told a man you met in mid-Manhattan.  
Only you could see how big it all would be  
As you took up Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta's baton.

"Westerners will never chant," they told you in the Dhāma,  
But still you came to save us from our māyā.  
Our senses running rampant, we had turned against the Lord  
But in our hearts were starved for something higher.

Māyāvādī nonsense had made us lazy men  
Just waiting for the "white light" to descend.  
"Real gosvāmīs preach,"you said. "Go out on saṅkīrtana.  
Lord Kṛṣṇa is your real eternal friend."

You gave us love and hope, and you saved us from despair.  
You showed us how to nicely serve the Lord.  
You gave us books to read and to place in people's homes,  
With love for you and Kṛṣṇa our reward.

So although we find it hard in our long progressive march,  
You've given us all we need to carry on.  
Struggle we may do, but we vow to you this day  
To always try to do what must be done.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, O servant of the Lord,  
Please help us be successful in our quest.  
You came to give us life, so we give our lives to you,  
And why not, Śrīla Prabhupāda—you're the best!
