## Nepal

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.*

Please accept our prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet. You are always completely engaged in Lord Kṛṣṇa's service in a way beyond our gaze of inspection. You are very dear to your spiritual master due to your complete surrender to his desire. Your divine appearance and activities in this world altered the course of history from a path of illusion and bewilderment to one of Kṛṣṇa conscious peace and prosperity.

Due to your unlimited mercy, by the desire of Lord Caitanya, we are now preaching in this country—Nepal. You had personally spoken the order to your disciples to "make a stronghold of Kṛṣṇa consciousness" in Nepal. We will try, but pray for the result to come from you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Although we fit well in the "Hindu Kingdom," many persons still remain to accept a taste of your chanting, dancing, and feasting program. Although there are many problems to solve, your genius will solve them. There are so many obstacles to cross over or circumvent—your endurance will prevail. You are, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the source of, and sole reason for, any success we may meet with here in convincing the people to enter and re-enter Kṛṣṇa consciousness and devotional service. By your kindness they may learn to lead a pure life—free from the mad desire for material sense gratificatory progress—and follow the four regulative principles which you are offering.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, a devotee so pure and so faithful as yourself is most rare in this material universe. Your love, your purity, is whiter than the ice- and snow-covered peaks of the Annapūrṇa range. More serene than the scenery on the path to Muktinath is the course which you have chalked out for ISKCON and humanity. The knowledge you have given so willingly must impress even Lord Gaṇeśa. Your ecstatic mood, which enabled us to reject so much material sense indulgence, awakens our inner love for God (Sītā-Rāma), which sometimes escapes when we are fortunate enough to enter Janakpur Dham. You are so mystical, so beautiful, that our eyes are satisfied fully, even when we are unable to visit Varāha-kṣetra. Upon visiting Lord Buddha's birthplace in Lumbini, the prevailing peace simply reminds us of Your Divine Grace, who are never shaken, even there being many reasons for disturbance. Whenever we take the *darśana* of Lord Pashupatinath, we are forced to meditate upon your chivalrous heroism in the matter of preaching *sad-dharma* throughout the world.

Due to time, place, and circumstance, we have introduced *yoga*-therapy courses. Whenever there is attendance, and tourists or locals visit your center, we are reminded of your position as the "highest*-yogī."* Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray that we may accept a glimpse of your mercy and that you may someday be satisfied with our meager attempts of serving you—ISKCON.

On this most holy day of your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration, please let us hear your beautiful, sweet bhajan, which, having the potency to melt stone hearts, shall allow us to serve you with actual sincerity. "Your fame is spread all over the three worlds." We beg to appreciate this by your unlimited, causeless mercy. Prostrated obeisances and "Hare Kṛṣṇa" from your disciples and granddisciples at ISKCON Nepal.

## New Gauḍadeśa, Australia

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.*

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your very soft, pink lotus feet.

I am feeling very happy to be able to write to you at this time and would like to thank you for maintaining me in my Kṛṣṇa consciousness over the past year. I have recently been to Śrī Māyāpur and Śrī Vṛndāvana *dhāmas* and would like to thank you once again for your genius and transcendental foresight in establishing these wonderful places of pilgrimage that are so easily able to cleanse the heart of such a fool as myself.

On this holy day of your appearance in this world I would like to repledge my full support for your ISKCON activities and your ISKCON G.B.C., which are nondifferent from your very self. I beg to remain your servant for my next one thousand births and eternally after that also.

*Your servants,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## New Govardhana, Puerto Rico

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Our dear lord and master Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

On this very auspicious occasion commemorating your appearance, all your disciples and granddisciples all 'over the world are gathering to offer prayers at your divine lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, knowing how unqualified I am to come forward and make a presentation to you in the presence of our senior Godbrothers, who have been directly empowered by you to write books, preach, and manage this great movement, with all humility let me pray at your lotus feet, begging at the feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa that we devotees at this Puerto Rico Yātrā may become fully surrendered to your desire of preaching the gospel of Caitanyadeva to every conditioned soul. May we cooperate with each other in the process of carrying out your instructions and faithfully follow the footsteps of the *ācāryas* by putting aside our material egotistic desires for fame, adoration, and distinction.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, very often you said that the spiritual master, although he may not be physically present before his disciples, still gives his disciples his association through his instruction. Gurudeva, you have gone back to Kṛṣṇa's abode, and it seems we're now bereft of your wonderful holy presence, which confers upon the conditioned living entity knowledge and remembrance of Kṛṣṇa. But being very merciful, you have given to us your books, which the devotees may study and distribute and thereby always be situated at your lotus feet, protected from *māyā's* illusion, under the guidance of your perfect instructions.

*brahmāṇḍa brahmite kona bhagyavān jīva*  
*guru kṛṣṇa prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bhīja*

Gurudeva, how will we ever understand our great fortune of having come in contact with you? Because of our rebelliousness to Kṛṣṇa, we tiny living entities have been wandering all over the universe, and under the spell of *māyā's* illusion we have been forced to accept so many different bodies and thereby suffer birth, disease, old age, and death again and again. But Kṛṣṇa sent you to pull us out of this terrible ocean of material suffering and take us to the eternal Vaikuṇṭha world, to Kṛṣṇa's Goloka Vṛndāvana. Such is our great fortune. We were crawling like miserable worms in the stool of temporary enjoyment, and you came to pull us out of it and are giving us the chance to dance with Lord Caitanya. How can we repay you? Is there any way we can?

Therefore our only desire should be to cooperate with each other and, under the direction of those you have placed to lead us, endeavor to satisfy your desire of distributing Kṛṣṇa's mercy to the suffering living entities of this Kali-yuga. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, although we are worthless, accept our meager service. This is our prayer at your lotus feet.

(Written by Viprahita dāsa)

*The devotees at ISKCON's New Govardhana Mandira, Puerto Rico Yātrā,*

## New Delhi

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*On this most auspicious day we offer you our most humble obeisances.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

As the sky around us loomed dark and gray,  
War asunder, psychedelic dismay,  
You chose to attend us, most / alien of all.  
A cry in the night, you heard our call.

The ship of life, the ocean of fear,  
The cruel hand, the trident near,  
O lost tiny soul, a hopeless fare.  
You threw us the rope. O devil beware!

From you the sweet Vedas like morning dew  
Revived our youth a sky so blue.  
Only one on this earth, sent by the Lord,  
You nurtured us, your eternal wards.

O lord of the Vaiṣṇavas, how can we say  
It seems so long—but just yesterday?  
Your glistening eyes, your gleaming smile,  
A million words could not repay.

Kali's blind men do not give a yen, cannot see,  
Topmost of all men, you are Lord Caitanya's . . .  
Bright and bold to deliver them all.  
His age of gold, ten thousand years.  
Your books surely read. All nations yield,  
Bhaktivinoda has said.  
O sages of siddhānta, our hearts are glad.  
We thank you—you gave us Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Devotees of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Pārthasārathi Mandira,*  
*ISKCON Delhi,*

Kṛpānidhi dāsa / Mohinī-mūrti-devī dāsī / Dāmodara Paṇḍita dāsa / Rasa-mañjarī-devī dāsī / Vijaya Kṛṣṇa dāsa / Govinda-rāja dāsa / Rādhā-ramaṇa dāsa / Yaśodā-priya / Prahlāda / Śruti / Rocana dāsa / Rudrāṇī / Rādhārāṇī / Acyuta Kṛṣṇa dāsa / Vrajapriyā-devī dāsī / Vrajalatā-devī dāsī / Abhayāśraya & Bhakti-rasa / Jayānanda / Pativratā-devī dāsī / Hanumān / Hari-bhakti-vilāsa / Tulāsī dāsa / Yadukuleśvara dāsa / Ādideva dāsa / Kṛṣṇa Caitanya / Puruṣottama dāsa / Rādhā-Dāmodara / Hare Kṛṣṇa dāsa / Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa / Keśava / Amarendra / Kālī dāsa / Bhakta Dadhu / Bhakta Pradeep

## New Kurukṣetra, Caddo

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.*

Without the warm sun and the warm air, the seeds in the ground will rot and spoil.  
Without plentiful rains, all the grains, the fruits, and the vegetables will be stunted and poor in growth.  
Without the soothing moon's rays, all the crops would be without flavor and succulence.

Without Kṛṣṇa's causeless willingness to accept these fruits as offerings of love and devotion from us, there would be no use for the fruits, nor any importance to our existence, and both the fruits and the pickers of the fruits would become degraded. That is the case in this material world today, where the entire creation and all good qualities of man are dwindling rapidly because of their misuse.

What understanding or realizations we may have about Kṛṣṇa are due to your causeless mercy upon the entire world. You've made everything in the whole creation something of importance by linking it to Kṛṣṇa. Therefore we humbly beg of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you will causelessly accept this meager offering at your divine lotus feet. It's just a small portion of the fruit of our love for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

(Written by Gopāla dāsa)

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## New Māyāpura, France

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*  
Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet:

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Traversing the path back to Goloka Vṛndāvana, we pass various milestones along the way. For us the celebration of your appearance in this world is such a marker, a great one indeed. For on this day we approach you carrying both the faithful desires and promises of the previous year as well as the fructifications of those promises and desires in terms of tangibly achieved devotional service. These we lay before you for your satisfaction, approval, and blessings. The blessing that we so anxiously seek is that you will continually engage us in your service of establishing and spreading the ISKCON society throughout the world. And thus, by our becoming purified by that most dear service, you will once again reveal your beautiful, shining form to us all. We have implicit faith in your words that on the basis of service we will never be alone or separated but will always be in your company.

Yet at those moments in which our eyes would meet your penetrating glance, which would slice through all of our pretentious coverings, you revealed us as we really were. While we sat awkward and ashamed, like one left naked in royal company, you would simply smile and encourage us: "Come on!" These moments we long for. They remind us of Lord Caitanya and Sanātana Gosvāmī. Sanātana's body was covered with oozing sores, yet the Lord still embraced him with delight. You also continually gave us your embraces and blessings. Those moments are locked in our hearts eternally—of sitting in your presence as you spoke the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam;* of your chanting *"Jaya Rādhā Mādhava,"* placing your glasses on your beautiful face: *om namo bhagavate v*ā*sudevāya,* chanting the verse and then miraculously opening our minds, as if pulling the curtains on some vast windows, revealing all the intricacies of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa and His eternal pastimes in the spiritual world. These moments we cannot help but recall, and in doing so we cry for the time in which we may be reunited. . . Or the times when all the devotees in the temple would gather around the envelope marked "Tridaṇḍī Goswami A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami" and close their eyes in an attempt to catch each drop of nectar of your instructions as the letter was read. "Preaching is expanding. Use this method of distribution. Do as they are doing in Los Angeles, London. Be pure in heart, be free from duplicity or the desire for fame and honor. Go on sacrificing for Lord Caitanya, and at the moment of death He will appear within your mind and take you back to Godhead." Because these moments filled us with such a burning zeal to love you and serve you, feeling the intimacy of your association, we cry out that they will never slip from our recollections into the oblivion of forgetfulness. For we know that as Arjuna lost all strength by forgetting his Lord's instruction, making him the victim of cowherd men, we also, forgetting your instructions, will be plundered by all of the agents of Kali. Giving up your instructions means giving up your divine association, but in your association Kali cannot raise any challenge.

So we become happy satisfying you by our efforts and by striving to win your approval. Once you told a reporter, "The spiritual master, being the representative of God, teaches the disciple how to properly serve God. The disciple may choose to satisfy the *guru* by arranging a nice Cadillac automobile for him to ride in. But we should not think that this is special, for if the Supreme Lord were present, you would offer Him nothing less than a golden car." So the question is how to satisfy you and win your approval. How to offer you something so wonderful that you will tilt your head to the side and lovingly glance at us from the corner of your eyes, expanding your lips into that smile that embraces the entire universe? We will use the gifts that you have given us to satisfy you and to attract the world to the shelter of Govinda-Mādhava's lotus feet. We will use your books, the grand classics of India, on every bookshelf of every home in the world. One scripture: *Bhagavad-gītā;* one prayer: the *Hare Kṛṣṇa mahā-mantra,* sung beautifully in all the streets of all the villages and cities of the world. We will use your farms to attract hundreds and thousands of people to come, live, and take shelter from material strife. We could not understand why you wanted to build a temple in Ramaṇa-retī, away from the village hubbub of Vṛndāvana. Who would come? But even here in distant Lucay-Le-Male, fifteen thousand people came in two days to behold the transcendental festival of life in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We'll use your school to train generations of pure Vaiṣṇavas. We'll use the contact *saṅkīrtana* program to enter the homes of the misled masses, where the hot, raging winds of sense gratification blow, replacing them with the cool, sweet breezes of *sādhu-satsaṅga.*

To do all these things, though, we know that it is necessary that you guide us. We realize that you had a vision to use all of these projects as a lance to pierce the boillike infected heart of modern civilization. We need the guidance and intelligence to take what you have given and use it to influence human society. We pray that we will never take what you have given and use it for the purpose of sense gratification. Please protect us from quarreling or fighting amongst ourselves over differences of opinion, falling into the sectarian concept that "our way" is the only and best way of serving you. Please bless us with the intelligence to see all of the situations of life with the perspective that you do. In your last days in Vṛndāvana, one of your Godbrothers was having an animated conversation with you. You were laughing, answering, participating, apparently appreciating all he had to say. But twice you replied to his statements,

*tadera caraṇa-sevī bhakta sane vāsa*  
*janame janame hoy ei abhilāṣa*

**"This is my desire, that birth after birth I may live with those devotees that serve the lotus feet of the six Gosvāmīs."**

So please allow us to forever expand our service to you by serving your ISKCON society of devotees and by serving the greater family of humanity by spreading your message.

We possess few strong or overwhelmingly spiritual qualities, but we do love you very much and are eternally thankful for your having changed our hearts with your love. You are our ever well-wisher, revealer of spiritual mysteries, our eternal master and spiritual preceptor, and, above all, our kindest and dearmost friend. Śrīla Prabhupāda, we hanker for that early-morning unexpected telephone call from some far off place like Los Angeles or Delhi that informs us of your flight itinerary and arrival time in Paris—that phone call that would begin a whirlwind of activity that would turn our community upside down. And just as in those wonderful dreams that all of us so often have, you will suddenly appear in the midst of all of us, and we will begin to cry in great happiness, calling out, "Prabhupāda has returned," but then realize that actually you have never left.

To our greatest misfortune, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that phone call will never come.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we come before you today not to ask for any varieties of liberation, and certainly not for any material benedictions. Nor are we seeking even to return to the spiritual world. We are asking that you always allow us to perform vast amounts of service for your pleasure. At the end of our lives please allow us all to return to you, wherever you may be, to again fall at your lotus feet, never to leave your association throughout all of eternity.

Our lives are meant for your service and glorification. Please do with us as you see fit.

(Written by Ayodhyāpati dāsa)

*Falling at your lotus feet on this most auspicious day, the devotees of New Māyāpura,*

## New Orleans

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

By your unlimited mercy this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is expanding at an astounding rate. There are jso many temples, farms, and restaurants filled with *kṛṣṇa-bhaktas,* all because of you. These devotees of the Lord are training others in all of the devotional practices, and thus we see your mercy is ever-expanding, as your instructions are being lived and taught all over the world.

The receivers of this knowledge in time begin to give it out also, and therefore there is no stopping this fire of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You have said that they can bring big, big fire brigades, but it is too late. Kṛṣṇa consciousness is overcoming the materialistic minds of the modern masses.

We see how you have not only changed the lives of so many devotees, but the people in general are taking to Kṛṣṇa consciousness in small ways. Since you came to America in 1965 the whole outlook on life by the people of the world has changed. Their values have changed, and more and more we can see their dormant Kṛṣṇa consciousness being slowly aroused. Although deeply sunk in the degradation of sense gratification, they have created huge movements for vegetarianism, antiabortionism, healthy living. New Age (spiritualism), more spirituality in political leaders (Moral Majority), and many other signs of desire for something better. Also, interest in India and its culture and philosophy are becoming very popular in movies, fashion, and many other areas also.

We also find that a tremendous amount of people are becoming interested in this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. Many of them are taking initiation from your disciples and are in turn becoming preachers themselves. However, the vast majority of new followers are those who are not going to move into the temple but who want to stay at home and cultivate Kṛṣṇa consciousness in a more gradual way. This field of preaching is so vast that we cannot possibly visit all of these people regularly, although we are trying. These devotees are so numerous already, and they are also preaching like anything. Many temples are keeping track of such people's names and addresses and are communicating through visits, phone, and mail. The growth of such mailing lists is very rapid, as the devotees are meeting so many people daily who want to take part in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

However, all of our attempts to reach and preach to such people are so limited by time, energy, and manpower that we are forced to see the obvious. The obvious is that the best way to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness is to distribute your transcendental books.

We came to Kṛṣṇa consciousness because of reading your books. The people are coming to Kṛṣṇa consciousness because of reading your books. To train everyone in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, your books are the best method. They are so concentrated, convenient, practical, and they contain all the knowledge necessary to go back home, back to Godhead.

Therefore, O beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda, please allow us the mercy to take part in assisting you in your preaching. Your mercy is all that we are made of.

(Written by Raṅganātha dāsa)

*Your servants of Saṅkīrtana-pura, New Orleans,*

## New Ramaṇa-retī, Gainesville

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. As the years go on, your divine qualities and the transcendental nature of your activities become more and more obvious for us to see. The instructions which you have left us are more than enough to liberate the entire universe. This is the potency of the *jagad-guru,* or that person who can free everyone from the clutches of *māyā.* We were all sunk deep in this whirpool of illusion, and by your grace we have been placed in the transcendental boat of devotional service. Please allow us, even though unworthy, to remain under the protection of your ISKCON movement. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, give us the strength to carry out your desire to spread this movement to the limits of this world. We are very miserly and worldly-minded and have no hope other than to receive the shade of your lotus feet.

(Written by (Ṛtadhvaja Swami)

*We beg to remain your servants,*

## New Tālavana, Mississippi

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Thank you. Your disciples at your community in southwestern Mississippi, USA, are anxious every morning to serve your divine instructions. We may be lowborn and fallen, but your service is all we are made of. Śrī Kṛṣṇa is the goal of the *Vedas,* and service to His pure devotee is the only means to perfect this goal. By your divine inspiration, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we live by and with the scriptures, Vaiṣṇava literature, as your disciple Satsvarūpa dāsa Gosvāmī has pointed out about his life.

Every day the devotees are hearing and chanting about Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. They serve Their Lordships' *pujd* and work hard in different capacities to please you. Devotees and conditioned souls are always coming and going, and we take pleasure in serving them as you wanted. Actually, we can't appreciate enough your transcendental manners and dynamic sense of arousing Kṛṣṇa consciousness everywhere. Please forgive all of our offenses and allow us the opportunity to remain as your loyal disciples—eternally providing facilities in different ways for the devotees and conditioned souls.

At New Tālavana your glories are all around. They are in the memories and minds of the devotees. They are in your disciples' literatures and speeches. Most of all, they are in your transcendental words, which enlighten one in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Hearing your speech is the perfection of our ears, and speaking the realizations it produces is our highest ecstasy. Intelligently conceiving of how to maintain and promote your programs generates self-satisfaction and enthusiasm to execute your mission's directions. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for being so ever-liberal as to engage these crippled souls in your supreme welfare activities.

Destiny is on your side, Prabhupāda, for you are moved only by *śuddha-sattva guṇa.* Your joy is boundless and ever-encouraging. The devotees see you as beyond compare. We only want your heart's desire. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the transcendental awareness that everything is moving by Lord Kṛṣṇa's will, even a blade of grass. You're always serving the will of the Lord, and we're wanting to assist you.

Please allow us to practice serving you again and again. That is the perfection of our lives. May we never disobey you, O great, gentle, and serene mystic. Please allow us to follow in your footsteps and serve Your

Grace, observing your wonderful intelligence and fortitude. Your kindness and compassion are oceanic, and yet meek and mild are your shores. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Although I am incapable of serving your disciples at New Tālavana by properly glorifying you, I beg you to please accept our written Vyāsa-pūjā offering and continue to enthuse the devotees by showering Lord Kṛṣṇa's mercy upon us.

(Written by Yogīndravandana dāsa)

## New Vrindaban

He lives forever by his divine instructions, and the follower lives with him."

This is *bhakti,* devotion. The eternality of the spirit soul is established in devotion, you taught us, following faithfully in service to your Guru Mahārāja and Kṛṣṇa. What did we know of all this before you?

Your divine instructions are eternally with us, and we are unable to sever ourselves from them any more than water can shed its liquidity, or salt its salinity. You called it our occupational duty to worship and serve Lord Kṛṣṇa with love and joy.

Love, you emphasized—*bhakti.* God is attained by love, by *bhakti,* and by no other means. That has always been at the core. And all that you did, you did out of pure love for Kṛṣṇa. You were pure devotion in action. You did not sit in meditation at Rādhā-Dāmodara; you threw caution to the winds and sailed forth to act out of love for the Supreme Person and compassion for His parts and parcels—that is your life and your message.

August 17, 1968, our first year here at New Vrindaban, you wrote us: "Today they will observe Vyāsa-pūjā ceremony (my birthday anniversary), so from this day, I will be stepping on the seventy-third year. I hope the remaining days of my life may be utilized to serve all you Western devotees of Kṛṣṇa. Please pray to Kṛṣṇa that He may give me the necessary strength to discharge the duty entrusted by my spiritual master."

As your prayer was to discharge the instructions of your spiritual master, we now look to your instructions: "Now we can work with great enthusiasm for constructing a New Vrindaban in the USA. New Vrindaban is nothing physical or bodily, but purely spiritual and for the glorification of the Lord, Śrī Hari. If we actually keep this view in front, certainly we shall be successful in our progressive march."

Now you, our ever well-wisher, more than ever with us, are leading this march of spirit souls back to Godhead. Thrown by *karma* into the world, enjoying and suffering in our heavens and hells, born into darkness to parents who in their turn were born in darkness, indoctrinated by sensory knowledge, how could we know of spiritual realms, or of that divine person? Until you came, we sat in our cave, entranced by shadows flickering on the wall, not knowing which way to go or whether to go at all. Then you entered with your matchless gift: news of Kṛṣṇaloka and free passage there. "Where?" we asked. "Goloka Vṛndāvana," you said, "Lord Kṛṣṇa's supreme abode."

Out of compassion, you presented us with an easy journey to other planets and a warning: "Not all roads lead to Goloka Vṛndāvana." While so-called *gurus* told their students to take any road, you pointed to Lord Kṛṣṇa's timeless words: *devān deva-yajo yānti mad-bhakta yānti m*ā*m api.* "Those who worship the demigods go to the planets of the demigods, but My devotees ultimately reach My supreme planet."

Now, residing in your Palace of Gold and on *vyāsāsanas* around the world and in the hearts of your disciples, you have multiplied yourself, like the greatest of *yogīs,* to shed the light of divine knowledge to millions. When a spiritual body enters the material pond, it is untouched, but it sends forth countless ripples, without end. How can we approximate the impact of this movement? What was the extent of Christianity just eight years after the disappearance of Christ? Had St. Peter opened centers in Auckland and Cuzco? Cebu and Guayaquil? Helsinki and Mombasa?

Yes, you have so quickly multiplied yourself! The sun never sets on you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Your instructions are shedding light from hundreds of *vyāsāsanas,* inundating the planet with transcendental knowledge of the Supreme Person. Books everywhere in all languages! Your divine books—who could have presented them but you? Who could have explained His pastimes but you? Before you, many tried, but all failed. *Bhagavad-gītā* has been with us for five thousand years, but the glory of Śrī Kṛṣṇa's words awaited enunciation by your lotus lips. In our dark cave, we couldn't understand until you came bearing your torchlight, brightening our world with the *mahā-mantra* and showing us that "unto Me" means unto the person Kṛṣṇa.

"Always think of Me and become My devotee. Worship Me and offer your homage unto Me. Thus you will come to Me without fail. I promise you this because you are My very dear friend." Your life served as an example of how this is to be done. Without you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we could never have guessed the meaning of these words. Blinded by commentaries of envious rascals and by the dirty things in our own hearts, we were licking at a sealed honey jar. But you opened up that jar and let the nectar flow. You took our hand and led us to the path of devotion. A patient, loving father to us all, you watched our first stumbling steps, our falls, and picked us up and kindly encouraged us on.

Now, dear father, Jagad-guru, seated in your palace, in your temples, and in our hearts, please guide us to the end of our journey back to Him. Grant us the grace to follow and serve you with greater determination. Teach us to know what to accept and what to reject. Fix us in our resolve to attain Him through service to you. Kindly, out of your infinite mercy, forgive our offenses and release us from our material hankerings, that we may realize your service in the purity of full Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

*The inmates of New Vrindaban,*

## New York

The misguided want miracles as proof of a great personality's spiritual potency, not understanding the difference between tricks (manipulation of material energy) and actions God empowered.

Walking on water, water into wine  
Walking the line of hot coals (for only $175)  
Without burning their souls.  
Levitating into the air (for only $3000),  
Mystic power stored in long hair,  
Raising the dead from their death beds,  
Or reading the future  
So that one may suture  
The wounds of a bleeding present or past,  
With the hope that some bit of happiness can be grasped.  
But you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, laughed  
At tricks of this sort,  
Seeing it only as sport  
For the foolish and crass.  
Why waste time turning water into wine?  
You turned dogs and swine, camels and ass  
Into devotees of Kṛṣṇa.  
You walked on water when you crossed the border  
Of the spiritual world, walking on the ocean of nescience,  
Without being affected or wet,  
Casting Lord Caitanya's net  
To capture drowning souls. Better yet,  
You walked on barren rock (Manhattan),  
Where only buildings grow and crumble  
With every tick of the clock,  
And planted seeds (bhakti-latā-bījas)  
In the hearts of millions.  
Those seeds took root and bore the fruit  
Of pure love for Kṛṣṇa.  
What more a miracle than that can one ask for?  
Your body and blood may not have transformed  
Into bread and wine,  
But in a short time into something more remarkable,  
This spiritually dynamic ISKCON society. (For you  
Have said, "I AM ISKCON.") Printing books, making  
Vaiṣṇavas, opening centers, all signs of its vitality.  
As for raising the dead,  
You took walking dead heads  
From their ignorant beds  
And breathed life into them  
Through the chanting of the holy name.  
(Now there are many Lazarus's preaching on your behalf.)  
You relieved the burning souls (from suffering)  
Who dared walk the fiery coals of material existence  
With the cooling shelter of your lotus feet.  
You not only saw into the future and past  
(Because the pure devotee is tri kala jña)  
But showed us how to live in the present,  
For you eternally dwell in Vṛndāvana, and  
In the spiritual world no past or future exists.  
It is a world of eternal bliss and cognizance.  
These, and more, are just a glimpse  
Of your greatness,  
Glimpses of the miracles that you manifest.  
We just pray for one more miracle, that somehow,  
Despite all the weakness of heart,  
We never depart  
From the shelter of your divine grace.

*Your servants at Rādhā-Govinda Mandira,*  
*Brooklyn, New York,*

## North Carolina

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

This year in ISKCON there has been much discussion about keeping you in the center of ISKCON. As a result, we have been meditating and praying to you to actually \[understand what it means to "keep you in the center." The meaning is not as apparent as one might suppose.

We have experience, for example, that *sahajiyās* appear to be always speaking about Kṛṣṇa and therefore seem to be absorbed in the transcendental form of the Lord. But in actuality, as you have informed us, they are simply meditating upon their own perverted desires. They are not keeping Kṛṣṇa in the center, although ostensibly they appear to do so.

Even the nondevotees, when they "fall in love," appear to be thinking of each other, but we understand that their thoughts are confined to the realm of sense gratificatory projections of their own contaminated selves. "There is no love in the material world," as you have many times told us. There is only the appearance of love.

So, in order to understand how to keep you, and therefore Kṛṣṇa, in the center of our existence, we must understand what is love. The topmost example of love that we have is your self-sacrificing life of service to your spiritual master, which is exemplified by the mood of the *gopīs* when they desired to cure the headache of Kṛṣṇa at any cost, even the cost of their own future well-being. They were ready to go to hell to satisfy Kṛṣṇa. Their own happiness or well-being was inconsequential to them.

Such is your mood in relationship to your spiritual master. So if we are to really understand love, and how to keep you in the center, we must learn to imbibe your mood and emulate it, for your pastimes on this earth must not only be "relishable stories" for us to tell each other, but must be the very form of perfection that we are always conscious of and striving for.

As you have so eloquently spoken in your Vyāsa-pūjā homage to your spiritual master, "But simply a festival of flowers and fruits does not constitute worship. The one who serves the message of the *guru* really worships him . . ." We must see that keeping you in the center does not involve making some change in the temple schedule or cutting down the legs of others' *āsanas.* Keeping you in the center means understanding your compassion, seeing the pain that you are experiencing at observing the plight of the conditioned souls, and acting to relieve you of that pain by taking up the burden of your compassion.

Anyone who acts in this consciousness is keeping you in the center, and to assist or even worship such a person is also part and parcel of keeping you in the center.

*Your servants at New Goloka (North Carolina),*

## Nueva Gauḍa-maṇḍala-bhūmi, Mexico

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Kindly accept our most humble obeisances given at your holy feet.

It is said in the scriptures that wherever the holy ones go, such a place becomes one of pilgrimage. Your steps have arrived at our hearts, and we want to convert them into places of pilgrimage. Above all, we want to clean this place, removing all the bad qualities that we have cultivated throughout so many births and deaths and guard very intimately and eternally the association you have very kindly granted us.

With great humility, we, the devotees of New Gauḍa-maṇḍala-bhūmi, beg you to take us into your care, primarily to protect us from the devastating influences of the external energy of the Lord. Our relationship with you is the supreme nectar, and we like to be under your care, like small birds taking refuge under the wings of their mother when they face the first obstacles of life.

Your infinite kindness and your innocent smile have showered our sensibilities. That is the potency of a pure devotee, to sensitize the living entity by his precise words according to the needs of each individual.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the active principle of our movement. Your instructions and teachings flow through our veins, irrigating the most hidden parts of our bodies as the Ganges transports its holy water, purifying the three worlds.

*Kāla,* eternal time, erases even the most prominent events, and its influence is unlimited. So it finds itself disarmed and defeated when confronted by the solid reputation that you have erected on the genealogical tree of Vaiṣṇavism. We want to become faithful to your works and associate ourselves with you in presenting your instructions in a bona fide manner, simply because that is your desire. Your desires become our order to the point of turning us into slaves in your hands, eager to raise up this society to the light of the sun as an offering to the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

## North Shore

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

On this most auspicious day of your appearance, your Vyāsa-pūjā, we are pleased to inform you that another temple, a Hare Kṛṣṇa Cultural Center, has opened for your glorification.

Just fulfilling your desire of a temple in every town and village, the new Hare Kṛṣṇa Cultural Center stands out like a beacon beaming transcendental knowledge to all inquisitive souls.

Simply by following your instructions and the regulated program you have given us, the temple is guaranteed success, and if we simply place your books and glorious self as the heart of the new temple, we are assured that you will pump life into the hearts of all the conditioned souls.

You are fulfilling the prophecy that you are building "a house in which the whole world can live." As I stand in that house, which has such solid columns as the present dcaryas, your disciples, the foundation being the Vedic knowledge in the parampara system, and the roof being your beloved ISKCON, I remember how you laid and molded each stone into place, trained and retrained your managers. Although I am not qualified, you have allowed me into your house. Please let me remember how much devotion you put into your disciples, so that we can carry out your mission purely to achieve all the goals that you have for your society.

(Written by Kārṣṇa dāsa)

## Ottawa

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my obeisances at your feet. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the foremost saṅkīrtana devotee and the bona fide representative of Lord Caitanyadeva.

The greatness of a *mahātma* is that he can make others *mahātmās*. You are the first person we've ever met who is treating us as spirit souls. You've reinstated us in our constitutional position. Most important, you've given us the fastest way to go back to Godhead: the *saṅkīrtana* movement. ISKCON will stay alive and vibrant as long as we continue to push on the *saṅkīrtana* movement; otherwise there is a possibility of our becoming fat cats.

Here in Ottawa, the northernmost *saṅkīrtana* outpost, there is still snow on the ground in mid-April. However, by the mercy of Lord Caitanya we're able to do *saṅkīrtana* with the minimum of discomfort; the *karmīs* have covered the main street of Ottawa with a glass roof, and the whole thing is heated. So even at minus 30 degrees we can chant, distribute BTGs, and do paraphernalia. You once said, "*Impossible* is a word in a fool's dictionary," and this especially applies to *saṅkīrtana*. Thank you for everything, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*The devotees of Ottawa,*

Kuṭicaka dāsa

## Panama

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Your lotus feet are the objective of the great souls, who always find a secure shelter there. The modes of material nature are very difficult to transcend, even though they can be surpassed if someone takes shelter in devotional service. But who can execute devotional service without obtaining the dust of your lotus feet? No one can do it! As Prahlāda Mahārāja affirms, *mahīyasāṁ pāda-rajo- 'bhiṣekam*: One should smear his whole body with the dust of the lotus feet of a pure devotee as you, if he desires to obtain pure devotional service and detachment from materialistic life.

The process of hearing your words as you express them in classes and in your books is the only authorized way to come out of the darkness and go to the spiritual light, eternal, full of knowledge and bliss. And when one becomes perfect by hearing you, then his only task should be to spread the words that emanate from your lotus mouth. As King Prthu prayed:

*mahāttamāntar-hṛdayān mukha-cyuto*  
*vidhatsva karṇāyutam eṣa me varaḥ*

**"My dear Lord, I want the blessings to have at least one million ears, since in that way I will be able to hear about the glories of Your lotus feet from the lips of Your pure devotee."**

*Guru-kṛṣṇa-kṛpā:* by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa one finds the guru, and by the grace of the guru one obtains Kṛṣṇa. That is why we pray to you that we can remain in Kṛṣṇa consciousness birth after birth, chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa. We pray to Kṛṣṇa so that He may permit us to remain as a grain of dust at your lotus feet.

Please Śrīla Prabhupāda, permit us to live in the shade of your lotus feet and simultaneously illuminate the entire universe by distributing your nectarean words.

*Your fallen servants of ISKCON Panama,*

Guṇeśvara dāsa / Keśava dāsa / Rāmāi dāsa / Māyeśa dāsa / Gauracandra dāsa / Rāghava dāsa / Sundarācala dāsa / Bhakta Eloy / Madana Gopāla-devī dāsī / Lavaṅgamaṅgala-devī dāsī / Devahūti-devī dāsī

## Penang

JAYA, ŚRĪLA PRABHUPĀDA!

**Personalism vs. Impersonalism**

The Buddhists seek nirvana to become one with the Supreme (they sacrifice their individuality)  
And thus deny themselves any personal reciprocation with the sweet form of the Lord  
In any of the ongoing transcendental relationships that take precedence over merging into the impersonal brahma-jyotir (kaivalya),  
Which for the devotee is no less a form of spiritual suicide,  
Simply nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī.

The Muslims claim to have a good sādhana, five times a day,  
But who do they pray to when no form of God graces any of their mosques?  
"God has no form!" they claim. "But we have form, "we counter. "Are we not part of God?"  
"Yes, but He," they say, "does not have to assume any of the forms He creates."  
Then if God is formless, where to repose one's love? (In any relationship, even within this world, there is form, what to speak of God?)  
Simply nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī.

The Christians loudly sing, "Give us this day our daily bread" as they view their perfect master nailed to the cross *(*which is akin to having Haridāsa Ṭhākura being mercilessly whipped in twenty two marketplaces in place of our worshipable Deity).  
"God is not your order-supplier, and Christ cannot forgive your foolishness indefinitely,"  
Guru insists. " You remain indifferent to His commandments, and therefore His worshipable Lord is not obliged to reveal Himself to such mundaners."  
When we cannot love the Lord's pure representative (since we're unable to abide by his good instruction), then where is the question of loving God!  
Simply nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī.

The Hindus. Hodge-podge, Your Divine Grace has said. Claiming all gods are same, but only Kṛṣṇa can save you at the time  
of death. Anta-kāle ca mām eva. (Bg. 8.5)  
Bhagavad-gītā rendered meaningless by so many pseudoreligionists and māyāvādī impersonalists until you came and gave us

Bhagavad-gītā As It Is;

No compromise, no misconception, no misrepresentation or misdirection,  
No intentional misleading or attempts at self-deception,  
No contradictory statements that "I am God" or "We are all God"; no flying in the air or seeing bright lights or immoral  
behavior all in the name of yoga or Hindu-dharma. You showed us how to practically love God (Kṛṣṇa) by personal  
reciprocation. You showed us real surrender, not sentiment or speculation.  
No more nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, you showed Kṛṣṇa is a person, the Supreme Person, and because devotional service is exchanged between persons (the devotee and the Lord), the worship of the Lord's personal form as the Deity is of paramount importance.

*teṣāṁ satata-yuktānāṁ*  
*bhajatāṁ prīti-pūrvakam*

**"To those who constantly worship Me in love and devotion, I give them the intelligence by which they can come to Me." (Bg. 10.10)**

This incites the heart of all religious instructions, "surrender to God," which is the original, pure teaching of Lord Buddha, Muhammad, and Lord Jesus Christ.

*mac-cittā mad gata-prāṇā*  
*bodhayantaḥ parasparam*

**"The thoughts of My pure devotees dwell in Me always, their lives are surrendered to Me, and they derive great bliss and satisfaction enlightening one another and conversing about Me." (Bg. 10.9)**

Lord Kṛṣṇa is the supreme proprietor, friend, and well-wisher of every living being. Being His pure representative, you likewise have no enemy. You always signed your letters "Your ever well-wisher, A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami." We cannot hope to repay you, but we must eternally serve YOU, glorify YOU, and thank YOU. Thank you for the association of devotees, for the glorification of the Lord in His *arcā-vigraha* form, for *prasādam,* for your books, for the regulative principles, for the chanting of the holy name, and all the concomitant factors that go to make up your ISKCON body

Moreover, we thank you for reestablishing our personal identities as *dāsānudāsa.*

*Begging for your mercy, your insignificant beggars of ISKCON Penang,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Dhruvanātha dāsa

## Perth

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

On this most auspicious day of your appearance, please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, just as the opulences of Lord Kṛṣṇa are unfathomable to the conditioned souls, so your opulences are also unfathomable. You have spread the holy name throughout the whole world and driven away the darkness of Kali-yuga. As Śrīla Bhaktivinoda has sung, there is nothing but the holy name within the fourteen worlds, and you are that divine personality chosen and empowered by Lord Gaurāṅga to propagate the holy name.

*Hari-nāma* is the *yuga-dharma.* Without chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa there is no chance of freeing oneself from the material world. The holy name should never be considered material. Rather, the holy name is transcendental and is nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa. Because the holy name is completely pure, only a great, pure soul can effectively deliver this holy name to others. This is actually a miracle that you have performed. One time, when asked if you could perform miracles, you said yes and pointed to your many devotees present, whom you had transformed from crows into transcendental swans. No one else could have done that.

The quality of the *guru* is seen in the quality of the disciples. Now your disciples have taken over the task of distributing the holy name, and throughout the world Kṛṣṇa consciousness is booming. This is all due to the foundation that you laid, this wonderful ISKCON movement, the only hope for the world in these troubled times.

So, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are simply praying that just as you performed miracles, we can also follow in your footsteps and deliver the holy name to others by your mercy. Please let us serve your mission eternally.

(Written by Puruṣottama dāsa)

*Your servants in Perth, Australia,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Puṇḍarīka dāsa

## Philadelphia

Let us loudly chant the holy names  
Gaurāṅga’s golden era is here  
Hare Kṛṣṇa—hari-nāma saṅkīrtana  
For the next ten thousand years.

Caitanya 's tree is overloaded  
Bearing fruits of love of God  
ISKCON is the worldwide distributor  
Founder-ācārya—Śrīla Prabhupāda.

In future cantos of the Bhāgavatam  
The story will be shown  
Of Prabhupāda's nonstop preaching  
Melting hearts of stone.

The world is becoming Vaikuṇṭha  
Historians will have to write  
The course of Kali has been changed  
By your spiritual might.

All the universe will worship you  
As the greatest of the sages  
Your Vyāsa-pūjā book will  
Then swell to unlimited pages.

Jagad-guru, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Let us fall at your lotus feet  
For many kalpas to come  
No one can compete.

High court judges wearing tilaka and mālā  
We know it will come true  
ISKCON teaching the innocent masses  
Making Vaiṣṇavas from Kali's zoo.

Kali's forces are forced to retreat  
Meeting an army of pure devotees  
Mahāprabhu’s plans are already manifest  
But are we qualified to see?

Śaktyāveśa-avatāra, nitya-siddha  
Our beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda  
You walked amongst our midst  
Preaching pure love of God.

Purports speaking absolute knowledge  
Let every family have a set  
Your association is in your books  
Please let us never forget  
It was you who planted the seed  
You gave us bhakti and showed us how  
If you had not come to us  
Then where would we all be now?

Let us walk in your lotus footsteps  
Allow us to become Prabhupādānugas  
Follow strictly—purity is the force  
Preaching in the abyss of Kali-yuga.

Today is the day to rededicate our lives  
To giving others your association  
Nothing can compare to the gift we offer  
For humans, it is the highest benediction.  
Prabhupāda's compassion for all humanity  
Is beyond our comprehension  
For ten thousand years your blessings will be given  
To those who follow your instructions.  
O Prabhupāda, there is no one like you  
In all the three worlds  
You are the most brilliant gem  
The most illustrious pearl.

You are the captain of the ship  
That can take us over māyā's ocean  
The Vedas are the favorable breeze  
Guiding us to pure devotion.

For those who take a bath in the dust  
Of your lotus feet  
Material suffering—birth and death  
No longer will they have to meet.

Preaching as a family unit  
Sanātana-dharma, Kṛṣṇa's laws  
This is the test of our love for you  
Cooperate for the common cause.

Devotees who deviate in some way  
Their dormant love cannot manifest  
What a tragedy to see them fall  
Another face like all the rest.

If our purpose is weak, māyā will catch us  
O servants of Prabhupāda  
No one can be proud on the path of devotion  
Love also means the chastising rod

Prabhupāda you are still with us  
In your books, tapes and Līlāmṛta  
By loving you we can love Kṛṣṇa  
Which is the essence of the Gītā.

Our realization of Kṛṣṇa is dependent  
On our understanding of you  
Our hearts will melt when we learn to appreciate  
Your love for Mahāprabhu.

Accepting anxiety on your behalf  
This is our highest ecstasy  
"If you love me, I'll love you"  
When will we have the eyes to see?

All glories to His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda!!

## Radio KHQN, Spanish Fork

Śrī Kṛṣṇa is Lord of contradiction  
Dilemma and irony  
He resolves the most amazing scenes  
With uncanny mastery

He manifested a wondrous event  
With great armies, snake and hill  
And all this just to pacify  
An itching on His shell  
He roped poor Ball shamefully  
In the throes of great reverse  
After having—with two dwarflike steps—  
Crossed the universe

His separated mind burned with anguish  
Intolerable to see  
And yet the one He longed for so  
Was ultimately He

Śrīla Prabhupāda came to this forsaken land  
Where no saint before had trod  
Yet by preaching and chanting in firmest faith  
He earned the blessings of God

He gathered armies from the lost  
The lonely, unshorn, haunted  
And yet his efforts to please the Lord  
Continue on undaunted

But we had mentioned ironies  
And certainly there's one at hand  
For now the radio broadcasts  
Kṛṣṇa’s glories in Mormonland

Mormon, Hindu, Muslim, Jain  
(Mere designations at best)  
All fade before the holy name  
The ultimate religious test

Are we attached to the Lord's servant's feet  
Or a show of deep emotion?  
Do we long for security  
Or genuine devotion?

Thus Prabhupāda has established on earth  
The highest spiritual state  
Forget your longing / or karma and jñāna  
They are but second-rate

The gift we’ve gleaned from Prabhupāda  
Must urgently be told  
To claim as many as we can  
Into Śrī Kṛṣṇa's fold

And when you 're passing through these parts  
By train or car or plane  
You 're sure to smile when you turn your dial  
To Kṛṣṇa on KHQN

## Rio de Janeiro

*Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**"I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, who has opened my eyes, which were blinded by the darkness of ignorance, with the torchlight of knowledge."**

Our hearts for quite an eternity had been searching for the truth. We had committed so many mistakes, we had suffered so many reactions, and life was going on, and nothing really good was happening—nothing, no revelations. Everything was the same, the same void. Time was passing, and we were drowning more and more in the mud of our own minds. In this life of ignorance the only real thing that had occurred was the realization of our own frustrations, our lost loves, broken hearts, and agitated false egos.

But one day, all of a sudden, by your mercy, by the purification of your sunny effulgence, came the Truth Absolute, satiating our desires. Our frustrations came to an end, our vain passions stopped, and in ecstasy we are born again, as new addicts who take seriously a new although very old medicine—your instructions.

He reasons ill who tells that Vaiṣṇavas die  
When thou art living still in sound.  
The Vaiṣṇavas die to live, and living try  
To spread the holy name around.

He lives forever by his divine instructions, and the follower lives with him.

## St. Louis

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet, which are traversing the world still in the form of your books, your instructions, and your devotees. All glories to you on this, the most auspicious day for all devotees of Lord Kṛṣṇa! Because you are the greatest personality and practically "all that is" for your devotees, we want to be able to associate with you even within this world. The Vedic literatures state that this association can be attained by following the *vāṇī* (instructions) of the spiritual master, and the secret of success is to fulfill the instructions of the spiritual master and thus please him.

I remember the first time we received some instruction, about twelve years ago. Many traveling *saṅkīrtana* parties had gone to Los Angeles to see and hear Your Divine Grace. It was an early January, Friday morning, which meant in those days a *māhā-saṅkīrtana* day. The lectures for the past few days spoke of the devotees' deathlessness. You compared the devotee's death to the kitten in the mouth of the cat, whereas the nondevotee's death meant the rat in the mouth of the cat. Then that morning you said something that rings in the ears and hearts of devotees all over the world to this day. "There is no literature throughout the universe like *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* There is no comparison or competition. Every word is for the good of human society, each and every word. Therefore we stress so much in the book distribution. Somehow or other, if the book goes in one's hand, he'll be benefited. At least he'll see, 'I have taken so much price—let me see what is there.' If he reads one *śloka,* his life will become successful. If he reads one *śloka,* one word! This is such a nice thing. Therefore we are stressing so much, please distribute books, distribute books, distribute books!"

That was all we needed. We left that place and traveled all over the country distributing books like madmen. We were touched whenever you would say anything about book distribution and took those statements as our life and soul. We were after your recognition as we heard you say, "The sales figures, this is the only solace in my life. When I hear that my books are selling nicely, I become energetic like a young man." There was no doubt about what to do and what our connection with you was. We weren't big guns who could sit at a meeting with you or get personal attention so much. Maybe some morning walks at the Māyāpur festival we got to sneak in with the *sannyāsīs,* but otherwise our book distribution was our personal association with you. You often spent your time translating the *Bhāgavatam,* and we would spend ours in distributing that *Bhāgavatam.* Then you laid it down: "Actually it is a fact that ultimately everyone should preach and distribute books if they want to please me in the best way Book distribution must be given stress always."

In the beginning of April 1974, a group of devotees distributing books at the World's Fair in Spokane wrote to you inviting you to come and speak there, as well as informing you of our book sales results. You replied on April 20, 1974, "better you yourselves do as much as possible to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness there by distribution of literature, *kīrtana,* and *prasādam,* and that will be as effective as my personally coming. I am very keen on the distribution of my books, and I am very indebted to all of you for your untiring efforts to see that every man and woman in America get one of my books. If they simply read one page, even if they do nothing else, they can become perfect. Please keep me informed of your progress as the Fair develops." We took this as our eternal instruction. When we were distributing books, you said that it was as effective as your personally being there. That meant that by continually distributing your books you would always personally be there. Furthermore, your order was that every man and woman in America get one of your books. Obviously this was a tall order that might not even be able to be fulfilled in one lifetime. It was also a very menial order, and sometimes, due to our being puffed up and wanting to be master, the order seems a little bit difficult.

Therefore we are praying to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Somehow or other let us be conscious of our position as menial servants meant to simply fulfill your order. Keep us in the mood, just like in the 70s, of embracing your intimate directions. Let us keep you personally present and in the center of our lives by following your order of distributing books till our very last breath and beyond!

Śrīla Prabhupāda *ki jaya!*

(Written by Sura dāsa)

Your menial servants,

## San Diego

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Inconceivable is the word which to me most thoroughly describes you, because your activities are cent per cent inspired by the divine energy of the Lord—*daivīṁ prakṛtim āśritāḥ.* Yet still, as your disciples, we are supposed to endeavor to understand you in our never-ending attempt to follow you in executing your mission of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the world. But it is futile. How can a lowborn fool like myself comprehend the position of a *paramahaṁsa*? You possess *acintya-śakti;* therefore I cannot measure your greatness.

Personally I was never with you when you were not either talking about Kṛṣṇa, preaching, or hearing *kṛṣṇa-kathā.* At those times I would be puzzled, considering how our movement was expanding throughout the world under your influence, while you were simply discussing philosophy, as if nothing were more important. You didn't fit my image of a sole managerial leader of a worldwide movement. It didn't look as though you were doing what someone would have to do to organize a large religious organization. In any case, I couldn't understand your position then, and I admit that today, even though I understand more, I still find your character beyond my grasp.

When I was personally initiated by you with a group of other devotees, the devotee who sat on my right had formerly been a heavy smoker. When he recited the four regulative principles, you questioned him in a humorous way about smoking, while making a gesture imitating a person smoking. When I recited the four principles you made no comment. When the devotee sitting on my left (formerly a heavy user of LSD) recited the four regulative principles, you said in a joking way, "What about LSD?" Again I was defeated in my futile attempts to estimate the position of my spiritual master.

In 1973, during the Christmas marathon, our temple decided that we could be just as successful by using direct preaching lines and wearing *dhotīs,* while other devotees were in *karmī* clothes, using more indirect tactics. We felt this "pure approach" was the topmost form of *saṅkīrtana.* Because we were successful, we wrote you, feeling that we had accomplished something wonderful and were setting a precedent as well. We were also a bit proud of our accomplishment, but being immature we didn't recognize it. We were certain that you would glorify us for discovering the ways and means to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness directly and purely. To our utter amazement, you replied that if some of us were uncomfortable wearing *karmī* clothes and using indirect tactics, we could wear *dhotīs* and be more direct. Thus we were made to feel inferior to the other *saṅkīrtana* devotees (which we needed to hear), while you encouraged the mass of devotees who chose to wear *karmī* clothes. None of us expected such a reply.

The 1970 San Francisco Rathayātrā hosted the largest gathering of devotees to date. When you blissfully walked into the temple and saw the room completely filled with shaven-headed men, you became so pleased that you mentioned that you called your Guru Mahārāja to come and see. You could call your spiritual master at will to visit and observe your activities. Thinking of this, I simply stand in awe with folded palms.

In one lecture you mentioned the story of Bhīṣmadeva lying on the bed of arrows and how wonderful he was. You also described how wonderful Lord Kṛṣṇa was for being present before Bhīṣma on his deathbed. You then stopped speaking because of your choked voice, due to transcendental ecstasy. We've all also heard this story many times, and we all have some appreciation as well. I cannot measure the depth of your attraction for Kṛṣṇa, for simply by thinking of the Lord's mercy on Bhīṣmadeva you become overwhelmed with loving sentiments.

When you visited Mauritius in 1975 you predicted that Mauritius, which is but a small island of one million people, would become the Vatican of ISKCON and would also be the first Kṛṣṇa conscious country. Sometimes we speak of big vision. To me this is telescopic.

In 1971 I ventured on one of the first traveling *saṅkīrtana* parties launched in ISKCON; in fact it was the first traveling *saṅkīrtana* party that was ever reported to you. The party was such a great success that I wrote the devotees a full report of our daily activities, which they then decided to forward to Your Divine Grace. My wife sent the letter, and for some reason she mentioned that I had a wart and requested a remedy, as in those days we depended on you for everything. I had had this wart for seven years and was unable to get rid of it, despite much medical assistance. Shortly after she sent the letter the wart mysteriously disappeared. In 1972 my wife asked through your servant if you had a cure for worms, to which you replied, "I am not your doctor, I am your spiritual master. Consult a doctor."

Another amazing feature of your personality was your knowledge of more obscure subjects. In Los Angeles, in 1972, you were explaining to the senior men how a model ship was built inside a bottle. Also, in the middle of the night in Māyāpur, you called for your servant to place your mosquito net around your bed and went on to give a complete explanation how, by the wind changing directions, the mosquitoes came flying into the room, whereas the wind's blowing in a particular other direction would cause them to fly elsewhere.

When we think of saintly persons like Jesus Christ, Prahlāda Mahārāja, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta, etc., we don't think of them as humorous persons. Although we consider you the greatest of saints and most sober, you would often be funny and tell light jokes to engladden your disciples. A great sage telling jokes—this certainly transcends preconceived notions of a world savior.

On a morning walk in Los Angeles in 1976, Rāmeśvara Mahārāja began glorifying the preaching work of Hṛdayānanda Mahārāja. Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja was present, and knowing his disposition, you told Rāmeśvara Mahārāja that he should not continue speaking in this way; Tamāla would have a heart attack. Immediately everyone burst out smiling, and you were laughing more than any of us.

While you were watching the debut of the film *Spiritual Frontier* in the Los Angeles temple along with all the devotees, there was a scene showing *puris* rising in ghee. When you saw the *puris,* you jokingly stated, "Let me have one," and began chuckling with the other four hundred devotees.

You are our spiritual master, our worshipable lord, the greatest personality to all of us, yet still you remain the most humble. In 1972 in Los Angeles, while coming into the temple to greet the Deities, a devotee immediately behind you shouted, "Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!" Thinking someone was calling you, you turned around to that devotee and said, "Yes?" The devotee again repeated, "Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!" to confirm that no question was asked but that you were being praised. You replied a humble "Thank you very much," and proceeded to pay obeisances to the Deities. I can't understand why you said thank you, but that's understandable, because I can't understand you.

At the 1970 Rathayātrā, in the "Family Dog" auditorium, I saw, by your influence, a hall filled wall to wall with people dancing and jumping. When you stood up and raised your arms, onlookers could not control their ecstasy and were forced to jump in the air under your influence. The fact is that chanting the holy name in your presence was the greatest ecstasy, to which no *kīrtana* will ever compare.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, whoever met you was never again the same. When I first saw you I cried, which is something I never do. I can't say why I cried, because I really didn't understand why I'd seen Godbrothers become inspired to distribute over one hundred big books in a day by seeing you arrive at the airport. I've seen myself become totally inspired to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness for an entire year just by seeing you for one day of that year. I've met life members who are fully dedicated to ISKCON because of meeting you. I've met people who are now beating their heads because they missed the opportunity to personally see your divine form. I've seen hoards of devotees who'd left ISKCON return with shaven heads and rededicated hearts when you would visit Los Angeles. I've seen wealthy grown men surrender hundreds of thousands of dollars to you. One time you stated, "Are you sure you want to give this all to me?" I've seen two-year devotees become first-class *sannyāsīs* by your inspiration, three-month devotees open new temples by your order, and inexperienced men develop a million-dollar incense business with your ideas. While all this was going on, I would wonder in amazement and think, "All Śrīla Prabhupāda seems to be doing is talking about Kṛṣṇa, yet the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is expanding by leaps and bounds. How is this going on?" *Acintya-śakti.* We know that we are insignificant. Our prayer is to gain, through following your orders, a particle of your potency, for that potency will deliver the fallen souls.

## San Francisco

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*  
Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu's *saṅkīrtana* mission!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

When you were here with us in your external presence, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we could come to you with whatever problems or differences confronted our society or ourselves. Everyone had full faith in your authority, because you manifested all the qualities of a liberated, pure devotee. You did not demand respect; you commanded respect.

In our experience, you always tried to overcome all obstacles progressively, one at a time, in establishing your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. You were never a rigid, authoritarian figure, always sticking to established dogma, but you were always expert in time and circumstance. This was possible for you because you were always fixed in pure service to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, whose satisfaction is the highest religious principle. Because of this pure devotional mood, your heart was always devoid of false prestige, pride, and envy, and thus your conclusions were authoritative, appreciated and accepted by all.

*Ācārya* means one who teaches by example. The topmost instruction of the *ācārya* is to show how to selflessly serve the Lord by delivering the fallen conditioned souls. The *ācārya* has no personal ambitions. His only ambition is to please the Lord by delivering the fallen souls to the Lord's lotus feet. One who understands this principle perfectly becomes a reflection of the previous *ācārya.*

As you are simply carrying the order of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, this is also your instruction to us. Lord Caitanya has instructed everyone, "By My order become *guru* and deliver this entire world." Lord Caitanya's order is not only an absolute instruction but is also the qualification necessary to fulfill that order. Therefore, we sincerely pray that you will be pleased upon our humble attempts to serve the will of the Lord in this material world and inspire us by your internal and eternal presence to more and more take up this most responsible task. Only when this is taken up collectively will ISKCON be able to unify with oneness of purpose, free from all traces of the illusory energy.

San Francisco is a very important landmark in your preaching mission in the Western world. It was here that you introduced Lord Jagannātha and His Rathayātrā festival to the world. It was here that many of your great devotees, such as Jayānanda dāsa and others, joined you for the first time. Indeed, it was Jayānanda who dedicated his life to pleasing you by making Rathayātrā carts all over America. Here, only a few blocks away from our temple with large installed Gaura-Nitāi Deities, is your original temple on Frederick Street. It is a gray-faced storefront, much the same as the storefront which you occupied at 26 Second Avenue in New York. Surely it is only by your blessings that our little temple is now expanding so rapidly. By your mercy only are new devotees joining us, and old devotees are rejoining also—so much so that the temple population has more than doubled in the last two months alone.

Now let us appeal to you in all of our humility for your blessings, so that we may make Kṛṣṇa consciousness grow like wildfire all over the San Francisco Bay area. You showed your compassion to help the world become Kṛṣṇa conscious, and now, by your mercy, we are sharing some of that burden of love and feeling the satisfaction that goes along with it. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! All glories to your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, which is the manifestation of your mercy upon the fallen souls of the Kali-yuga. *Haribol!*

(Written by Vipramukhya Swami and Triyogī dāsa)

*Your humble servants at the ISKCON Bhaktivedanta Fellowship and temple,*

## San Salvador

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura has instructed us in his *Śrī Gurv-aṣṭaka,*

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto 'pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**"By the mercy of the spiritual master one receives the benediction of Kṛṣṇa. Without the grace of the spiritual master, one cannot make any advancement. Therefore, I should always remember and praise my spiritual master. At least three times a day I should offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of my spiritual master."**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is only by your mercy that we have received some benediction from Kṛṣṇa, for we have not lost the enthusiasm in our struggle against material illusion. On the contrary, we have achieved firm determination in our loving devotional service.

On this auspicious day, when we celebrate your appearance in this world, we wish to offer some special adoration to Your Divine Grace, for it is only by your mercy that we have gained some attachment for the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

*Your humble servants of New Kurukṣetra Mandira and Hogar Escuela Hare Kṛṣṇa,*

## Santo Domingo

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet.

On this Vyāsa-pūjā day we are thinking and meditating on the glories of your appearance and mission, \[wondering at the same time how it was possible that fallen souls such as ourselves could have received your guidance and protection. As we ponder, we realize it had no cause. Your mercy was causeless—another of your fathomless glories. That souls of such low caliber could be close associates of you only reflects the greatness that you alone possessed. We here at Santo Domingo are eternally indebted to you for giving us shelter under ISKCON, your life and your body. For ten years you built this institution of book distribution and *prasādam* distribution. And, of course, you gave thousands and thousands of practically subhuman living entities initiation and a chance to see the eternal sky and it's owner and controller, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. But we are so fallen that only once a year do we think to glorify you in a special way. We pray, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this Vyāsa-pūjā day, that you allow your eternal instructions to remain permanently within our memory. You said in the beginning that you tricked us into serving Kṛṣṇa. We are so happy that you tricked us that we want to cry out of gratitude. But, instead, we beg to be a part of your transcendental army of book distributors. This, you said, was the proper way to repay the spiritual master. Of course, you also said that we could never repay the debt to the spiritual master, but this service of accepting this preaching spirit was very satisfying to you. So we ask:

Make us preach,  
Make us preach,  
Make us preach, as you like.  
Make us distribute,  
Make us distribute,  
Make us distribute, as you like.

*Your eternal soldiers at ISKCON Santo Domingo,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Ūrjasvat dāsa / Raghupatiprāṇa dāsa / Amala-harināma dāsa
