## Sao Paulo

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances unto your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace. All glories to this all-merciful Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement you founded.

Throughout all these years I have been struggling to stay at your feet. So many of my Godbrothers have come, served you with the best of their sincerity, but still have gone away. But many have stayed, in times of struggle or rejoice. Yet, for all of them, be they heroes now or failures, their lives have been transformed and will never be the same. You have planted the seed of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti,* and as the foremost gardener you knew how to do it in the best way. And as a result, this plant of *bhakti* is strong, and in many cases it is fructifying, allowing your mercy to be spread even more through these wonderful disciples of yours.

As for myself, I see this miracle happening in wonder, begging to be always engaged in service at your lotus feet. I see my Godbrothers taking huge steps towards you, going back home with you. I wish I could do the same. I simply beg them not to leave me here, despite my many faults. I do not want to just bark as the caravan passes on. I want to always be with you, serving all the Vaiṣṇavas you are creating. I may be a fool, but let me be a fool before you if that keeps me beside you. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the light for this dark world, so please, direct this fallen soul. Plant this seed of devotion in my heart. Being the best gardener, you are the most expert to prepare a fertile soil.

One truth that has to be said is that your mercy is here and everywhere, to whoever comes and grabs it. But that is my real foolishness: many times I am still bewildered by the things *māyā* is offering. Please light my intelligence so that I can always be on the right path. You planted this seed in such a simple way. You are so simple, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Your Kṛṣṇa consciousness is so simple. I only want to be simple like that, and not be a sophisticated rascal. You gave simple instructions. Let me not complicate them. Your Kṛṣṇa consciousness program, which includes the four rules, sixteen rounds, *Bhāgavatam* class, and book distribution, is very simple, and it is simply wonderful. Your Kṛṣṇa consciousness world is so different from this place full of politics and rascaldom. Let this world be inundated by your simple formula. The nondevotees are amazed when they see you have such a simple solution. But what need is there to be a sophisticated dog? Let me always be your simple servant, simply engaged in whatever you want me to do.

*Your servants at ISKCON Sao Paulo; ISKCON Santos; ISKCON Ribeirao Preto; Portuguese BBT*

## Śaraṇāgati-dhāma, Canada

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

You are the dearest and most intimate servant of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, as you have spread His glorious *śrī-kṛṣṇa-saṅkīrtana* all over the world. Due to your pure, concentrated desire to serve and please the Lord and His intimate servants, you poured down mercy on our dry and shriveled planet just as a rain cloud pours torrents of rain on parched, dusty earth. Like an inexhaustible well of pure, clear water, you slaked the thirst of souls running madly in *saṁsāra* circles, chasing mirages in the desert of this material world.

As you touched your lotus feet here and there across this planet, sweet water bubbled form, and luxurious oases appeared on the cruel sands. In these oases, cool shade and succulent fruits gave full shelter to the dejected desert nomads.

As the residents of one of your verdant oases, we have to care for the sacred waters of your mercy very seriously. We have to continue to drink and bathe in this water again and again. We have to take this water and freely share it with others, lest we become misers. We have to perform the task of irrigating the lifeless desert with the water of life you have given, so the oasis will expand in all directions and not shrink from the force of the sand-bearing desert winds. We must not kick the dirt of our material attachments into this pure water, and we must uproot the weeds of desire for wealth, women, and followers (profit, adoration, distinction) that also appear.

In Śaraṇāgati, a new oasis in North America, the climate is very dry, and the land around our valley is always brown and barren. We see how quickly the grasses become green and juicy when the water from the lakes and springs is directed to the fields. We also see how quickly the grass turns brown and dry again if the flow of water is stopped. In this simple place (the nearest neighbor is five miles away), we find a renewal of purpose to drench ourselves in the cleansing shower of your instructions. All the devotees eagerly attend the morning and evening program and feel the potency and shelter of your merciful instructions. We pray to always remain in your green oasis, taking the water from your well of mercy, to finally produce the fruit of love of God, *kṛṣṇa-prema,* that you, as Lord Caitanya's chief assistant, are always offering.

*Your servants at Śaraṇāgati-dhāma,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## South France

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

As various flowers appeal to various bees,  
And many flowers to one and the same bee,  
And many bees are fond of one flower only,  
The souls and emotions have various tendencies,  
Which in you, Prabhupāda, find all their ecstasies.

O Your Divine Grace,  
We can praise you in sadness  
Or praise you in happiness  
Or even in distress.

Your songs have taught variety,  
Respect shown to the Lord almighty,  
But to our soul's content we chant freely  
The naughtiness and love affairs of Śrī Hari.

As the raga evolves along the day,  
Separation will make one cry along the way,  
And tears of compassion will melt one's heart away,  
But preaching your glories, the soul will fill with joy,  
And in your saṅkīrtana, all energies employ;  
The drops of nectar will even wet the soil.

As various flowers appeal to various bees,  
And many flowers to one and the same bee,  
And many bees are fond of one flower only,  
The souls and emotions have various tendencies,  
Which in you, Prabhupāda, find all their ecstasies.

We fear, we rejoice, we lament,  
We hope, we regret and worry,  
We cry and smile and think,  
We plan, earn, build, and spend,  
We worship, suffer, and enjoy,  
As you have shown us to do so for Kṛṣṇa.

Isn't that the way you lived, Śrīla Prabhupāda?  
Isn't that the way you live?

This is the Vaiṣṇava!  
He fights and rests,  
And eats and fasts.  
He smiles and cries.  
From rose to thunder,  
The Vaiṣṇava,  
And everything in between.

It is living raga, living music of devotion,  
The music of life sung for the pleasure of God.  
All emotions, various flowers, of various sizes,  
Colors, shapes, and smells,  
in the unlimited garden of Your Divine Grace,  
At the lotus feet of Rādhā-Śyāma.

As various flowers appeal to various bees,  
And many flowers to one and the same bee,  
And many bees are fond of one flower only,  
The souls and emotions have various tendencies,  
Which in you, Prabhupāda, find all their ecstasies.

We, the many bees, wish to be caught  
At any time in any of your flowers,  
And be enjoyed, kidnapped, and exploited;  
O Prabhupāda!  
O my lord!  
Please exploit me,  
Exploit me, exploit me  
For your unlimited pleasure.

Praying that you protect and guide us through the fully blown garden of your ISKCON movement, we offer you our most humble obeisances.

(Written by Caraṇāmbuja dāsa)

*Your wonder struck servants from the South France Yātrā,*

## Spain

Who can measure how much time  
We've wandered 'round in vain,  
Lifetimes wasted uselessly  
Earning nothing more than pain?

Never slowing in your mission  
To dispel illusion's glare,  
Countless souls you have delivered  
From māyā’s cruel snare.

One can never estimate  
The extent of our debt to you;  
Without your kind compassion  
We'd have never known what's true.

Preaching like a lion,  
Causing impersonalists' defeat,  
Never did you loosen your grasp  
Of Kṛṣṇa's lotus feet.

From the start you personally saw  
To translate, compose, and edit.  
Millions of books have now been sold  
To your transcendental credit.

Your given name from childhood  
Means "he who knows no fear. "  
The reason is because to  
Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa you're most dear.

You've come out as a victor,  
Having fought the greatest fraud.  
Mankind always will remember  
The gifts of Śrīla Prabhupāda!

(Written by Datta dāsa)

## State College, Pennsylvania

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace. So many times when I read the pastimes of the Lord, I am reminded of you and your transcendental pastimes.

As Lord Rāma enlisted the monkeys to bridge the ocean to Lanka, destroy Rāvaṇa's kingdom, and reunite the Lord with His consort, you kindly engaged us, ill-equipped as we were, to join the fight against Kali's demoniac influence, building a bridge across the ocean of Kali's faults.

Śrī Vāmanadeva appeared before Bali as an innocent *brāhmaṇa,* begging for just three steps of land, then took away all his power, reinstalling the *suras* on the throne. So also you appeared as a humble beggar in this country, but as soon as you received enough ground to put your lotus foot down, your ISKCON immediately expanded to cover the whole world. It is still expanding, and will continue to do so until it inundates the entire universe, bursts through the outer shell, and causes a Ganges-flood of nectar to flow everywhere, kicking out the asuric misleaders and ignorant speculators.

Matsya-avatāra appeared tiny at first but rapidly increased in size, surprising King Satyavrata and rescuing the *Vedas,* just as you began in an unassuming way, but then unexpectedly grew to be so large, working so hard to successfully salvage the Vedic knowledge "as it is" from the Kali Yuga hodgepodge of confusion and misdirection.

And as the cowherd boys walked fearlessly into the serpent's mouth, completely confident that their friend Kṛṣṇa would protect them, you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, without hesitation entered into the center of this most foul uncivilization, accomplished your mission, and returned home unscathed.

In all your pastimes I find parallels to Kṛṣṇa's. In fact, yours are more wonderful than those of any other incarnation, because you are giving most freely the most precious gift.

As much as I love to read your books, I think I like the *Līlāmṛta* best, because your pastimes are more important and meaningful to us than anyone's. And of all prayers and *bhajanas* by great *ācāryas* like Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura, the poems you wrote upon first arriving in America are most dear to me.

I am embarrassed to submit this to you; you know how fallen and contaminated I still am, but if you allow me to remain in your association, I know I will eventually return to my natural condition, as the sun purifies the most filthy place without itself becoming soiled.

Please allow me to remain in your service, though I am like the spider, kicking in my little grains of sand, compared with your powerful disciples and granddisciples, who are lifting huge boulders and accomplishing great feats for you. Please let me stay so that one day I will be worthy of being your humble disciple.

(Written by Trilokeśvara dāsa)

*Your servants,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Trilokeśvara dāsa

## Surfers Paradise

**Homage to Śrīla Prabhupāda**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

"Absence makes the heart grow fonder" is an expression which truly conveys our feelings toward you. Because of your purity and strength, Kṛṣṇa endowed you with the full potency to introduce a society of Vaiṣṇavas, first-class human citizens, into this rapidly degenerating godless society. This movement is continuing to spread out of continuing remembrance of you and the desire to help you fulfill your mission of spreading Lord Caitanya's *saṅkīrtana* movement.

When difficulties arise in our service to you, we take solace in reading about your pastimes in the *Līlāmṛta,* especially about the early days, when you were trying to establish Kṛṣṇa consciousness, tirelessly walking from place to place in the burning Indian sun selling your BTGs in order to buy more paper for printing; your attempts at opening centers for the League of Devotees; how you lived so austerely at the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple, walking extra miles to save bus fares; and finally all the trepidations of your journey to the West. But you always accepted all these difficulties as Kṛṣṇa's mercy. By hearing of your pastimes we can appreciate our fortunate situation, because compared to yours, our troubles are embarrassingly trivial.

By remembering you, we take heart from your exemplary activities throughout all of your life and beg your mercy to help us push on this wonderful ISKCON movement.

(Written by Vāsuśreṣṭha dāsa)

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Vasusrestha dāsa

## Sweden

This is definitely the most auspicious event,  
From Kṛṣṇa, Śrīla Prabhupāda was sent.  
And in the observance of this day,  
We remember the debt we must try to repay.  
This is no ordinary debt which we possess,  
To repay is impossible, we all confess.  
What he (Śrīla Prabhupāda) has done for us is so great,  
To Goloka Vṛndāvana he has shown us the gate.

As we endeavor in our humble manner  
To help him carry the saṅkīrtana banner  
To every town and village in every nation  
By book distribution and book publication

We find our debt increase and increase,  
A huge liability, from which there is no release.  
Thus we are caught in his network of love,  
Which no sane man would want out of.

We are unqualified, lowborn, and unfit.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda lifts us from māyā's pit.  
Such compassion, unseen throughout history,  
Rescuing the world from its endless misery.  
Now on this most celebrated occasion  
Let us review our loving obligation.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda bestows such wonderful gifts  
To the whole world, he spiritually uplifts.

Without him we wouldn't know the holy name,  
By chanting our lives have never been the same.  
It's the prime benediction for all humanity  
Which otherwise is lost in its own insanity.

Then there are his books. Oh, what a glory!  
Where else can we read the Supreme Lord's story?  
By regular reading, it cleanses the heart.  
Let us encourage everyone to start!

Śrīla Prabhupāda 's given the Deity to adorn,  
Saving us from attraction to material form,  
Regulating our lives, which were so restless,  
Showing us the way to become deathless.

Prasādam is certainly an attractive feature;  
It can be taken by any living creature.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda knows what we are needing,  
Lord Kṛṣṇa can be known simply by eating.

To the world absorbed in impersonal conception  
He takes and sets in the proper direction,  
Teaching we always keep our individuality  
And have a relation with the Supreme Personality.

His mercy is like an ocean without limit;  
Our mission is to pull everyone in it.  
Let us push this movement on and on,  
There's nothing anywhere like Prabhupāda s ISKCON.

*From the servants of Śrīla Prabhupāda in Sweden,*

## Sydney

*Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda.*

Please allow us to offer you all obeisances, falling at your feet on the ground. To invoke your mercy, we wish to offer you (fearfully) some small measure of glorification, although it will certainly remain inadequate.

As the pure representative of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa, you are perfect and complete. You are none other than the Lord's intimate associate, but you moved amongst the fallen souls of this world, bewildering us with your magical energy. Whatever you produced, such as this wonderful, "phenomenal" ISKCON, is perfectly complete in itself. There is no need to take advice from anyone else, because it is perfectly equipped as a complete whole. And although it keeps expanding, producing unlimited devotees, you will always remain in the complete balance.

Everything we have, our body, mind, and self, books, temples, and *prasādam* is controlled and owned by you. We therefore accept only what you have given us and are satisfied with that. We will not accept anything else, knowing well that anything separate from you is māyā.

We can aspire to live for hundreds of years, or even lifetimes, continuously serving you in this way, because that sort of work will increase our love and attachment for you. And there is no alternative to this way for us, because you were sent here by the Lord Himself to act as His representative.

Those who are envious of you, whoever they may be, must enter into the darkest regions of ignorance, the worlds of the faithless. They cannot attain any sort of perfection, material or spiritual. They fall down. Thus they return to the path of repeated birth and death in this material world.

Because you were fixed in the order of your glorious predecessor spiritual master, you were able to overcome all others running. No one could even dream of approaching the glorious standard you set. Although you were absorbed in translating, uninterruptedly, books to be used for the next ten thousand years, still you controlled everything within ISKCON to the minutest degree, as you do even now. You surpassed all in excellence.

You allowed us to walk with you, but now in your physical form you have gone. Although at present you are personally serving the Lord in His own abode and seem to be very far away, you are very near and dear to us still. We see you within everything, and everything within you, for without you there is nothing.

We see everything in relation to you, and as your disciples and grand-disciples, we are all part and parcel of you. Therefore we will not allow disagreements to interfere with our cooperating among ourselves for your pleasure.

One who always sees all living entities as prospective recipients of your mercy, knowing that by the mercy of the Lord one gets you, and by your mercy, one gets Kṛṣṇa, and who thus sees your mercy as nondifferent from the Lord's, becomes a true knower of things. What then is there as illusion or anxiety for him?

Such a devotee must know in fact that you are the greatest of all devotees, irreproachable, pure, and uncontaminated, but perfectly able to extend the mercy of the Lord even to the most insignificant creature, thus fulfilling their desire, since time immemorial, to love Kṛṣṇa.

It is said that one result is obtained by having implicit faith in the *guru* and that another is obtained by devīating from his order, even fractionally. All this has been explained by previous authorities who clearly understood it.

One should perfectly acquaint himself with your transcendental qualities, activities, and attachment for Kṛṣṇa, for by knowing these, one surpasses death and thus enjoys an eternal life of bliss and knowledge.

O lord, sustainer of our lives, your real identity is covered by your humility. By hearing and discussing your wonderful qualities, however, we become enthused by newer and newer glimpses of your eternal personality.

Let this temporary body be burned to ashes, and let the air of life be merged with the totality of air. Now, O lord, remember that we are eternally related to you in service, and because you are the mercy incarnation of the Lord, please do not forget us. Please lead us on the right path to reach you, and free us from our sinful desires, so that there will be no hindrance to our progress.

Śrīla Prabhupāda *ki jaya!*

*Your loving disciples and granddisciples at ISKCON Sydney.*

## Taiwan

**Unlimited**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Your love for Śrī Kṛṣṇa is unlimited.  
The demigods are chanting your glories, which are unlimited.  
Even the Buddha would acknowledge that your mercy is unlimited.  
This world has never seen the likes of your purity, which is unlimited.  
Only now are we realizing that your exalted Kṛṣṇa consciousness is unlimited.  
Year after year we are seeing that your devotional service is unlimited.  
We know that the hardships you overcame were practically unlimited,  
And it seems that our difficulties in serving you are unlimited.  
The spiritual rewards for facing those problems are said to be unlimited,  
While the dire consequences of neglecting your instructions are certainly unlimited.  
The pretenders who will not help your movement criticize without limit;  
Their cheap talk and promises of service are heard almost unlimitedly.  
They speak fine philosophies to justify their deviance and knavery,  
But a man who will not remember, love, and serve his spiritual father is actually only a beast.

*Begging to remain your children and servants of ISKCON Taiwan,*

## Tampa

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*  
Please accept our most humble obeisances.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

A most intense presence you manifest  
Māyā's illusion you contest  
Delivering Śrī Kṛṣṇa for all to rest  
Upon His lotus feet—protection's best.

Always conscious of others' plight  
Your kindness guides us from Kali's night  
In the night of quarrel where wrong is right  
Your presence and preaching brings Kṛṣṇa's light.

O Prabhupāda, our divine master and guide,  
In whom Lord Kṛṣṇa does personally confide  
Please force us to stand on your victorious side  
Apart from illusion and Māyā’s high tide.

Allow us to serve you and preach Kṛṣṇa's name  
Without change, as you have shown us exactly the same  
Our disease of change for name and fame  
Makes knowledge spoiled—O master make us tame.

Founder-ācārya—guru of gurus  
Heavy with knowledge—transcendent news  
Traversing towns and villages in your book-jacket shoes  
Living in your books, bound never to lose.

We are fallen and foolish; what can we say?  
A drop to your ocean—we cannot repay  
For your gifts an unlimited array  
In humbleness we will try day after day.

Distributing your mercy (books) pleases the most  
Your Divine Grace has made this a public post  
So raise your cups high in transcendental toast  
Distribute his books—from coast to coast.

Jaya Prabhupāda!

Victory to your ISKCON!

Please shower your mercy on Tampa, Florida.

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Vivasvān dāsa

## Tehran

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda! You are our eternal savior, and we are indebted to your divine mercy. We are lost in the world of *māyā,* and there is no hope for us to save ourselves. You are our only hope. You have the power to save us. Please do so; we cannot help ourselves. For us to be at your lotus feet, we must be qualified. But we are in a helpless condition. Please give us the taste of your lotus feet so we can give up the false shelter of profit, adoration, and distinction. You have given us Kṛṣṇa through your books, but without you and your contact, we have no appreciation for Kṛṣṇa. To see that Kṛṣṇa is yours, we are joyful; but if we think we have Kṛṣṇa and Kṛṣṇa is ours, we become proud and miserable. If the child accepts his position in relation to his father, he is happy; if he does not accept his subordinate position and tries to imitate his father, he will suffer. It is not difficult to learn the philosophy, but it is very difficult to learn the art of relationship. We cannot make the attempt to have the necessary relationship with you, but please be kind and help us to establish what we need and lack.

*Your eternal servants,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Tokyo

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Glorifying your good qualities is the tongue's real activity, for in the material world it is very difficult to find a pure devotee of the Lord.

Jaya Prabhupāda! It is only by your inconceivable mercy that we are able to continue in devotional service. So please, do not reject us or cut us off. Without your mercy, what hope is there for us?

You are Kṛṣṇa's pure devotee. You came into this world to start this great ISKCON society. We are eternally indebted to you. Now the only purpose of our existence is to continue spreading this movement all over the world, by the distribution of your transcendental books. This is our life.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, let us remain always completely absorbed in the printing and distribution of your books, not only in this life, but birth after birth. Jaya Prabhupāda!

*Your tiny servants,*

## Topanga

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**We Will Never Forget**

We will never forget who prayed to Kṛṣṇa  
to convince the Westerners about His message  
and make him a success or failure, as He liked.

We will never forget who arrived on the shores of America  
with hardly a penny in his pocket,  
not knowing whether to turn left or right off the dock.

We will never forget who sat in Tompkins Square Park  
chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa mahā-mantra  
and fed hippies and bums bhagavat-prasādam.

We will never forget who had his manuscript and typewriter stolen  
as he embarked upon his preaching mission  
and wrote it all over again.

We will never forget who authored the articles  
of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness  
and refused the suggestion to say "God" instead of "Kṛṣṇa. "

We will never forget who wrote and published  
Back to Godhead in India, America, the world,  
and organized the mass distribution of transcendental literatures.

We will never forget who translated while everyone was sleeping  
and greeted us with gifts of spiritual knowledge  
every day embellishing the unlimited fathoms of the Absolute Truth.

We will never forget who revealed the Deity form  
to the blank stares of the candalas  
and taught his disciples the highest standards of worship.

We will never forget how he walked regally,  
head up, broad stride, with great dignity,  
followed by a long line of his disciples eager to catch the nectar.

We will never forget who circled the world  
over and over again while in his seventies—  
yet who always carried his home of Vaikuṇṭha with him.

We will never forget his grave expressions,  
warning all humanity of its precarious condition,  
chastising his disciples for taking matters lightly.

We will never forget his otherworldly voice  
singing praises of Kṛṣṇa and his devotees,  
saturating the air with vibrations of loving exchanges.

We will never forget the smile on his face  
when he opened the pages of his Kṛṣṇa book  
and read the pages aloud with great satisfaction.

We will never forget your chanting, your dancing,  
your eating, your gestures, your teachings, your examples,  
your sacrifices, your friendship, your dedication, your purity, your love.

We will never forget that you are a pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa  
who has opened our eyes, blinded by the darkness of ignorance,  
with the torchlight of knowledge.

We will never forget. . . because you have given us so much to remember you by:  
You've given us Kṛṣṇa.  
There is no gift that we can give you  
that can be compared with yours.  
You've given us your mercy.  
There is no greater friendship  
that can be offered to the spirit soul.

You've given us a chance  
of millions of lifetimes.

On this, your appearance-day celebration, we simply pray that you will accept whatever service we can humbly submit.  
It is nothing, but by your divine grace it is accepted as something wonderful.

*Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for everything. We beg to remain,*

*Your servants,*  
The Pyramid Center for Bhakti-yoga,  
Topanga, California,

Nṛsiṁhānanda dāsa / Puruṣottama dāsa / Śacīdulal dāsa / Bhakta Philip / Bhakta Tom / Mohana-devī dāsī / Revatī-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Cindy / Bhaktin Christina / Jīvānanda dāsa

## Toronto

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Although we are unqualified to be able to express any genuine sentiment for you, by practicing to glorify the spiritual master, we can gradually qualify ourselves to pay you homage. In the thousands of letters you sent to you disciples over the years, you took every opportunity to glorify their service. By digging into the treasure house of your letters, we can reflect on the importance of your mission to represent Śrīla Vyāsadeva here in Toronto, New Remuṇā Dhāma, and the Canadian zone.

To our first G.B.C. in Canada, Jagadīśa Goswami, February 5, 1972, you glorified those who were carrying out your orders to preach: "I am very pleased to hear that you are putting your full energy into distributing books and magazines very widely all over Canada. It is not so much numbers of books sold, it is the sincerity which Kṛṣṇa appreciates, and 1 am encouraged by your determined enthusiasm to increase in every respect. In traveling *saṅkīrtana* and preaching in the schools and colleges which you are organizing is excellent proposal, and I think it is the best program for pushing on this Lord Caitanya's movement: simply traveling, preaching, and infiltrating the schools where young boys and girls are interested to learn something that will satisfy them. In both activities you may distribute many of our literatures and that will please me very much."

It was due to your prayers that Lord Kṛṣṇa reciprocated and awarded us our present temple, where devotees continue to take shelter. In a letter to Viśvakarmā dāsa, January 10, 1976, you congratulated him after empowering him to purchase the temple building. "Yes, it is the mercy of Kṛṣṇa that we have got such an important building in Toronto. It is topmost triumph. If you can fix up a neon sign always moving with the *mahā-mantra.* Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare, that will be a great achievement. The sign can be on from evening until midnight. That will be the proper utilization for such an ideal building and location. Christianity is now declining; therefore they are having to sell all their churches to us. Because they have got no clear idea of God people are not satisfied with them. But we can explain what God is, so people are appreciating more and more our movement. Now by Kṛṣṇa's grace you have a beautiful temple. Use it to preach very vigorously. If you all very strictly follow the principles and chant sixteen rounds, your spiritual strength will be insured and our movement will become increasingly prominent."

It has been eight years now since ISKCON increased prominently and since you last walked among your disciples in your physical form. Those of us who by your mercy have been able to survive *māyā's* onslaughts have been able to continue to find solace in the association of your spiritual form. Since your birth, your physical form was always spiritual. There was never a time when you were not fully conscious of your eternal relationship with Lord Kṛṣṇa.

For those of us who have the purified eyes to see and ears to hear, the causeless mercy of your spiritual form is manifested through your *mūrti,* Bhaktivedanta purports, music, lecture tape cassettes, videos, *gurukulas,* farm communities, archives, temples, museums, restaurants, preaching centers, and a myriad of preaching programs too numerous to mention here. In other words, by your personal example and instruction you are the embodiment of preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

But above all, what helps many of us most in remembering you is to associate with your dedicated disciples who have taken your spiritual strength and humility to remain engaged in your ISKCON, despite whatever discrepancies there may be amongst us. Your disciples are the living proof of your mercy, because it is all that we are made of. For this reason we should offer all respects to our Godbrothers and Godsisters who continue to be nourished and sustained by you. We beg you to permit us to always remain in their association and allow us to remain qualified to appreciate it by giving us the sincerity and intelligence to stop committing offenses against you.

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Trinidad

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances on this most auspicious day. My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, my lord and master, because of illusion we experience difficulty in serving you in separation, which is the most important means of going back to Godhead. Arjuna became helpless after the disappearance of the Lord. But this disappearance can be likened to the setting of the sun. The setting of the sun does not mean the end of the sun, it means that the sun is out of our sight. Similarly, the end of your mission on this planet only means that you are out of our sight. Arjuna then drove away that grief and anxiety completely from his heart by remembering the song sung by the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the instructions of *Bhagavad-gītā.* In the same way, we can be free from illusion by always meditating on and executing your divine instructions.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is so difficult to understand your personality, to know who you are. I can realize that you are a pure devotee of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, but your preaching activities are still bewildering. You wrote in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (1.15.13purport), "The Lord is all-powerful, and by His grace His pure devotee can be as powerful as He may desire, and there is no limit to it." Your preaching potency is unlimited. There is no record of any personality who has done what you are doing. You are creating so many pure devotees from all walks of life on this earth planet, without any discrimination. Kṛṣṇa says in *Bhagavad-gītā* that the most dear devotee to Him is he who preaches His glories. Your dearness to Kṛṣṇa is unfathomable; it is impossible to comprehend how dear you are to the Lord.

You have spread the glories of the Lord all over this planet, even to the people of this Western world, who never knew anything of the Supreme Personality of Godhead or spiritual life. You have given the opportunity to perform the highest activities, which are desired even by the demigods.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, so many of your disciples are preaching and managing so nicely all over the world and keeping so many wonderful programs which you desire, like Rathayātrā, etc. I'm totally unqualified to do anything properly. Mahārāja Ambarīṣa said that even if one can do one of the nine processes of devotional service, he can become perfect. But I'm not even qualified. Thus I beg you with whatever sincerity and seriousness is in my heart—and which I'm desiring to increase by your mercy—to please let my mind, my body, and my words be interlocked in the service of assisting you in distributing these literatures. I want nothing again in my life but to remain the servant of this book distribution. I know the whole world will be benefited by this book distribution. The light of the sun in this material world will be darkened by eternal time, but the results or benefits of your books are the eternal light. It is like the light in Godhead itself. People will be benefited for thousands and thousands of years by this book distribution if they read one sentence of your writing. If not in this life, the next life they will definitely become devotees of your lotus feet. These books will change the hearts of even steel-hearted men. It will definitely change the people of this world. We simply have to become determined and enthusiastic and patient and steady in spreading your glories and the glories of Lord Kṛṣṇa through this book distribution. I beg to remain eternally assisting you in distributing books,

(Written by Tārkṣya dāsa)

*Your unworthy servants at ISKCON Trinidad,*

**Disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

## Vancouver

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*  
Please accept our *daṇḍavat* obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Your exalted position as the self-effulgent *ācārya* who appeared to take the message of Godhead all over the planet is unique and exceptional. Spilling the *bhakti* revolution over the shores of India, inundating the world with love of God, you are the topmost jewel in the trinity of Bhaktivinoda, Bhaktisiddhānta, and Bhaktivedanta. Your stature as an exalted saint and perfect Vaiṣṇava exhibiting the full ornaments of devotional qualities cannot and should not be imitated. Your swanlike qualities were perfectly apparent even to the nondevotees. Even the speculators have joined together to praise your monumental gift of spiritual literatures.

There is no one who can compare with you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Compared to you, even the best of your disciples are like tiny gemstones surrounding a massive diamond of perfect clarity and priceless value. This will always remain the case, now and in future generations.

As we approach the five-hundredth anniversary of the appearance of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, our immense responsibility to you becomes more clear. We must carry on your mission with all intelligence and dedication. You have built the ship and set the course across the ocean of material misery. Although such a great journey will not be without storms, and the ship may pitch and roll (even making a few seasick), the ship is very strong, the navigational charts are without error, and you, the captain, know every trick the waves and wind may create. You have invited the whole world to embark on this adventuresome journey, and this special ship can expand unlimitedly to take everyone across. Our duty is to be courageous, loyal, and obedient crew members to assist you. You will always remain the captain of the ISKCON ship, and no one can ever replace you. Your crew members may trim the sails, even take the wheel, but you are always the captain, and the successful crossing simply depends on our fully adhering to your orders and directions.

You have instructed us that the mood of the crew members must be one of love and trust. It must be a unified effort. You have instructed us that our love for you will be demonstrated by how we cooperate to serve you. We feel that you singled out this test of love because its fulfillment is the most essential ingredient for the health and strength of your beloved ISKCON. To actually cooperate together means that we must deeply aspire for the humility, tolerance, and respect for others that is exalted by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu Himself in the *śikṣ*ā*ṣṭaka* prayers. This attempt must go on at all levels and most especially must go on at the level of the leaders or more prominent members of ISKCON. Love and trust do not mean, for example, that I should demand that the devotees love me and trust me; it means that I must love and trust my Godbrothers and that I must aspire to see them as saints, while seeing myself as the most unworthy. This principle is the extraordinary attitude of the purified Vaiṣṇava, and it must become the operating principle of ISKCON, exemplified by the highest leaders, or ISKCON's potential to flood the whole world with the mercy of *śrī kṛṣṇa-saṅkīrtana* will be greatly hampered.

This is a most significant year. It is not only the five-hundredth anniversary of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu's appearance, but it is the year in which ISKCON will attempt to formulate a constitution that will guide and direct devotees for generations.

In this year we hope we can offer you the most precious offering: an ISKCON strong in cooperative fraternal spirit, free from sectarianism, egotism and strife, that will, sticking exactly to the directions and principles that you have given us, enshrine this Vaiṣṇava spirit in a just and wise constitution. This will be the most perfect Vyāsa-pūjā offering to your lotus feet.

*Your servants in Vancouver,*

## Vienna

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupada,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. We wish to keep your mission as the very sustenance of our life, the remembrance of your instructions as the dearmost and most intimate object of worship. We do not know who is Kṛṣṇa. We only know what you have taught us about Him, and only through these instructions can we someday have some sort of realization of the Absolute Truth Bhagavān Śrī Kṛṣṇa. You have, without our knowledge and awareness, forcibly dragged us out of the desert of useless material existence and engaged us in the pastimes of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. These pastimes, the spreading of the *saṅkīrtana* movement around the world, are so sweet and filled with immortal nectar, but foolish and dull-headed as we are, we have no attraction for them. Especially the spreading of Kṛṣṇa consciousness through the distribution of transcendental literatures, which you have so kindly manifested, brought from the spiritual world, is not at all attracting us. Foolish and fallen as we are, we instead work hard to earn money by buying and selling things on the market of *māyā,* thus entangling ourselves more and more in the anxieties of material existence. By your unlimited mercy you are creating situations through which we can see the absurdity of our endeavor to avoid the process of surrendering to your divine *vāṇī.* Once a reporter asked you how you were supporting your movement, and you replied that Kṛṣṇa has given us these books, and only by selling these transcendental literatures is our whole movement being maintained. And this, we believe, is an eternal fact. It is not that only during your "physical" presence our movement could be supported only by selling your transcendental literatures; it is always a fact. "Books are the basis"—how deep and axiomatic is this statement.

We pray, therefore, on this auspicious day of your appearance, that you give us the conviction and appreciation of the significance of your books, so that we can be picked up by them from the dangerous ocean of faults, which Kali-yuga is, and placed as atoms at the lotus feet of the book production and book distribution mission.

*Your servants and disciples in 1SKCON Austria,*

Rāma-mohana dāsa / Hanumān dāsa / Jaya Gurudeva dāsa / Puruṣottama dāsa / Varāhadeva dāsa / Ananta-rūpa dāsa / Mathurā-mohana dāsa / Sādhusaṅgānanda dāsa / Haribolānanda dāsa / Tattva-sāgara dāsa / Oṁkāra dāsa / Jaṭāyu dāsa / Vivasvān dāsa / Kṛṣṇa-sūrya dāsa / Vilāsa-vigraha dāsa / Bhakta Gerald / Bhakta Klaus / Bhakta Sasa / Bhakta Branco / Bhakta Markus / Conny / Prema-bhakti-devī dāsī / Rādhā-sevā-devī dāsī / Vṛndāvanī-devī dāsī / Kṛṣṇā-devī dāsī / Cintāmaṇi-devī dāsī / Sarasvatī-devī dāsī / Urjā-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Hana / Kṛṣṇa das / Balarāma / Nṛsiṁha

## Vṛndāvana

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. During a discussion of the verse "As they surrender to Me . . ." you said that we have to know how to take from Kṛṣṇa. It is a well-known fact, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that everything you say or do at any time of the day, that word or activity is always one of *giving.* Your every breath gives the life of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the whole world and satisfies the deepest needs of the soul. And yet, even though you are a veritable human desire tree, you spoke of "how to *take* from Kṛṣṇa." You went on to give the example of when you were about to embark for America. First you came to Māyāpur to take the *darśana* of Śrī-Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and your spiritual master. At that time you had only forty rupees, and even though you were taking the blessings of your Lords for your journey, you didn't even know where the money for your ticket was coming from. A few years later, you returned to that same Māyāpur with forty crores of rupees. In this way, you taught us that unconditional surrender is how to take the mercy of Kṛṣṇa.

Here in your eternal home of Vṛndāvana Dhāma, the devotees are seeing how your legend is only increasing with the passing of time. There is no doubt that you will soon be recognized as India's greatest saint of the century. We feel very proud to be your disciples and to be responsible for maintaining the personal home of such an important dignitary as yourself. Living in Vṛndāvana requires a special sort of surrender, but just as you taught us how to take from Kṛṣṇa, so we want to take from you. We want to maintain your home in such a royal fashion that the hundreds of thousands of visitors who come to see you will be suitably impressed. We also want them to know that the disciples of the great Śrīla Prabhupāda are worthy of that eternal distinction. In this way, your legend will live rather than become mere history; in this way we hope to always feel your presence; and in this way we hope to take from you, just as you have taken from Kṛṣṇa.

Sincerely yours,

*The inmates of Vṛndāvana Dhāma,*

## Washington, D.C.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Sitting here in Vṛndāvana Dhāma I am getting many realizations. Not realizations as you experienced here—of detachment from family and friends, from the world of names—but realizations of my own deficiencies. Coming before you here in your eternal home at the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma is painful for me, because I have nothing substantial to offer. You have mercifully given all the ingredients I need to offer you some nice service, but I return to you emptyhanded.

When I remember your example—how you served your spiritual master—I am simultaneously inspired and frightened. How long will I continue to ignore your desires and your example? That is my fear. You did everything on the order of your spiritual master. There was no organization with men and money to help you. You did it all yourself by the strength of your devotion to Guru and Kṛṣṇa. I am frightened that I will never do anything. I have such a long way to go, and life is so short. Although it is short, I nevertheless waste it trying to make so many arrangements in the name of facilitating my so-called advancement. But what is advancement if not the dedication of one's full energy to the service of the spiritual master? It does not have to take one hundred years. You did it in one second, unhesitatingly. But I cower. I'll stand on the edge for a million years rather than make the leap of full surrender to your instructions.

Unfortunately, I still think I'm so intelligent. 1 can bluff a few neophytes. Saffron cloth hides my clouded heart. I actually think I'm doing all right. "Never mind the weeds, they're so small," I think.

But when 1 read your books the charade is over. At least I can't cheat myself. Reality is described there on every page, piercing, penetrating, enlightening even one so covered as I. What a contribution you have made! Like a *yogī* not even mentioned in the Vedic age you have expanded yourself into millions of forms and entered the homes and hearts of all classes of men. Like Kṛṣṇa in Dvārakā, you simultaneously perform your divine *līlā* in the hearts of millions of fallen souls. In one heart chiseling, in one heart soothing, in one heart blasting, in one heart weeding. This is your inconceivable potency and your unbounded mercy. You are present in your books, and you are allowing anyone, even one with no previous qualification, to begin a relationship with you. I was also given that opportunity. In the beginning I took to the relationship with great enthusiasm. Associating with you through your books was a joy I had never before imagined. For hours daily I would sit and hear from you. It was a transcendental experience. As a mundaner slips into even more illusory worlds by reading his rubbish literature, I was transported to the spiritual world on the sweet words of your translations and purports. It was an incredible experience, one that laid the foundation for my further advancement in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Unfortunately, I have developed bad habits. I have not adequately developed my relationship with you by reading your books diligently and steadily over the years. I have developed an attitude that there is nothing new to learn there, that I have to look elsewhere to find fresh ideas. But what do I really know of your books? At least here in Vṛndāvana *that* I am beginning to understand. For a few days I have tried to absorb myself in your glorious *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* It has been wonderful. Once again I am amazed by your scholarship, your devotion, and the apparent ease with which you glorify Kṛṣṇa page after page. If one does not look closely it may appear that it is all simply repetitious. But that is not true. On closer inspection one will find the unlimited variety that is the nature of the spiritual realm. The subject matter is limited not by any lack of news but only by our limited desire to hear.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please let me approach your books in a mood of reverence and humility, as well as an intense eagerness to receive instructions from you. Please enter my heart through your purports and root out every last weed hidden there. Otherwise, I will never be able to offer any valuable service to your lotus feet, and my life will be wasted.

(Written by Nāgarāja dāsa)

*Your servants,*

## Wellington

**"We offer our respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism."**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.*

You are the savior of the world, the universal shelter, the embodiment of purity. You are a transcendental person, greatest amongst the great. All the great devotees declare this of you; they praise you; they love you. \[O lord, the truths that you have spoken are accepted by great souls and are relevant to all.

O master, the depth of your glories is unknown, but a few know something of your wondrous qualities. On this auspicious day we hear details of the divine opulences by which you pervade these worlds.

O Prabhupāda, O great mystic, we are never satiated in hearing about you; in fact, the more we hear, the more we want to be with you.

Dear lord, please continue to bless us. You are everything to us; we cannot exist without you.

O mighty conqueror of illusions, we fall down to offer you our respectful obeisances and ask you mercy. We beg you to tolerate the wrongs we may have done.

Dear master, your transcendental form is difficult to behold and can be appreciated only by submissive service at your lotus feet. Please, therefore, allow us to live in your house and to serve you in any menial way. O master, O lord, all glories unto you!

(Written by Subala dāsa)

*Your servants in Wellington,*

**Granddisciples**

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from the Publishers

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*tvaṁ naḥ sandarśito dhātrā*  
*dustaraṁ nistitīrṣatām*  
*kaliṁ sattva-haraṁ puṁsāṁ*  
*karṇa-dhāra ivārṇavam*  
*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 1.1.22*

**"We think that we have met Your Goodness by the will of providence, and thus we accept you as captain of the ship for those who desire to cross the dangerous ocean of Kali, which destroys all the good qualities of a human being."**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

On this occasion of our celebration of your appearance amongst us, we find that our primary contemplations are those centered upon the unparalleled humility embodied in your transcendental moods of devotion to your Guru Mahārāja, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī. While you are the most important person in our lives, and while you are also the manifest loving mercy of God appearing solely for our sakes, and while you are also *jagad-guru* in this Kali-riddled world, still we also know, by Your Grace's having revealed to us the logical / illogical wonders of divine truth *(acintya-bheda-abheda-tattva),* that your opinion, as revealed and stated by you, regarding your own personal qualifications is a singularly necessary key which we must use if we are to be of any true service to your mission and, indeed, if we are to be spared from the turbulent ocean of Kali . . .

"... Personally, I have no qualifications whatsoever for accepting disciples and directing so many activities, but my Guru Mahārāja has asked me to come, and I have come, and, by his grace, so many things are now going on nicely."

"... My only qualification is that my Guru Mahārāja has sent me and that therefore I came. So now I am just arranging with the help of you boys and girls for the fulfillment of the desires of Guru Mahārāja."

In these statements and in your living example, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we find true renunciation shining with absolute and inexorable brilliance, without diminution from even the slightest mote of self-interest, of spiritual pride.

Therefore, for ourselves, we are simply praying that we can always be thoroughly absorbed in the mood and mission of you, our Guru Mahārāja . . . you have shown us how. Only by such transcendent absorption are we able to avoid the abode of the venomous serpent of spiritual pride, whose at-first-subtle bites cause one to contemplate himself as being possessed of so many good qualifications and of excellent intuitive abilities as regards scriptural interpretations of such questions as "What is spiritual advancement and what is not?" "Who (else) is spiritually qualified and who is not?" "What is preaching and what is not?" etc., etc. One who, due to not being absorbed in *guru-sevā,* fails to endeavor to slay this snake of false pride with the twin sword of transcendental knowledge and genuine renunciation soon finds himself distanced from both his peers and any worthwhile followers alike, and he is thereupon very likely to further endanger his spiritual future by manifesting envy and its ensuing anger.

We worship you as *jagad-guru* and give our lives to the service of your mission, confident that by your grace we are enabled to be ever mindful that, per your exemplary relationship with your Guru Mahārāja and his mission, whatever seemingly "personal" qualifications which we appear to have brought to or which manifest amidst our worship / service of you and your mission are simply ornaments which flourish and are exalted with ourselves by devotional usage but which are also tarnishable and can become leaden should we, amidst such exaltations, forget what we're about,. . . should we forget that which is the only qualification to which we can lay valid claim: personal determination to serve you *on your terms.* To ascribe to ourselves within our hearts any other "spiritual" qualifications is great folly and cannot, by constitution, lead to any positive spiritual result . . .

(Written by Mahendra dāsa)

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from the Sannyāsīs

## Agrāṇī Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

I have read that it is impossible to calculate the extent of one's debt to his *guru.* This I believe very sincerely. Although I am just in the childhood of my spiritual life, I feel completely indebted to you. In addition, as I pass each day relishing every facet of the wonderful new life of Kṛṣṇa consciousness that you've given me, I become increasingly more indebted. I cannot pretend to know much about *rasa* or advanced devotional concepts, but I do know, as much as I could know anything, that you have saved me from the dark hole of material existence and initiated me into the ecstatic realm of devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa. In my previous unhappy condition such beauty and bliss were unimaginable. Now, each new day I meet with hopeful enthusiasm in the further cultivation of my sprouting devotional creeper. As every feature of devotional life grows blissfully more illuminating, I find greater attraction to meticulously executing even the smallest of its details. I love so much just being a devotee: rising early to chant the holy name, attending *maṅgala-ārati,* and reading your books. I love associating with your devotees and working cooperatively within this great institution (ISKCON) that you have so painstakingly created for the salvation of the whole world in pursuance of Lord Caitanya's desire. I love preaching, proclaiming your glories to the world, distributing your mercy to the sincere, and cutting down the fools and rascals with the powerful arguments of *Bhāgavata* philosophy that I have learned from you. And, most of all, I love serving you and seeing you pleased in all respects. Knowing within my heart that you have accepted my service and that it has pleased you (although it is insignificant) is your transcendental reciprocation and my greatest pleasure. Out of my deepest gratitude for the kindness you've shown me I strive only to give myself completely in your service.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, within the garden of my heart grow weeds of all description. These weeds, which represent material desires, are deep-rooted and very stubborn. Yet, growing powerfully in their midst is the emerging creeper of attachment to your lotus feet. As I grow more aware of the unprecedented mercy you have bestowed upon this miserable world in this God-forsaken age, attachment to you grows endlessly more and overwhelms me with love for you, my spiritual master. By force of this love, though yet immature, I seek to please you with my every thought, word, and action. I pray always to be given the strength and determination to become pure in Kṛṣṇa consciousness so that the tender creeper of my devotional service, not being hampered, may grow strong and quickly bear the fruit of unmotivated, uninterrupted love for Your Divine Grace. Admitting that I am a very ordinary disciple with no exceptional talents or qualifications, I pray that out of your causeless mercy you accept me into the confidential circle of your pure devotees (although I am most unworthy). My sole aspiration is to become,

*Your servant eternally,*  
Agrāṇī Swami

## Bhaktimārga Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.*

Please offer your merciful blessings and accept my *daṇḍavatas.*

The mundane man cannot begin to understand the feelings of regret and remorse that the disciple expresses before the *guru.* Lord Caitanya felt fallen when His *guru,* Īśvara Purī, labeled Him as a fool, and when Kṛṣṇa reprimanded Arjuna, the Lord's disciple, Arjuna, felt mortified. The guilt complex that the student experiences before the spiritual master is an expression of genuine devotional sentiment.

In my own personal relationship as a disciple of Your Divine Self, I cannot find a trace of this genuine guilt, having not stepped beyond the formality of *dīkṣā.* Although you did not personally speak to me when you were amongst us, had the opportunity arisen, you likely would have complimented me, because words of praise are effective on those who need the most encouragement. In the case of Mahāprabhu and Arjuna, who were advanced, criticism became the effective tool. But then, it is simply ludicrous for me to compare myself to these personalities.

Many persons in the past have had the golden opportunity to meet Your Divine Grace, and all have had to make the choice of either embracing your virtuous instructions or to take to the defiant ways of the false ego. Unfortunately, many have surrendered to the latter. According to the *Gītā, manuṣy*āṇā*ṁ sahasreṣu kaścid yatati siddhaye / yatatām api siddhānāṁ kaścin māṁ vetti tattvataḥ.* Those who make the righteous choice are few. The majority of people are caught in a wild pursuit for the "will o' the wisp" offered by the material nature, and even many of those who were your personal servants, tending your needs, bypassed your good instructions and grasped for the myriad mirages of *māyā.*

In 1975 in Detroit you delivered a *Bhāgavatam* lecture, and I recall that you concluded by emphatically stating, "Do not be proud." Such a statement is profound and refreshing to hear in the dark Kali-yuga period that we live in. Such impressionable words, and still I'm devoid of any spiritual integrity!

Therefore, the only explanation that accounts for the fact that I am maintaining any level of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, considering that all odds are against me and the chances for some success are so slim, is your causeless mercy upon me.

Please continue to be merciful to me. There is nothing but your mercy that can sustain me.

*Your fallen servant,*  
Bhaktimārga Swami

## Bhakty-abhaya-caraṇa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet, where the entire universe can find shelter and protection.

The effect of your being present here was that everything was easy and pleasant. Your ability to resolve conflicts made us think that there were no conflicts. Your intelligent guidance maintained the confidence of all your followers. It caused us to think that we were very qualified, when in reality we were being moved by your divine impulse.

We were born into spiritual life carefully nursed and protected by you. Now we are experiencing how difficult it is to resolve every small problem, being separated from your personal presence.

Every year we are gathering in Māyāpur to celebrate and attend intense meetings, as you wanted. In this way we can unify our efforts to increase and improve the preaching work all around the world, guided by the eternal torchlight of your books.

Our only desire is to please you, even though at times that becomes mixed with personal interests. Nevertheless, by your unlimited mercy, clear ideas quickly arise regarding how to continue expanding with determination your teachings to all the conditioned souls.

Working cooperatively, we are understanding the greatness of your mission. Our desire is to establish a solid foundation for the coming generations. By your divine grace many literatures, in all languages, are being distributed in great quantities, and more temples are being built around the world, spreading the atmosphere of Vṛndāvana everywhere.

Millions of persons already know you, and we, your disciples, want you to reign in their hearts. You are the unquestionable leader for us and for the future generations as well. You are the immaculate swan, illuminating the path for the confused souls in this complicated era. The world is just waiting for some solution to its desperate situation. Humanity can be saved from mass suicide by your books, which are not equalled anywhere.

My greatest desire is that you remain in my heart, reigning in opulence, so that everyone will become fixed on you when I speak.

I want to keep hearing the *mṛdaṅga* of your books in order to fill my ears with transcendental emotion. Your words awaken me upon taking your *darśana,* your gestures captivate me, and your silence speaks to me. Allow me to be surrendered to your will, for that represents complete freedom. Nothing would be possible for this poor soul without your divine grace.

I will always learn from you, and I will always serve you. Being dependent on you is our real pleasure. To follow your instructions assures us of success.

When I used to make deities in Vṛndāvana, you would instruct me, and everything was going nicely. But once you gave me a lesson I'll never forget. In your house in Vṛndāvana at the end of an unforgettable interview, you approved the drawings of the proposed Deity of Kāliya-Kṛṣṇa I wanted to make. Nevertheless, I later found in the market some other posters, thinking to draw ideas from them, and I presented them before you for your comments. You fixed your sight on me so severely, and with the fury of a lion, shouted, "I told you what to do!" This has made a lifelong impression upon me concerning the significance *of guru.* Before me was the greatest *ācārya* of all times, the pure devotee who knows every detail about Kṛṣṇa, and in spite of that, I was going to the market to get ideas! How foolish and illusioned I was!

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for relieving my anxiety and letting me serve your lotus feet for one more year, despite my being so foolish and incompetent.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Bhakty-abhaya-carana Swami

## Bhaktyānanda-vidya Goswami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

You are everything to me. Please accept my humble obeisances.

Your divine lotus feet are the shelter of the fallen,  
The only hope for happiness and peace.

Just like Kṛṣṇa is one and different, you are the same as Sahkīrtana;  
You did not come to pay your karma, but to free us all from hell.

Your land is Śrī Goloka, the highest of abodes,  
From whence Lord Caitanya brought down His love.

Like all other avatāras, your coming was predicted;  
Great saints and ācāryas had proclaimed your deeds.

Though you said nothing is impossible, your movement amazed the world,  
And for the rest of eternity your glories I will sing.

The greatest incarnation is Caitanya Mahāprabhu;  
You are the embodiment of His teachings, Prabhupāda mahā-guru.

As the greatest of all sounds, we sing the mahā-mantra;  
In your bhajanas and instructions we hear the mahā-siddhānta.

Just as Viṣṇu spoke the Vedas and Vyāsa wrote them down,  
Your books are the teachings of Gaurāṅga's saṅkīrtana.

There is only one Kṛṣṇa, one golden Lord Caitanya;  
There is one yuga-dharma, and no one equals you, Prabhupāda.

Your movement and structure are not just a concoction,  
But for humanity at large the greatest benediction.

ISKCON will withstand the greatest of all storms,  
Although this age of Kali is a vast ocean of faults.

From inside and outside we have been attacked;  
You taught us how to fight and not be surprised.

For us to win is easy, your mission being unconquerable;  
We simply have to follow your footsteps with devotion.

You have walked everywhere, with your vapuḥ and vāṇī;  
The seeds are already growing—we have to pick the fruits.

All preaching is your mercy; you are the owner of the seeds;  
As everything is Kṛṣṇa's, all the fruits are yours.

As the bhoga must be offered, you must first receive all credit  
Or, as food turns to sin, our preaching turns into pride.

Alas, why am I so attached, ungrateful, and so selfish  
That it is even possible for me to forget you?

Why try to independently irrigate the desert?  
Outside your worldwide ISKCON, just a drop I be.  
This ISKCON is like an ocean, a treasure house of nectar;  
You filled it up with mercy to inundate the world.  
Some say there is more, something that you did not give;  
They are people haunted by sense gratification.

One who has Kṛṣṇa has everything, and you gave us Śrī Caitanya.  
Now what more can we ask? Let us begin to serve!

Our ambition should not be the position of the father,  
But service in separation with intense lamentation.

Our beloved father left us a gigantic task of love,  
A challenge to all, impossible for one.

You are the only one, the avatāra śakty-āveśa,  
And since you are one with ISKCON, you never went away.

Your body is not material, conditioned by time and space,  
But the pure form of service, of divine, eternal love.

You are the youngest and most beautiful, an angel from Vṛndāvana,  
Your every gesture an offering of devotion.

Your strength is immeasurable; you are conquering the world,  
Your wisdom unsurpassable; you are the best devotee of God.

Like Brahmā, Lakṣmī, Rudra, and the four sages Kumāra,  
You created your own ISKCON, Prabhupāda-sampradāya.

The G. B. C. should keep you forever as the head,  
The master, savior and guide, the hero of the world.

To say that in the center your vāṇī we should worship  
It's good, but like the law, it is too impersonal.  
Scriptures may be interpreted, as lawyers do the law;  
Bhagavad-gītā became impersonal when Kṛṣṇa was removed.

Just as we never thought that you were an older man,  
So now let us not think that you are no longer here.  
Rāma's shoes ruled Ayodhyā, and Bharata remained their servant;  
For one who really loves you, your vapuḥ is absolute.

If we do not think like this, we will all begin to quarrel,  
For as many are the vapuḥs, as many conflicts will develop.

What is ten thousand years to sufficiently glorify you!  
With all the time of Brahmā, how could we properly repay you?

I have just begun to serve you, and already want all honors.  
Please excuse my childish play; in confusion I imitate you.

Success will not come by becoming our own masters  
But by serving Prabhupāda in cooperative spirit.  
We have not yet fulfilled one billionth of the instructions;  
What else is there to do than full speed push on together?

The goal is very clear—the whole planet Kṛṣṇa conscious—  
And there is only one way, your movement, Prabhupāda.

Prabhupāda, I am so fallen, so far from an example,  
In despair I feel like crying. When will I please you?

Surrender means to dive into the yajña of saṅkīrtana,  
More humble than the grass, more tolerant than a tree.

Without pride or false prestige, with all respect for others,  
Let us give them Kṛṣṇa consciousness in Prabhupāda's name.  
Please empower me with strength; please illuminate my vision;  
Please keep me in your service; my life and soul are yours.

Once finished on this planet, you may send me somewhere else.  
Birth after birth, please keep me eternally at your feet.

Until the whole universe sings your glories, Prabhupāda,  
This is my only mission, my samādhi and satisfaction.  
Your līlā did not finish in 1977  
But is going on eternally for your surrendered souls.

If I continue preaching, I am sure one day I will  
Receive your greatest blessings—to be with you again.

All glories to Lord Caitanya and His mission of Hari-nāma!  
All glories to Prabhupāda and his ISKCON saṅkīrtana!

*Aspiring to become worthy to serve you,*  
*Your insignificant son and disciple,*  
Bhaktyānanda-vidya Goswami

## Bir Kṛṣṇa dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

Before you left this material world you told us, "Your love for me will be shown by how much you cooperate to keep this institution together after I am gone." Because we all accept axiomatically that the goal of our lives is to develop love for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda (and thus for the Supreme Lord), we must demonstrate this love in a practical manner as mentioned above. Otherwise our declarations of love are rather shallow. To fully understand all of the ramifications of your injunction that we should demonstrate our love by cooperating to keep the movement together, we must understand the meaning of your two key words *cooperate* and *institution.*

The word *cooperation* does not refer to a one-sided situation. That is, it refers to situations where there are two or more parties involved, and *all* of the parties are willing to forsake sectarian interests in the light of achieving a common goal. This means that the common goal is seen by the different parties as the overriding purpose behind all actions. All actions are meant to reach this goal, and party or sectarian interest must be sacrificed to achieve this end.

What is this goal in our movement? You have expressed this goal very clearly in your Vyāsa-pūjā offering to His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Prabhupāda: "When will that day come when a temple will be established in every house in every corner of the world? When will the high-court judge be a Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava with *tilaka* beautifully decorating his forehead? When will a Vaiṣṇava winning votes be elected president of the land and preaching be spread everywhere?"

That is to say, the goal of all the sincere Prabhupāda-anugas is for all the living entities in this world to become Kṛṣṇa conscious. Therefore the pure disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda should be willing to sacrifice any personal interest in order to achieve that goal.

As we have said before, the sacrifice of personal interest involves *all* parties, not just the lower echelons, but the upper echelon too. *Cooperation* should not be simply a slogan to enforce one's will. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have stated that our movement functions by "love and trust," which entails a two-way relationship.

The leaders of our movement should recognize, as you did, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that anyone who is member of our movement is a member voluntarily, and his service is voluntary (due to his possessing free will).

The only thing which ties him to his service is love: for his spiritual master, for other Vaiṣṇavas, and for Kṛṣṇa. It is the leaders' duty to encourage others to increase this love and therefore their service. The other members of this movement should recognize that the leaders are also tied with love, and they should respect and honor that love, and also encourage their managerial superiors to increase that love.

That brings us to the point of defining the word *institution.* What is the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement? Is it the buildings? Is it the bylaws? Obviously not. Just as the body is useless without the soul, our movement is useless without its members. So the members of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement have to be seen as its real assets, and just as in any business the protection and augmentation of the assets is the prime concern of the business, in ISKCON we should always be meditating on how to increase and protect the members of the institution.

Sometimes people think that they can be Kṛṣṇa conscious outside of this movement, but you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have strongly replied to this in your books, stating that, "One who thinks he can be Kṛṣṇa conscious outside ISKCON is simply living a hallucination." Why is this? Because one outside of ISKCON can never be instrumental in fulfilling your wishes that the whole world become Kṛṣṇa conscious. He can never become part of a preaching scheme meant to change the face of the world. His interest will always live in the sectarian sphere. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are *jagad-guru,* which means the *guru* of all the universes and indeed even all species of life. If we can't assist even in a small way in this movement, can we actually call ourselves your disciples?

So, if one wishes to become Kṛṣṇa conscious, he must become your disciple by assisting you in the form of this movement, and regardless of all difficulties, he must remain in this movement. This movement is you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We must be chaste to you under any and all circumstances.

Also, the leaders of this movement should do everything in their power to prevent members from leaving, even if it does require some personal sacrifice on their parts, either a sacrifice of some particular managerial philosophy or perhaps a sacrifice of something more tangible.

The only way that your desires can be carried out is if this movement is maintained and strengthened. That is the business of each and every one of us, regardless of our position. We must see, as you said, "the big ISKCON."

*Your servant,*  
Bir Kṛṣṇa dāsa Goswami

## Devāmṛta Swami

Everything is darkness  
Because I cannot see  
Without your light  
Shining ahead of me  
Ineffectual and foolish  
With no humility to command  
My only chance—  
To fulfill your demands

The defeat of māyā's forces  
Is already engineered by you  
We keep you in our hearts  
You'II reveal all the clues

What looks hopeless to me  
Is ecstatic preaching for you  
What is the use of intelligence  
If it’s not purified in full?

Nescience is king—  
The world's completely dark  
Except when your footprints  
Illuminate our hearts.

Devāmṛta Swami

## Dhṛṣṭadyumna Swami

*evaṁ janaṁ nipatitaṁ prabhavāhi-kūpe*  
*kāmābhikāmam anu yaḥ prapatan prasaṅgāt*  
*kṛtvātmasāt surarṣiṇā bhagavān gṛhītaḥ*  
*so 'haṁ kathaṁ nu visṛje tava bhṛtya-sevām*  
*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 7.9.28*

**"My dear Lord, O Supreme Personality of Godhead, because of my association with material desires, one after another, I was gradually falling into a blind well full of snakes, following the general populace. But now your servant kindly accepted me as his disciple and instructed me how to achieve the transcendental position. Therefore, my first duty is to serve him. How could I leave his service?"**

The other day your dear disciple Girirāja Swami mentioned to me that he has just finished rereading the entire *The Nectar of Devotion* after many, many years. I asked him what he had found. His reply, while humorous, struck a deep chord of truth—"Well, I'd advanced a couple of paragraphs."

Later on, I was reading the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā,* Chapter Twenty-three, wherein Lord Caitanya explains to Sanātana Gosvāmī the ultimate goal of life—love of Godhead—and all stages leading up to it. Again I was humbled to realize how far from perfection my spiritual life was after all these years (15) of practicing Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Then Girirāja showed me this verse printed above from the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (7.9.28), how even after attaining *darśana* of the Supreme Lord for his unflinching devotion, Prahlāda Mahārāja prayed not to be swayed from pure devotional service. Inwardly, I guiltily confessed at my own inability to muster such a sincere prayer when confronted by Māyā's arsenal in the form of profit, adoration, and distinction.

Again, I was talking with a Godbrother, and we were deeply considering the seriousness of having accepted you as our spiritual master long years ago. How little did we understand then! How much do we understand now? We concluded there are many levels of devotees of Kṛṣṇa, but to be called a true disciple of (and by) Your Divine Grace—manifesting perfectly all your instructions, deceptively simple as they are—is no cheap thing.

With each passing appearance-day celebration, my amazement at your qualities and achievements grows immeasurably, my debt increases, and my resolve for the tasks you have set strengthens. Following in the footsteps of Prahlāda, I pray to never forget my vows and always execute your final instruction to me in Vṛndāvana:

''Preach and be blessed."

*Your eternal servant,*  
Dhṛṣṭadyumna Swami

## Gaṇapatī Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*
