Please accept my humble obeisances and this offering at your lotus feet, forgiving my arrogant nature and offensive attitude to Your Divine Grace and all your disciples.

Material world—  
Māyā's domain  
Abode of rebels—  
gone insane.  
Birth and death,  
disease, old age  
The only payment—  
saṁsāra gained.

Creators of bodies—  
organic machines  
Skin-deep beauty—  
home of disease.  
Senses like serpents—  
poisonous tongues  
Our only success—  
ashes or dust.

Attachment to kin—  
"Isn't he cute?"  
Lust's fruit—  
new bag of skin.  
"Eternal" love  
carved on a tree  
Legal prostitution—  
Kali's matrimony.

Work like an ass—  
American dream  
The Joneses next door—  
"Better than we."  
Mind-altered states—  
cheap solution  
"Uppers" or " downers"?-  
total confusion.

After frustration—  
mokṣa's next  
"I'm God after all—  
illusion a jest.  
Playing hide-and-seek—  
myself to find."  
Māyā's last snare—  
darkest of blind.

Liberation a fancy—  
mental concoction  
Icchā and dveṣa—  
false conception.  
Chance for perfection—  
Vaiṣṇava Guru  
Bhakti-latā-bīja—  
gift from you.

Prabhupāda, Prabhupāda!  
most merciful one  
Kṛṣṇa your friend  
Vṛndāvana your home.  
No reason to do  
what only you did  
Causeless compassion—  
bring life to the dead.

'Midst madmen and insults  
you chanted the names  
Gave us your purports,  
left us your fame.  
"Just do as I did—  
at least maintain  
Ten thousand years—  
ISKCON to reign.

"Purity the force—  
no party factions  
Consider the world first—  
'your' project second.  
By book distribution  
and Śrī Māyāpur Dhāma  
Do it together—  
Vaikuṇṭha at hand."  
Can soldiers quarrel  
amidst a great war?  
Thinking, "My vision—  
broadest of all."  
Mundane societies,  
for political favor,  
Cooperate too—  
was this meant by you?  
Worldwide mission—  
delicate balance  
Autonomy one hand—  
restraint on the other.  
"Love and trust the key"—  
you clearly decreed  
Only a motto—  
or vital reality?  
Books are the basis—  
forever you'II guide  
Māyā no power  
with you at our sides.  
Help us represent you—  
the world to see "  
You become glorious—  
that will glorify me."

*Begging to become an insignificant servant of Your Divine Grace and your transcendental body, ISKCON,*  
Gaṇapatī Swami

## Guṇagrāhī dāsa Goswami

*Most beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my prostrated obeisances on this most auspicious day of your divine appearance.

Prabhupāda, you are the greatest personality of this age. Your mercy and compassion are limitless. In the mood of Lord Caitanya, you are giving everyone, regardless of their position, the opportunity to serve you and your glorious mission. Your exalted self in the form of your books is now pervading every continent and is enlivening the hearts of millions. Thus the seeds are being planted and watered to effect the transformation of the whole world. Only you know how it should all unfold. We therefore pray fervently for your mercy in the form of continued guidance and inspiration. Without remaining pure, we know we will not receive it. And because I am such a poor candidate for such mercy, I must take help from your intimate disciples whom you have trained for boldly taking up the lead. I pray that I can assist them humbly and not hinder them in any way in the spreading of your divine legacy.

Prabhupāda, somehow or other you have accepted me as your disciple, although I am more like a naughty son than your dedicated servant. It is said that in this day and age, even a pauper is proud of his penny. A pauper in your service, I pray to become free from the bewilderment of false pride. Anything of value that has ever or will ever manifest within myself or within this movement is all coming causelessly from Your Divine Grace. Always remembering this, we will be able to keep you in the center of our lives, and the continued success of your ISKCON will prevail.

Prabhupāda, you have told us that only a fool is ungrateful to his benefactor. This offering to you is a meager attempt to express some appreciation so as not to appear like the number-one fool that I actually am. Your Divine Grace knows my heart, however. Moreover, since one's real love is seen by action, not simply by words, my lack of gratitude is further revealed. I am thankful, nevertheless, for this opportunity to search deep within myself to express to you what little devotion I am capable of, and I pray prostrated at your lotus feet, begging to become free from offense so that 1 may properly dedicate myself to you life and soul.

*Your unworthy servant,*  
Guṇagrāhī dāsa Goswami

## Guru-prasāda Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace. When I reflect upon the inheritance you have given us, it leaves my mind reeling. You introduced us to the chanting of Kṛṣṇa's holy name, you gave us your books, temples, *gurukula* system, and you gave us devotional service, what to speak of countless other transcendental tools to assist your building a house in which everyone can reside. You have made the perfect floor plan, the frame, and the foundation, and you have left the materials to help us finish it.

However, the most important material, the binding force which is holding the whole house together, is the association of devotees:

*kṛṣṇa-bhakti-saṅga vinā śreyaḥ nāhi āra*

**"The only auspicious activity is association with devotees of Kṛṣṇa." (Cc.** ***Madhya*** **8.251)**

You gave your association to undeserving, conditioned souls, whose only qualification was your causeless mercy. Although they could not even properly reciprocate, you nurtured their tender devotional creepers. As our movement grew, you constantly took time, demonstrating extreme patience, to give everyone the necessary care and attention which they needed, and demanded, to advance spiritually. Even at the expense of interrupting your translation work, which was so important and essential to the future of ISKCON, you gave your personal association. At the very end of your manifest stay with us, you emphasized that by cooperation we could keep your personal association intact and thus keep ISKCON strong and healthy.

Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I pray that you continue to bless me with your association: through your books, your disciples, and your dearmost ISKCON. Please give all of us the proper vision, so that we can associate with one another in the same loving, compassionate, and intelligent way you did with us. Please help us to give that association not only to those who want it, but also to those who need it, as you did. In this way your spiritual mansion will engulf the world.

*Your unworthy servant,*  
Guru-prasāda Swami

## Indradyumna Swami

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to you!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most auspicious occasion of the celebration of your appearance in this world, I am intensely feeling separation from you, remembering your kindness and compassion upon this fallen and conditioned soul. In the summer of 1970 you appeared before me through your disciples preaching your eternal message of devotional service to the Lord. From that very moment I did not doubt you, though I remember so many foolish students turned away from your words on that hot summer day upon the grass of the university campus. Actually, I cannot even remember seeing them go, as I was transfixed upon your clear and lucid message that cut through all the illusion in which I was so deeply embedded. In my confused existence your words offered hope and light as I gazed in amazement upon your disciples, who radiated such purity and life from your divine association. When you appeared before me that day, I found it very easy to give up whatever little I had to be attached to; those first special moments made everything else appear dry and lifeless. Though I was previously dragging myself through this material existence, I suddenly felt I could run after you! And now through these last fifteen years I've continued to run (although I've often stumbled) as you have swept me up in the ecstasy of your devotional service to the Lord.

Actually, all these years have passed as moments, giving me an understanding of the eternality of my soul. My youth seems like yesterday, though I know I've grown much older. When I was younger, this body promised so much and was full of strength and power! But yes, your words have proved so true; it's failing on me even now, before I pass the crossroads at the middle of my life. Its glory has already faded, leaving me the stark remembrance that everything which you have said will come to pass. Some foolish nonsense presumed that life begins at forty; myself, I simply hear the chilling winds of the autumn of my life just around the corner, and I lose my faith in this material existence and resolve to completely surrender unto you. Your instructions are my life, not this useless body which I watch in amazement fall apart before me. Yet by your grace so many other worthless things seem to fall along beside it! See all the foolish, fallible soldiers stricken by your mace of transcendental knowledge . . . useless attachments that once also seemed so real! Now the false hopes and promises of wealth, honor, fame, and women all appear dead and lifeless as I stand before you today. I see only the real life of service unto you, which always seems to make me younger than even yesterday, just as you always appeared to me with the fresh countenance of an eternal youth. What is this miracle of devotional service? I want to give up once and for all my attachments to this world of birth and death!

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, give me the sword of your transcendental might, and help me cut forever my bondage to illusion. Help me cut off the final ropes of attachment that keep me bound from freely serving you. Bless me with the power to take up your mission within this world, wielding your heavy mace of transcendental knowledge. Allow me to preach forcefully, using the sharp edge of detachment from anything which is not meaningful to your service. Make good my formal renouncement of this world, and bless me with the qualities of those who serve you purely. I really do want to be finished with this business of chewing the chewed. I gradually feel the strong purgative of the *sannyāsa* order freeing me from the waste of material life. I want to offer you the gift today that I do feel I have grown in devotional service through the years. I would like you to think that you could be proud of me, despite my foolish attempts to serve you as I do. I want to work hard for you throughout my entire life, spreading your *saṅkīrtana* movement all over the world. Doing so, I will keep your instructions as my life and soul, and 1 will gain inspiration remembering the chosen moments that 1 spent close to you. Such precious times, walking and talking together on the way to the plane in 1972, in your room at the Bury Place temple, when you slapped me on the back in firm appreciation, and in Paris when you patted me on the head, cut the hard knot of my attraction to this world, and left me forever indebted to you. I will always try to serve you well, Śrīla Prabhupāda, in the sweet expectation that someday, somewhere, I will have the chance to see you again as I did in the infancy of my spiritual life. Now, in your absence, I sometimes dream of sitting with you again, like before, in the trance of watching you play your *karatālas* from the *vyāsāsana* in the special way you did, seeing you open your transcendental eyes, moist with tears of *kṛṣṇa-prema,* momentarily studying your audience in your ecstasy of chanting the holy names of the Lord: *jaya rādhā mādhava kuñja-vihārī.* We could see you were there, in the groves of Vṛndāvana, and that we were here, foolishly attached to this material world.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, I want to go with you there! Or anywhere in the three worlds where your preaching is going on. My only request is that you keep me with you always in your service to the Lord. You once told me that things are very difficult in this material world, but that when I go back to Godhead everything will be very easy. So please, I depend upon you! Take me there! I'll try my best to always be your humble and submissive servant and do everything you ask. And I pray that the next time you gaze out at us sitting at your feet, you won't see this bumbling, stumbling disciple of yours nodding off to sleep, but a matured devotee, alert to your every word and seasoned by many years of dedicated service unto you, ready to give this and every other life I lead in the service of Your Divine Grace. O Śrīla Prabhupāda, I look forward to seeing you again, and next time it will be especially sweet, coming from the field of service as I will, having carried out your orders and purchased my ticket back home.

*Your aspiring servant,*  
Indradyumna Swami

## Jagatguru Swami

O friend of the poor and lord and master of the devotees,  
all tribute to you, the dearmost servant of  
Lord Caitanya and Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura.  
All tribute to you on this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā

A happy day for all the world when you appeared by the grace of Kṛṣṇa to spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya in every town and village! How bright the future looked, when you walked among us, to lead us on to victory! But then our hearts fell deep and sank when you vanished from our sight. And life since then, I sadly say, has never been the same.

By your order every chance was there to remain by your side, but then the darkness fell. Illusion overcame me, and my heart filled with pride. I forgot that I am only a servant whose position is very low. I began to think myself very advanced, like the glowworm who becomes enamored by his own luster when the moon is out of sight. I thought I had become " the highest among men, a *paramahaṁsa;* and even worse, I thought I had always been. The desire to take position burned like a fire within. I wanted to be the *guru!* That was my greatest sin. I thought I should be worshiped, and 1 would become the redeemer of men. What foolishness it was! And all these things have caused me pain. But who am I to blame? I am the greatest fool, and I hang my head in shame.

Had I only remembered my real position as the servant of my master's desires. Woe is me, woe is me! You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, were the most selfless servant of your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, and you sacrificed everything in his service. Had I actually understood your great humility I could have saved myself from the demon of my pride. Seeing that I was falling, you tried to save me by sending your more sincere men. But I could not recognize my brothers as my actual friends, and in fear they were all my enemy—I kicked them all away. And now I am alone, and *māyā* is all around. Only you can save me. Please let me try again!

Having cried out my heart to you, I see a future hope. Like a loving father, tirelessly you work to save the fallen. I see your form in golden hue, and my heart fills with joy. With your head held high and your cane steadfastly in your hand, you stand above all, the leader of your men. Your words dispel the clouds of doubt: "Preaching is the way." By the power of your mighty pen the demons fall away, and from their ashes rise again a host of saintly men. Like a great army they are going out to conquer *māyā,* carrying your books in hand. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the master of saintly men.

But who am I, and where am I, and what am I to do? And then your solemn words, I remember when you said, "You are Jagatguru dāsa, the servant of the spiritual master of the world."

*Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.*

Jagatguru Swami

## Jayādvaita Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble and respectful obeisances at your lotus feet.

When you first came to America, you came only to satisfy Lord Kṛṣṇa and your Guru Mahārāja. They inspired you, guided you, protected you, and reciprocated with you. They were in your heart, and you in theirs.

By your mercy, while thinking about this Vyāsa-pūjā offering, I realized that what I was thinking of was expressed by a verse in *Bhagavad-gītā.* It happened like this: At class this morning I spoke on a verse, *mac-cittā mad-gata-prāṇāḥ.* To find the page, I looked it up in the verse index. And the verse indexed above it was *mac-cittaḥ sarva-durgāṇi.* I thought nothing of it, but now, as I write, that verse popped into my mind. And as I turn the pages of your purports, I see the theme and mood I wanted, already spread out before me in your books. Devotee reader, turn to the page and just see!

Back up only one verse, to 18.58. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you tell us, in perfectly realized words, how to act—not as masters of the world but as humble, obedient servants, fully dedicated to your order. You pass down to us the same instruction that inspired you: one should make the spiritual master's order one's heart and soul.

And in the next verse you give great comfort and assurance: If we simply follow your instructions, your mercy will carry us above all trouble.

You personally penned in the last words of your purport. In the original press proofs the purport said, "a person in Kṛṣṇa consciousness is free to act because everything is prompted by Kṛṣṇa from within." Then, with perfect wisdom and authority, you wrote in: "and confirmed by the spiritual master."

We're conditioned souls, puffed-up fools, incompetent to act on our own. If we act in false ego—inventing, concocting, rationalizing, neglecting your instructions—we entangle ourselves in perplexities. But when we act as simple, obedient servants, doing what you have asked, sticking to the path you have confirmed as right, we are always on the safe side, on Kṛṣṇa's side, chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa and dancing in ecstasy to please you.

If we simply remember you and follow your instructions, you will always inspire us, protect us, guide us, reciprocate with us, and everything will be all right.

I beg to remain,  
*Your servant,*  
Jayādvaita Swami

## Kavicandra Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most fallen obeisances at your divine lotus feet. You dedicated your Bhaktivedanta purports to the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* in these most profound words:

To Śrīla Prabhupāda  
Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī  
Gosvāmī Mahārāja  
MY SPIRITUAL MASTER  
On the 26th Annual Ceremony of His\|  
Disappearance Day

He lives forever by his divine instructions and the follower lives with him.

You are the personification of your spiritual master's instructions, and there is no doubt that you are living forever with him. You never did anything which didn't glorify the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa. Your spiritual master ordered you, "If you ever get money, print books!" You were so determined to follow his order that even when you didn't have money, you printed books. When you came to Japan you convinced the big publishers Dai Nippon, who never give credit to foreign accounts, to extend you a very big credit, for printing your books. Such is your spiritual power.

You have ordered us, your aspiring followers, to assist in your service to your spiritual master, to distribute your books "As much as possible, in as many languages as possible." You have assured us that if we do this, then this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement will spread automatically all over the world. Now we are beginning to distribute your books to the Japanese people, in the Japanese language. Of course, we feel that we face difficulties, but actually you have removed all obstacles. You were alone, with no money, and in old age. You worked unlimitedly to arrange everything so that we could distribute your books. You made Hare Kṛṣṇa a household word. You traveled everywhere and created devotees and opened so many temples. You taught us everything by doing everything yourself. And you live forever by your divine instructions. Your appearance was so wonderful that naturally we want to be with you, so you inspired us to take up and follow your instructions.

In your *pranam* you mention you came to defeat the *"śūnyavādīs."* Here in the Orient we can understand what is a *śūnyavādī.* This voidist conception is deeply rooted in their language and culture. But your books have the perfect power for converting them into pure *Vaiṣṇavas.* You speak directly to the soul. Your teachings penetrate any type of mental speculative covering and awaken anyone who listens. By hearing they remember their original constitutional positions as servants of Kṛṣṇa. We simply must present your books and teachings so purely, and surely we will be successful. By your grace we must follow your instructions. Then, being with you, we will be able to assist in glorifying the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

You taught us that this ISKCON is your body and that the BBT is your heart. I want very much to please your heart, to give all of my energy for producing and distributing your books, as you did, and as so many of your empowered disciples are doing. I do not understand anything, but I am following blindly. By your writing you created a continuous glorification of your spiritual master. Our prayer is to continuously glorify you by distributing your books to everyone, everywhere. This is our eternal desire, life after life, to go on giving your teachings, your association, to the fallen souls, and to thereby gain the privilege of being with you. Please don't let us forget that a united effort to distribute your books profusely can conquer any opposition from *māyā.*

*Your aspiring servant,*  
Kavicandra Swami

## Keśava Bhāratī dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Hallowed halls vacant  
Portent to future  
Holocausts

Downtrodden, time-worn faces  
Ring down the curtain of  
Merrymaking mirth  
Organized sycophants  
Suck vital forces from within  
The world's social institutions

Racketeers disguised as  
Educators and scientists  
Vend nescience as knowledge

Knowledge-deprived couples  
Abuse, desecrate, assassinate  
One another, even their offspring

Camouflaged fiends lurk as  
Magi eager to plunder  
Innocent pilgrims

Hopeless clamoring recalls  
Historic saturation of  
Impersonal, hedonistic glee

*Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Into this nightmare of spiritual vacuum, armed with the order of your Guru Mahārāja and directly empowered by the Supreme Personality of Godhead Himself to execute that order, you made your transcendental appearance into this material universe and finally into the modern Western world—alone. The impact you made on world history will only be perceived by attaining a life-span of ten thousand years. Solely the demigods and higher spiritual authorities, then, will be watching in awe and full appreciation of your contribution to the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the deliverance of the holy name of the Lord, and love of God to every town and village.

We are sharing, by your causeless mercy, a drop of that comprehension of how much you accomplished on this planet single-handedly. So powerful is that drop of realization that it is bringing the conditioned mind to its knees, begging to be relieved of the enjoying spirit. We want to take an infinitesimal portion of the responsibility you took for relieving the anxiety Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu feels upon seeing the fallen conditioned souls laboring uselessly under the burden of self-created duties, deprived of the real pleasure of eternally serving Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govindacandra.

Your books have begun an avalanche of knowledge that overwhelms anyone who is actually sincere with renewed hope and real happiness. Because your books are nondifferent from the Supreme Lord Himself, having been spoken directly by Him through your lotus mouth, they are effectively changing the consciousness of the earth's population. You said yourself in the *Antya-līlā* of *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* that unless your disciples continue to read all of your books, they will fall down. "They are not falling down from distributing my books," you said, laughingly. "They are falling down when they stop distributing my books!"

To congregationally glorify you is our unique hope. Then we can remember *saṅkīrtana* and glorify Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is Śrī Kṛṣṇa Himself, and you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the incarnation of His mercy. Therefore, only you could teach the whole world to follow in the footsteps of Lord Caitanya by performing *saṅkīrtana.*

The void created by dry mental speculation on the nature of the Absolute Truth has been filled with the bliss created by your hearing and chanting of Lord Gaurāṅga’s sweet holy names and transcendental pastimes. We fall down at your lotus feet and worship you as the *avatāra* of mercy.

You alone could open the shutters to the spiritual sky, bestowing upon us forms, tastes, sounds, smells, textures, and activities so sweet that our senses naturally want to reject the activities and forms created by material sense gratification. We fall down at your lotus feet and worship you as the supreme transcendental magician.

Giving long awaited employment to the spiritual senses, you have cured us of all lamentation and hankering caused by inactive apathy and aimless furtive endeavor. We fall down at your lotus feet and worship you as the transcendental doctor of the soul. Please continue to vaccinate us with the serum of your mercy.

As the champion of truth, you have defeated all false arguments with factual ascertainment of the ultimate feature of the truth, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. We fall down at your lotus feet and worship you as the supreme defender of nature's laws, Gaura Hari's chief commander.

Having revealed life's purpose, you have cast the goal within our reach. Our minds are thus feeling simultaneously enthused and pacified. We fall down at your feet and worship you as the savior of the world.

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, your separation is unbearable. You could unite us all by a mere glance. In our fumbling attempts to serve you, only you can approve of us. And only in that way can we receive the mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the essential ingredient for expanding your ISKCON branch of His family tree. Please be merciful to us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, lest we forget our positions as your menial servants.

Lip service will not qualify us as your menial servants. Only following your instructions will qualify us. Give us the humility, forbearance, enthusiasm, and determination needed to follow all your instructions, to "Do as you are doing." Force us to see and appreciate each other's service to you, for by properly respecting one another's abilities to lead and to follow will our offenses cease and order be restored.

On this most blessed day of your appearance, we cannot express adequately our gratitude for all the matchless gifts you have given us. Let us all band together in the highest gesture of love for you—full cooperation for the purpose of fulfilling your mission.

*Begging for any place in the shade of your lotus feet,*  
*your eternal servant,*  
Keśava Bhāratī dāsa Goswami

## Mahāśṛṅga dāsa Goswami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble and fallen obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet.

I'm sorry I've never written you before, but I've always felt unqualified. Not that I'm qualified now, but since entering the renounced order of life I should act more responsibly. So please excuse this ten-year-old's attempt at glorifying you.

You have said in the *Bhagavad-gītā* that by understanding this philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness we will not take birth here again. But there is no way to grasp this knowledge except through your explanation. You have translated the Vedic literatures perfectly for time, place, and circumstance, for all people's mentality. And by advancing our understanding you can empower us to push on ISKCON for the next ten thousand years. Unfortunately, many of our Godbrothers have not had faith in your teachings and have chosen another guide for trying to make spiritual advancement. These poor souls are victims of Kali-yuga and are being misled by their illusioned mind, thinking that the grass is greener on the other side of the fence. Please tolerate these naughty children as a mother tolerates her child's passing stool on her. Because if you do not excuse them, then their transferal into the spiritual world will take millions of years. So please give us the strength, tolerance, and knowledge to explain this philosophy correctly to all people and bring them under your banner of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

While you were physically present with us, ISKCON continued to grow and expand. Your disciples were always increasing, even though some were leaving. You yourself said, "Don't be surprised who goes; be surprised who stays." But since you're not here to give direct guidance and chastisement, now confusion, mistrust, and apprehension have become household words, with the results of more Godbrothers and -sisters leaving. Therefore my prayer to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is that you make us all grow up and learn to trust in Lord Kṛṣṇa more and not trust our material minds and material intelligence. Since you are with Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, maybe you could ask Him or Lord Nityānanda Prabhu to directly interfere with our false egos, so we can follow and fulfill your plan to install ISKCON as a cooperating body to guide the world. Since there are no pure devotees of your stature on this planet today, we have to call on you to save us. So please excuse my boldness in asking your help, but we really don't have any other choice!!

Hoping to remain a loyal son to you always,  
*In your service,*  
Mahāśṛṅga dāsa Goswami

## Mahāviṣṇu Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet! You are the true representative of the Supreme Lord, Kṛṣṇa, who has walked this very earth just to save myself and so many others who were suffering so much. You are my real father, because you gave me birth to eternal life in your Kṛṣṇa conscious family, ISKCON.

Of course, I realize that it can never, ever be possible to thank you sufficiently on this Vyāsa-pūjā day for having come and saved me from hell and for having given me complete happiness in Lord Caitanya's *saṅkīrtana* movement. As a new devotee, I am just beginning to find my feet in spiritual life, and only by your special kindness (the kindness of your dearest disciples), I am just about avoiding very dangerous pitfalls at every step. I am only a child, and I can hardly talk or write in a mature and proper way in praise of you and your wonderful ISKCON.

However, the most wonderful thing for me is to be able to go out on *saṅkīrtana* and preach about Lord Kṛṣṇa just as I did on the first day I joined your society in London, years ago. Then I feel the greatest fulfillment and happiness, as though I were an eternal servant of you and Kṛṣṇa. I can feel and also see the waves of nectar affecting all the listeners and onlookers as we chant in the streets. They are also chanting and smiling. Some are buying your books, and this gives me deep satisfaction to be satisfying you. In fact, when I am distributing your books, then I know I am quite safe, and you are close in every way. I feel like a real person becoming fully alive again, becoming fully conscious, fully satisfied, and complete in Kṛṣṇa's service.

In the words and terms you have given me, out on *saṅkīrtana* especially, I transcend the modes of nature.

*māṁ ca yo 'vyabhicāreṇa*  
*bhakti-yogena sevate*  
*sa guṇān samatītyaitān*  
*brahma-bhūyāya kalpate*

**"One who engages in full devotional service, unfailing in all circumstances, at once transcends the modes of material nature and thus comes to the level of Brahman." (Bg. 14.26)**

All my previous life I was actually dreaming of being in this *saṅkīrtana* movement under your shelter, but of course, I never knew it. My previous "life," so-called, seemed pointless and a waste of time—just the passing of time in banal and gross activities which ended in more and more misery. By your preaching and book distribution you found me lurking in the grim shadows of accumulating sins. Through your enthusiasm and compassion you inspired your disciples to come to England and drag me back from my living death—doom.

Now, on this most auspicious day of your holy Vyāsa-pūjā, I can only try to rededicate myself to being always at your service as a part of your ISKCON movement. Please let me always be safely serving you by serving usefully in your preaching family so that 1 may eventually make a worthwhile contribution which will really please you.

One day after many, many births of becoming qualified in the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya, through your divine mercy, when I can really make your order my life and soul, I hope and pray to be able to be with you face to face and be recognized as your lost child.

*Your humble servant,*  
Mahāviṣṇu Swami

## Pāraṅgacit Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you say in the *Upadeśāmṛta* that only a true devotee can get a perfect sight of another devotee. This means that if someone wants to actually understand your sublime position, he must become an unalloyed devotee of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. You have taught the art of how to use everything in Kṛṣṇa's service and at the same time be fully free and detached. Your behavior is confirmed by Kṛṣṇa in the *Bhagavad-gītā:*

*yoga-yukto viśuddhātmā*  
*vijitātmā jitendriyaḥ*  
*sarva-bhūtātmā bhūtātmā*  
*kurvan api na lipyate*

**"One who works with devotion, who is a pure soul, and who controls his mind and senses is dear to everyone, and everyone is dear to him. Though always working, such a man is never entangled."**

So you have shown that we should work but never become entangled; rather, we should keep ourselves strongly attached to the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa. Your greatest desire is that all persons become changed into pure devotees. So, this must be our goal of life. All your instructions were in that way, and one who follows them will attain this life's transcendental goal.

O my eternal father, by following this policy the imperishable connection with you is established. Therefore I always beg for your mercy that this goal never runs away from my mind and intelligence. Your nectarean instructions are the guideline of my existence. By your causeless mercy only I shall one day understand how insignificant I am. By your behavior I can feel Kṛṣṇa's supreme control.

You instruct one how to be confident in the Supreme Lord. *Avaśya rasibe kṛṣṇa:* "I fully trust that Kṛṣṇa shall protect me and help me so that I become successful in the performance of devotional service."

In this way you have showed that above all one should never forget Kṛṣṇa. Therefore, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I beg that you accept me, despite my disqualifications.

*Your eternal and humble servant,*  
Pāraṅgacit Swami

## Prahlādānanda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

The supreme Personality of Godhead's message comes down through an unbroken succession of bona fide spiritual masters, which originates with Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Himself. Today is the celebration of your divine appearance day. It is especially auspicious because you are the most exalted representative of Lord Kṛṣṇa who has distributed full Kṛṣṇa consciousness further around the world than any saint or incarnation within recorded history. Having gotten a glimpse of your lotus footprints, we can only pray for the strength to be able to follow them.

*yasya deve parā bhaktir*  
*yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ*  
*prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

**"Only unto those great souls who have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed."** ***(Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad)***

You have stated that your disciples' love for you will be tested by their ability to work cooperatively together. Certainly this will at times require great patience and determination, but to satisfy you we must be prepared to do anything. Pradyumna, Lord Kṛṣṇa's son, once said that if he was placed in an ocean of nectar but could not remember Lord Kṛṣṇa, he would experience the ocean as being poisonous; but even if he was in an ocean of poison, if he was in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, it would seem to him like an ocean of nectar. Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this age also means consciousness of you—Prabhupāda consciousness.

Once in San Francisco, in 1974, when I was fanning you, you were radiating with compassion as you began talking about the conditioned souls of this material world and said, "I just want them to be happy." So by your mercy let our desires be transcendentally pure and simple like yours; let us not be burdened by our petty material dreams connected with our material bodies and desires; let us turn inwards, into our hearts, and listen to the Lord, yourself, and your sincere representatives, and thus let us become divine instruments to spread Kṛṣṇa and Prabhupāda consciousness around the world.

*Your humble servant,*  
Prahlādānanda Swami

## Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**"I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, who has opened my eyes, which were blinded by the darkness of ignorance, with the torchlight of knowledge."**

Recently, while relishing with a Godbrother many topics about Your Divine Grace, he made the following observation: "In each of his actions, Śrīla Prabhupāda would behave as if his spiritual master were standing right next to him." The simplicity of this statement does not betray its profundity nor its significance in our own humble quest for perfection.

To also go about in life feeling your overwhelming presence at every moment, and simultaneously committed to act accordingly, is a most sublime state of consciousness, for it means having attained feasible self-realization. But such an achievement is not an easy one, and so we should view such traumatic events as your external departure as another phase of our spiritual training. As always, teaching by your own perfect example, you instruc'. us how to discover your presence within ourselves, and keep it alive in the most mature of spiritual relationships.

You personally gave us the standard to follow by referring to your intimate relationship with your own Gurudeva. You would describe him as "always seeing my activities and guiding me within my heart by his words. As it is said in *Śrīmad- Bhāgavatam, tene brahma hṛdā ya ādi-kavaye.* Spiritual inspiration comes from within the heart, wherein the Supreme Personality of Godhead, in His Paramātmā feature, is always sitting with all His devotees and associates."

My only concern now is. How do I guard against developing a pseudo Prabhupāda consciousness? What if I end up like a biblical Pharisee—very proud of following you wrongly? Or like a businessman, exploiting your holy name as if a cliche, simply to promote my own mental concoctions?

So in today's joyous Vyāsa-pūjā celebration, I beg from Your Divine Grace the benediction of being able throughout my life's remainder to clearly understand what it means to follow you, and live as if you are physically present, sitting right next to me.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa Swami

## Rādhānātha Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Remembrance of the transcendental quality of your pure devotion to Kṛṣṇa is the inspiration which alone maintains my life. In a world of endless illusions I have no place to seek refuge save and except in the remembrance of your lotus feet. If not for your kind mercy I would certainly be a hopeless victim in the hands of *māyā.* When I first met you in India in 1971, I was a lost soul and aimlessly wandering in the forest of impersonalism and voidism. You kindly cast your merciful glance upon me and with your compassionate words lifted me up to the shelter of your lotus feet. Now my life is in your merciful hands. You are Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu's most intimately empowered servitor. The most exalted realizations of all the previous *ācāryas* are manifested in your every word and action. You have fearlessly come to teach knowledge, detachment, and devotion, to a world pervaded by ignorance, sensory attachment, and rebellion against God. The perfection of our lives is to educate the world that you are Kṛṣṇa's pure devotee, the most compassionate friend of all living beings, and to bring them to the shelter of your lotus feet.

You have many loyal and dedicated disciples, in comparison with whom I am very useless and insignificant. Like a helpless child, I can only cry for your mercy moment after moment. It is explained in the *śāstra* that by the mercy *of guru* a lame man can cross mountains, a dumb man can speak eloquently, and a blind man can see the stars in the sky. In preaching the message of Lord Caitanya to the conditioned souls of Kali-yuga America, such a lame, dumb, and blind man as myself can only take refuge in remembering you.

I pray that my only desire in life be that your presence is ever manifested in my heart and that I can remember your divine instructions in all conditions. In this way perhaps I can please you by always remaining,

*A servant of your servants,*  
Rādhānātha Swami

## Śacīsuta Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Remembered Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Today is the most auspicious day of this Kali-yuga, because you appeared to preach *kṛṣṇa-prema* in this material world. As the eternal servant of Kṛṣṇa you taught by your example of your determined and humble service to your Guru Mahārāja in coming to the West all by yourself to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness, without expecting any credit for yourself but rather giving all honors to your spiritual master.

Just once you heard, "If you get any money, print books," and that was always your motivation, and in turn you would say, "Distribute my books! Distribute my books!" Your instruction is so simple, but it is so difficult for us to execute it that we always fall back again to the distribution of paraphernalia, with the excuse that it is "for financing book distribution."

As usual, I am forgetting, and I deviate easily from your instruction and become again an enjoyer, because I am not very intelligent so as to represent you and be the spokesman of your mission. I resort to your mercy very anxiously so that you may give me courage, and thus I can conceal my limitations.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, let me always help my Godbrothers to spread the preaching of your life, and in this way they may protect my devotional service. Help this insignificant servant to become useful in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness and book distribution in this country. All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda and this *saṅkīrtana* movement! May their glories be proclaimed throughout the three worlds.

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Śacīsuta Swami

## Śivarāma Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept this humble offering as a small token of our debt to your causeless mercy upon us, on your Vyāsa-pūjā day.

Sounds of kīrtana flood the air, all but the holy name is heard.  
The dust of Vaiṣṇavas 'lotus feet bless all throughout this war-torn world.  
Ocean waves of clashing karatālas, gleaming golden in the sun,  
Mṛdaṅga rhythms pace our hearts, Vaikuṇṭha dancing carries on.  
Godless darkness cripples hope, but one sun lights the way  
Breathing life into dull matter—a token price to pay.  
O brother, hear my broken song, though unfit I am to sing;  
In Kali's age the one last chance to us my lord did bring.  
And now he leads the kīrtana as it resounds through all the worlds.  
Come and see his great exploits, please listen to my words.

Prabhupāda leads the kīrtana party, Gaurāṅga by his side,  
Dancing, chanting in prema-bhakti, the holy name his pride.  
Nityānanda, Adwaitacarya, on either side of the Lord,  
Gadādhara and Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura attend His every word.  
A little behind the Pañca-tattva, six Gosvāmīs dance:  
Śrī Rūpa, Raghunāthas and Jīva, Śrī Gopāla, Sanātana.  
Behind Gosvāmīs come the ācāryas, then Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura,  
Gaura-kiśora dāsa bābājī, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Ṭhākura.  
They all praise Śrīla Prabhupāda, bestowing him their best;  
Blessings are for those who carry the orders of the rest.  
They urge him on with pleasing smiles, jay a Prabhupāda,  
But he is just a humble servant, serving their command.  
Prabhupāda plays a blue mṛdaṅga, a palanquin behind,  
Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, bedecked with flowers, ride a golden siṁhāsana.  
Loving devotees carry Their Lordships, worshiped by all the worlds,  
Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha, whose beauty surpasses all words.

The procession passes through towns and streets; people come to see.  
Of those who view, a few decide to take our company.  
Prabhupāda clasps them by the hand. How would you like to serve?  
Take a position to your liking, just do not leave or swerve.  
There are other mṛdaṅga players following Prabhupāda's beat;  
Karatāla players, singers, dancers, all clasp his lotus feet.  
Thousands of voices join together, the ecstasy of the name  
Swells in tides of transcendence, to share one common aim.  
Behind the party are devotees, editing Prabhupāda’s books,  
Layout, translation, production, just one word, one look  
Will change the heart of conditioned souls, start them on their way.  
Spiritual life begins anew, hope for a better way.  
Legions of devotees with tear-filled eyes, books in every arm,

Take every risk and every chance, lest others go to harm.  
Without his books, what hope is there, forlorn fellow man?  
Drink The Nectar of Devotion, worship Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.  
Some launder the soiled clothes of those on hari-nāma,  
Others cook, with gladdened hearts, sumptuous prasādam.  
Some wash pots and others set up tents in advance,  
Some bring water to the thirsty or massage those who dance.  
The saṅkīrtana party of Prabhupāda, oh, what great bliss  
The International Society for Krishna Consciousness.

Traveling through the universe, the procession makes its way  
To lonely planets of concrete streets, where smog blots out the day,  
Into the heart of asphalt jungles, where the animal is man,  
Transform the atmosphere anew into Śrī Māyāpur Dhāma.  
The jīvas come in different dress, white, black, and old,  
Young, males, and females, there's room for all, they're told.  
While passing through one town in a snowbound north country,  
He cast his loving glance my way, as I had come to see.  
He caught me up and pulled me out, one foot in the grave,  
As I embraced my chains of madness, another industrial slave.  
Sometimes I distribute books, sometimes I erect the tents,  
Sometimes I cut trees to clear a way for great events.  
Whatever position, being a member is certainly a great boon,  
Managing others, taking prasādam, having darśana in his room.  
Bliss it is, but hard-earned bliss, for māyā is always there;  
Prostitutes lining the road are poised to drag one in their lair.  
Charlatans offer different types of intoxicating brew,  
Bewilder minds of naive Vaiṣṇavas who look for something new.  
Demoniac scientists create computers from homogeneous soup;  
Bewildered by mile-long formulae, some may even bloop.  
Religionists and speculators quote sectarian books.  
Bewildered parents wave old diapers, casting longing looks.  
Imitation kīrtana parties often pass the other way.  
For those who don't keep him in sight may dearly have to pay.  
Conditioned minds attached to madness sometimes are inert,  
And shameless senses given rope will always tend to flirt.  
But the party moves undaunted, though some may fall away.  
Prabhupāda goes to save them, or "they will be back some other day.'  
City to country, planet to universe, hari-nāma must go on.  
Prabhupāda leads in ecstasy an eternal rising sun.  
Then, one day, we turn to see Śrīla Prabhupāda has gone;  
No longer dancing with us, he has decided to move on.  
Some other part of the party has received his kindly glance,  
Some other land, some other world is witness to his dance.  
For us who tend his orders, to lose sight of him?  
But looking down, his footprints clearly mark the path we're in.  
Now those who assisted Prabhupāda must maintain the same beat,  
Recalling the instructions they learned at his lotus feet.  
Others come to help keep time, and make the pace anew.  
Cooperation is our guide—Śrīla Prabhupāda wants us to.  
The holy name, the holy name resounds in every ear;  
Its ecstasy removes all darkness, frees us from all fear.  
Others come, his desire is heard, take up the banner too.  
This movement is infallible, our shelter is Mahāprabhu.  
Chant with us, dance with us, read the books we bear,  
Take prasādam, know Lord Kṛṣṇa, be happy is our prayer.  
After going round the kīrtana, what things I haven't done?  
I find myself with bag in hand, a service to be won.  
Within the bag unlimited books, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam  
"Take some help from others, "says the drummer Bhagavān.  
Bottomless bag of unlimited books do Prabhupāda’s will.  
Distribute books, distribute books, distribute more books still.  
As we distribute, people take and read with great delight.  
This is our vow to do the needful and take up the fight.  
Our last request that with unflinching mind we may go on  
Undeterred by Māyā’s traps to chant Lord Kṛṣṇa's song.  
And in our service sometimes we may gain his company,  
That he may come to where we chant, that sometimes we may see  
His radiant smile, loving glance, and forceful reprimand.  
Our master, Śrīla Prabhupāda, let me forever be your man!

Śivarāma Swami

## Trivikrama Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Most dearly beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble obeisances at your holy lotus feet. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your kindness and courage in bringing the message of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu to us here in the West.

Sometimes you gave us the saying, "The heart of a Bengali mother and the courage of a British soldier." I always thought that this was such a fitting description for you. Caring without courage equals sentimentality, or wishful thinking and courage without caring leads to brutalization and callousness. It is like a razor's edge. So my personal prayer is to allow me to be as you were. Please save me from being a sentimentalist or a bully. The bully who likes to force takes pleasure in throwing his weight around, feels satisfied when others are serving him. Or the sentimentalist who actually likes to be forced lacks the courage of his conviction, refuses to represent Kṛṣṇa in an uncompromising way.

Except for your mercy there is no way to cross over these huge pitfalls. I personally feel utterly and completely inadequate to stand erect against these temptations, and I find myself repeatedly swaying to one side or the other. So I am taking this opportunity of my Vyāsa-pūjā offering to especially beg you for protection. I am lowly and factually devoid of courage. Please let me be as you were; give me the backbone to overcome this cowardly inclination toward sense gratification and sentimentality, and thus I may earn the right to be known as one of the faithful followers of Your Divine Grace.

*Your lowly servant,*  
Trivikrama Swami

## Vipramukhya Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Once again we celebrate your appearance  
Within this material world,  
When again we recount our good fortune  
For your great and invaluable gift.

You gave us the opportunity to hear  
Lord Kṛṣṇa 's deep instructions;  
We rejoice the opportunity you gave  
'Midst this curtain of māyā to lift.  
Precious few times were those when you were with us,  
Holy Vṛndāvana and also elsewhere.  
Countless millions of births I have passed through;  
Your association I did not deserve.  
In the Bhagavad-gītā Lord Kṛṣṇa declares  
Out of many thousands of men  
One may search for truth, and thousands like these  
Hardly one to the Lord he will serve.

And once again out of many such persons  
Hardly one knows the Lord in truth;  
Now it's here we remember our fortune:  
For out of those, how many had you?  
Let this offering be my humble prayer  
To seek your unlimited grace,  
For a lame man may cross the great mountain,  
And poems may I struggle to write few!

*Your humble servant,*  
Vipramukhya Swami

## Viśvambhara dāsa Goswami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.*

On this most auspicious day of your appearance we offer our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. Kindly allow that our offering to you be a fervent prayer. We are destitute of all spiritual assets and reduced to begging again from you even on this day.

We know that you sit in the august assembly of the Vaiṣṇava *sampradāya.* Who can imagine the ocean of nectar of your *saṅkīrtana* pastimes with all the intimate associates of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu? As all of you, most benevolent and merciful personalities of the entire creation, engage in eternal activities in the spiritual world, do you still hanker to save the conditioned souls of the material world of illusion? If so, how is it possible that we of this world do not become enlivened with the urgency of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness? Why are we content to let the sun pass overhead day after day without ever doing any significant service for the benefit of the conditioned souls?

Like some mystic vision, we see, ever so clearly, how the Supreme Lord is crushing the world under the weight of His justice. The jaws of time are closing on the nations of the world. Never before has His justice been so swift or so evident; and never before has the immediate future seemed so grim. Yet, every day, calmly, we chew on the already chewed. Willingly we offer ourself to the tortures of our heretical mind. Daily we quarrel unnecessarily. Foolishly we plod on, blinded in our understanding by the material conception of life. How is it possible, my spiritual master, that we be so insensitive to your greatness and to your compassionate desire to save the people of this earth planet?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray that your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement may revolutionize the course of human civilization now. Let your books guide the destiny of this world now. Let your sublime philosophy overcome rampant atheism and impersonalism now. Let the religions of the world unite under the banner of Vaiṣṇavism. Let the holy name be the international anthem and rallying cry of all nations of the world. Kindly appeal to the Supreme Lord Himself on behalf of this distressed planet. For Him, all things are possible. But let it be now. O most beloved savior of the world, all of us, your disciples and granddisciples, have been cursed by you with the expectancy that Kṛṣṇa consciousness will flood the world any moment now. But due to our neophyte nature we fail and are still unable to overcome our differences and cooperate together to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness as you wanted. The world is tasteless, our soul is starving for the nectar of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu's *saṅkīrtana* revolution. On the eve of the five-hundredth anniversary of His appearance, we are crying out across the boundaries of this material universe for your mercy. Will our voice be lost somewhere in the dust of stars? If you do not use us fully as instruments in the grand design of your will, we are but a lifeless doll shaking in the wind, and the world is a hopeless chaos of evil. Our very breathing is an offense, and we wish to die. Please allow us to preach your message boldly. So we pray on the most auspicious day of your appearance.

*Your disciple,*  
Viśvambhara dāsa Goswami

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Other Sources

## Back to Godhead

**The Best Glorification of All**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

As i sit to write this offering I am mindful of the precedent you set in your Vyāsa-pūjā offerings to Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Prabhupāda by addressing your Godbrothers and preaching to them. On this occasion, I aspire to follow in your footsteps. Repeating your message is pleasing to you and to Kṛṣṇa, for He says that no one is dearer to Him than he who preaches His message to the devotees.

My dear Godbrothers, on this day, along with the many glowing memories of our transcendental savior, let us keep in mind his parting instructions to us all: "Your love for me will be shown by how well you cooperate." Let us not overlook or minimize this vital instruction. Our ability to put aside self-interest and cooperate responsibly is intrinsic not only to the success of ISKCON, but to our personal success in Kṛṣṇa consciousness as well. Cooperation for Kṛṣṇa's pleasure is an ever-present symptom of Vaikuṇṭha consciousness. Unless we perfect the technique in ISKCON, it is unlikely we'll be allowed to enter the spiritual world at all.

Cooperation necessitates surrender. Not that I surrender to you or you surrender to me, but that we both surrender to whatever is in the best interest of Śrīla Prabhupāda's ISKCON, the ship upon which we all hope to cross over the ocean of repeated birth and death. Naturally, whatever is in the best interest of the ship is also in the best interest of the passengers. There is no reason, therefore, to be anxious that you may not receive your due by surrender and responsible cooperation.

My dear Godbrothers, with this in mind, let us not forget Śrīla Prabhupāda's warning: "Personal ambition will ruin everything." I must honestly say that I'm infected with this disease of spiritual life. I ask, therefore, that you all consider what I'm about to say as beneficial for my purification and for the purification of those amongst you who may inadvertently fall victim to personal ambition.

When Śrīla Prabhupāda cautioned us against personal ambition, I had no idea how broadly his warning applied. With the passage of time, however, I'm coming gradually to appreciate that it not only hinders our ship's progress but threatens sometimes to shatter it on the shoals of selfishness, something I think we all dread. At least we don't want to see it happen in our lifetime. But it very well could happen.

After undergoing some self-analysis and contemplating different events and states of affairs in ISKCON, I've compiled a list of personal ambitions, which I present here:

If you engage persons in your charge in service but don't take the responsibility to look after their growth and development in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you, regardless of how enthusiastic you may be to preach to the nondevotees, are indifferent, harsh, callous, or cavalier about the fears and cares of your Godbrothers, you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you develop the mentality that you have an exclusive appreciation and realization of Śrīla Prabhupāda and his mission, a monopoly on Kṛṣṇa consciousness, so to speak, you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you are motivated and inspired to serve by the desire to gain prominence historically, to "play for history," you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you think that by becoming *guru* you can wield absolute authority over a Godbrother, rather than find mutual grounds of agreement by open discussion, you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you concoct some criteria for love and trust amongst the Vaiṣṇavas other than following the principles and rendering sincere service, big or small, you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you have the Hollywood syndrome, i.e., you believe the goal of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is to make it big, be a megasuperstar-devotee, you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you are complacent, idle, ease-loving, and don't think your predicament is treacherous, you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you think, "I'm too saintly to get involved in feuds, debates, controversies, etc.," even when the truth is at stake, you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you cannot approve the way another devotee does his service simply because he doesn't do it to your taste, or because you are not in charge of it, you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you cannot recognize that it's the right of every disciple of Śrīla Prabhupāda to be concerned about ISKCON's direction and, if desirous, have some say in it, you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you are in a position of authority and you believe that having the final word means having the only word, you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you cannot recognize that your first responsibility as a preacher of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is to open and enrich others' lives, devotees and nondevotees alike, to bring out the best in them, you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you are attached to your position, disciples, zone, region, temple, project, or department, over and above the greater interest of ISKCON, you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you think, "I have no complaints personally or in my immediate area; therefore all is well," you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you are fearful that by delegating responsibility your own stature will diminish, lacking the faith and confidence that "a rising tide raises all the ships," you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you find that you are only comfortable in an environment where everyone else is your subordinate, you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you have the Hanuman-and-the-spider syndrome, i.e., the mentality that you are a big devotee and can therefore take the liberty to cajole, harangue, and intimidate Godbrothers who try in their own humble way to serve Śrīla Prabhupāda, rather than try to help them in their service, you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you think that by putting on a certain air, posture, or attitude in imitation of Śrīla Prabhupāda you've become like him in terms of spiritual realization, you are a victim of personal ambition.

If your mentality is, "I want to be the ONE to succeed in Kṛṣṇa consciousness," rather than thinking, "I hope to be among those fortunate devotees who make it back to Godhead," you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you, as a disciple of Śrīla Prabhupāda, neglect your duty to preach, maintain, rectify, and improve the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you become so enchanted with and full of yourself that you cannot even appreciate another's devotional merits and spiritual desires, or even try to see their point of view, you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you are guilty of some impropriety and defend yourself by charging, "What have you done to spread the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement?" you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you think preaching, book distribution to the nondevotees, alone is glorious, and all preaching directed within ISKCON is politics and due to envy, you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you cannot find within yourself the heartfelt desire to serve and encourage your Godbrothers, help them to realize their fullest ambitions to serve Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you think this or that Godbrother cannot preach to me, cannot show me some nuance in discharging devotional service, because he did not outdo my temple, region, or zone, or someone else's, you are a victim of personal ambition.

If when you associate with devotees you develop the habit of only hearing your voice, you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you readily concede that Kṛṣṇa is the center of all attraction, but your mentality is "I'm right next to Kṛṣṇa, in the center," rather than "I aspire to become the servant of the servant of Kṛṣṇa," you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you think, "Now I'm a big devotee, I'm not obliged to be humble, kind, cooperative, understanding, tolerant, patient, detached, and, if need be, a repentant Vaiṣṇava," you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you lose your courage for pursuing Kṛṣṇa consciousness, being overwhelmed by the inevitable hardships ISKCON must endure for Śrīla Prabhupāda’s sake as well as for individual purification, you are a victim of personal ambition.

If you are party to the plight and flight of any devotee in ISKCON, you are the victim of personal ambition.

If you cannot feel sympathy for a Godbrother's trials and setbacks in spiritual life, you are a victim of personal ambition.

My dear Godbrothers, all these symptoms I've listed here and their resultant modes of behavior have one thing in common: any one of them can effectively ruin our Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. My list is not exhaustive—you may certainly add to it—but it is useful for pointing us in the right direction of singling out the enemy within. Unless we disavow all desires and designations save and except the desire to become the humble servant of Śrīla Prabhupāda, with whom there is no equal in the fourteen worlds, we will sooner or later fall victim to personal ambition.

The best way to avoid ruination by this vicious enemy is to continuously and conscientiously pray, with all our hearts, to become nonenvious Vaiṣṇavas.

My dear Godbrothers, we all know that Śrīla Prabhupāda was such a nonenvious Vaiṣṇava. We all know that he wants us to become nonenvious Vaiṣṇavas ourselves. He said many times that would please him the most. On the celebration of his auspicious appearance day, I can think of no more fitting subject for our meditation. I humbly pray, therefore, that by his immeasurable kindness upon us all, we shall soon rise to this standard of perfection. For our beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda, that would be the best glorification of all.

All glories to *jagad-guru* Śrīla Prabhupāda, the supreme personality of servitor Godhead! All glories to his devotees!

(Written by Kuṇḍalī dāsa)

*Your fallen and insignificant servants,*

## Bāla Books

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

Once upon a time, there were some useless souls without any purpose in life. By Kṛṣṇa's causeless mercy they met you, and you asked them to do something nice for children.

They didn't know how to do anything nice, so you guided them by suggesting they publish books—beautiful, transcendental books for children, describing the pastimes of the Lord and His devotees.

You also told those useless souls why such books would be important. "The way we can help make the world wonderful is by helping children to become wonderful. Our regular *saṅkīrtana* must go on," you said, "but the way we will practically influence the future will be through our children. So this Bāla Books project is required."

This is a story with a happy ending, because those useless souls were made useful by your mercy. "Make books for children everywhere," you said. "Put those books in schools and libraries. Make the books beautiful, and make them Kṛṣṇa conscious, because what is learned early in life is never forgotten."

You told us about when you were a child, how you would hear the stories of Ramāyana and go to sleep dreaming of Lord Rāmacandra and Hanumān. By your mercy, one day children all over the world may go to sleep dreaming about Lord Rāmacandra and Hanumān.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for having saved us and for having given Kṛṣṇa to children everywhere.

*Your humble servants at Bāla Books,*

Yogeśvara dāsa / Prahlāda-priya dāsa / Garuḍa dāsa / Kathāmṛta dāsa / Sunītā-devī dāsī / Sītā-devī dāsī / Vṛndā-devī dāsī / Pūrṇaśakti-devī dāsī / Viṣṇu-priyā-devī dāsī

## Bhaktivedanta Institute of Religion and Culture

*Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

On this anniversary of your auspicious and historic appearance in this world, we, your disciples and grand-disciples, fall at your feet with love and gratitude. Placing your effulgent lotus feet on our heads, we beg you to hear our inarticulate but heartfelt prayer.

Due to dullness resulting from the modes of nature, we often take our activities in devotional service for granted, as if mere routine, as if merely a vocation rather than the sublime means of connection to your holy lotus feet and to the eternal lotus feet of Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Although objectively engaged in activities favorable to the spreading of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, we spend much of our time in a state of dull forgetfulness, like unfortunate dumb animals—barely conscious of that glorious person who is the very object of our service and of the sublimity of his sacred mission.

On this Vyāsa-pūjā day, however, our meditation on your transcendental presence and your mercy becomes much more intense. The dark clouds of self-centeredness and mundaneness part, and we can catch a fleeting glimpse of the radiance of your transcendent grandeur. That little glimpse fills our hearts with bliss and causes us to curse our inexcusable lack of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This momentary and partial vision of your glories and your saving grace makes us crave the solace of your living association, which lifts the burden of material contamination from the heart and the mind and causes us to hanker after Śrī Kṛṣṇa, that mischievous cowherd who steals butter and hearts, who is glorified by the greatest of saints, mystics, philosophers, and poets. Reflecting on our smallness and meanness, and on the goal you've set before us, unalloyed *prema,* we wonder how we have become so fortunate as to even peer into the antechamber of the mansion of *bhakti,* much less enter its courtyard. Tightly clutching the hand of our father, we—like the privileged children of a king—enter that mansion and enjoy its beauties, and long for the day when we will enter into spiritual adulthood and take up residence in that majestic abode.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, help us become what we are supposed to become, what you want us to become: pure, unalloyed devotees of the Lord, who have no business in this world and in this life save and except to glorify the Personality of Godhead and assist in the liberation of conditioned souls. When we stray from the sanctity of your lotus feet, which is our only sanctuary from madness and misery, please drag us back, drag us back, drag us back again and again. Despite all pretentions of intelligence and sophistication, we are mere children and do not know what is best for us. Please forgive our unending mistakes and offenses and give us your causeless mercy. Please save us, and engage us in your sacred service.

II

In light of our particular service, we pray that you might empower us to communicate the depth and riches of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to those whom you call "the intelligent class of men." Although material intelligence is not in itself a qualification for Kṛṣṇa consciousness, those who are thoughtful and reflective, who try to understand the origin, essence, and meaning of things, who try to comprehend things in broad, systematic perspective—in other words, those who are genuine intellectuals—should, more than others, be able to appreciate the philosophical and spiritual wealth of *Bhagavad-gītā, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* and of the spiritual culture they define and sustain. Though they themselves may not take directly to the path of *bhakti,* at least these intellectuals can *appreciate* the philosophical and spiritual depth of Vaiṣṇava civilization and the authenticity of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, and that appreciation, that acknowledgement, is the beginning of their devotional service.

Due to their respected position in society, intellectuals and scholars are influential far beyond their actual numbers, and their sympathetic attitude towards ISKCON is transmitted to a wide audience through their own teaching and writings. It is members of the academic community further, who, along with our friends in the Indian community, most strongly come to the defense of ISKCON when it is attacked by ignorant and bigoted people.

For these and many other reasons, it is our desire and our mission to educate the "intelligent class" about ISKCON and the spiritual tradition it embodies, as well as to inject the profound riches of Vaiṣṇava thought into the contemporary academic disciplines. It is also our hope to develop programs for training intellectually gifted devotee children, so that they may one day join us, and hopefully surpass us, in this effort.

Our aspirations are lofty, but our capacities are limited and determination unsteady. We therefore beg you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to grant us the intelligence, the purity, the devotion, the expertise, and the determination to succeed. Let us not allow our service to become afflicted by the twin diseases of fruitive intent and self-gratificatory mental speculation. We can do nothing if not protected and empowered by you. Kindly empower us, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Empower us and keep us fixed at your eternal lotus feet. Let us do something wonderful for you and for the sublime cause of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this world.

(Written by Śubhānanda dāsa)

*Your servants, always aspiring for your mercy,*

## Ministry of Public Affairs

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

With your unlimited wisdom and patience you inaugurated the International Society for Krishna Consciousness among the uncultured youth of the West, rescuing us from our frustration and apathy and giving us the joy and freedom of spiritual life.

You formed ISKCON not as a "mutual admiration society," but as a dynamic, transcendental movement for changing the hearts of all those who seriously participate; and by that purifying process you created preachers who, by their genuine spiritual qualities, can attract others to the devotional life.

Gradually, as the people of the world recognize the potency and substance of ISKCON, we are experiencing an ever-expanding appreciation for the incomparable work that you have done, and are continuing to do, and on this most blessed day we offer our deepest and most sincere obeisances for being allowed to take part in your devotional offering to Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

## Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu Saṅkīrtana Pādayātrā

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādī-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Jaya Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya, Prabhu Nityānanda, Śrī Advaita,*  
*Gadādhara, Śrīvāsadi Gaura-bhakta-vṛnda*

*Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare*  
*Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupada,*

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu! From your *Līlāmṛta* we understand that way back, when you were still at Jhansi organizing the League of Devotees program with your only disciple, you set out on Pādayātrā. You spent at least five nights in five villages, holding festivals in the evening.

In 1976 you ordered us to travel with bullock carts, and in the year that followed, just prior to your departure, you expressed your desire to revive and expand by traveling all over India with bullock carts. You personally wanted to travel with such a program. But Kṛṣṇa had another plan, so the scheme to travel never worked out.

After you left us, I made some unsuccessful attempts to organize the kind of traveling program you had so much desired. Ultimately, by Lord Caitanya's mercy, we were able to organize the Saṅkīrtana Pādayātrā in commemoration of Lord Caitanya's five-hundredth appearance anniversary After some experience and many detailed instructions from you on how to run such a program, we are already eight months on the road, having visited numerous villages and brought Kṛṣṇa consciousness to uncountable doorsteps. As the Pādayātrā passes through villages, towns, cities, and states, it is making headlines in the national daily newspapers, on television, and on the radio. Everything is going on fairly nicely on the Pādayātrā, and we feel it is increasing in its strength.

The Pādayātrā has many different features. One of them, however, is conspicuous only by its absence, and that is mass *prasādam* distribution. I remember how our bullock cart *saṅkīrtana* party met you in Allahabad. At the Kumbha-melā we presented our program, and you expressed how much you were pleased by the *prasādam* distribution. That satisfaction of yours was the perfection of our preaching efforts. Many seemingly convincing reasons and excuses may be cited as to why we have not as yet started distributing *prasādam* on a mass scale. We are praying to Kṛṣṇa to help us rectify this situation so that we may become absorbed in mass *prasādam* distribution. We have already tried and had some success at this several times. Since our goal is your satisfaction, we would like to use any end all facilities at our disposal to make this program a success.

As the Pādayātrā, the largest preaching program ever organized in India, is going on successfully, we have received suggestions from many devotees to continue it past our scheduled date of termination. A strong suggestion came from Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami. In his letter to us he wrote, "Frankly, I was feeling that this Pādayātrā should not be stopped at the five-hundredth anniversary celebration. Why should it not continue indefinitely into the future years to come!" He reminded us that Prabhupāda said we could conquer India by perfecting Pādayātrā. Yes, Kṛṣṇa consciousness should conquer India! If Pādayātrā is the means to that end, it must be adopted at all cost!

Though our immediate destination is Māyāpur, your place of worship, we are contemplating continuing to finally reach your home, Vṛndāvana Dhāma. There in Vṛndāvana we would like to inaugurate a ten-year traveling *saṅkīrtana* program to celebrate the centenary of your sacred appearance. While keeping you in the center, we would travel, preaching *gaura-vāṇī* to all we meet. It would be a befitting celebration of your appearance! To some it may sound like trying to "shoot the rhinoceros," but that is exactly what you always wanted us to do. It is not an impossible goal.

(Written by Lokanātha Swami)

*Begging for the necessary strength and determination to continue this Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Saṅkīrtana Pādayātrā, we beg to remain, your servants,*
